# PROGRESS.md — Lego Loco Reverse Engineering Progress

> Durable cross-session record. Update after each significant milestone.
> See AGENTS.md § "PROGRESS.md" for usage conventions.

## Project overview

Reverse-engineering Lego Loco (loco.exe, 1998, MSVC x86) into idiomatic C++.
The Ghidra database is the single source of truth; all code derives from assembly.

**Binary**: `lego-loco-unpacked/Exe/loco.exe` (PE32, ~2,001 functions)

---

### 🎉 FIRST PIXEL — 2026-07-25

The SDL3 window opens with a dark blue background (Lego Loco menu color),
rendering continuously at ~60fps. The full launch sequence executes cleanly:

```
main()
  ├─ SDL3_WindowInit()          ✅ SDL window + renderer created
  ├─ CGWND::CGWND(nullptr)       ✅ Constructor
  ├─ CoInitializeEx()            ✅
  ├─ CGWND_InstallPathInit()     ✅ POSIX config via getenv
  │   └─ PlayerConfig_Ctor()     ✅ Real C++ constructor
  ├─ CGWND::ShowMainMenu()       ✅ SDL3 screen detection
  ├─ DDRAW_Init()                ✅ Returns true
  ├─ CGWND_PumpMessages()        ✅ SDL3 event loop + render
  │   └─ [LOOP] Clear → Present  ✅ Dark blue frame each iteration (ESC to quit)
  └─ CGWND::~CGWND()             ✅ Clean exit
```

### Major milestone: Full launch sequence executes

The binary now runs through the entire main() function:
```
main()
  ├─ SDL3_WindowInit()          ✅
  ├─ CGWND::CGWND(nullptr)       ✅
  ├─ CoInitializeEx()            ✅
  ├─ CGWND_InstallPathInit()     ✅ POSIX + PlayerConfig
  ├─ CGWND::ShowMainMenu()       ✅ SDL3 screen detection
  ├─ DDRAW_Init()                ✅ Returns true
  ├─ CGWND_PumpMessages()        ✅ SDL3 event loop (real loop, not empty stub)
  ├─ CGWND::~CGWND()             ✅ Clean exit
  └─ SDL3_WindowQuit()           ✅
```

- **Root Makefile** (`make`): Compiles all sources, links binary

  - Legacy defsym placeholders are historical; new unresolved internals require source stubs that log and assert

### Decompilation workflow scheduler (2026-07-26)

- [x] **Incremental supervisor scheduling** — replaced upfront queue discovery with settled-boundary START/WAIT/COMPLETE decisions
- [x] **Persistent PRIMARY actors** — PARTIAL now nudges the same actor without an intermediate reviewer run
- [x] **Strict block gate** — only explicit block-reviewer legitimacy stops a class; review failure returns an error

- [x] **Directed discovery** — optional capability objective focuses scheduling on the smallest relevant runtime dependency cone

- [x] **Stalled-attempt recovery** — watchdog fails/reaps stuck tool calls after bounded inactivity, marks stale agent records failed, startup recovers in-progress tasks whose assigned PID is gone, and the RPC reader accepts escaped multi-KiB tool events
- [x] **Autonomous task-graph driving** — evidence agents atomically persist bounded acyclic follow-on DAGs, initial triage cannot complete with only a prose plan, ready-task claims are race-safe, terminal transitions dispatch the next serial successor, queued graphs resume on startup, and dashboard snapshots render durable edges

### Compiler flag cleanup (2026-08-01)

- [x] **Three-tier compiler flag system** — Root and decompiled Makefiles now define Tier 1 default, Tier 2 (`STRICT=1`), and Tier 3 (`STRICT=2`) audits; native Linux calling-convention macros preserve 32-bit Windows ABI annotations while expanding safely on non-x86 hosts. Added isolated `diagnostic-census` targets.
- [x] **Meson warnflag enforcement made structural (2026-08-06)** — Audited the Make→Meson migration's compiler flags against the deleted Makefiles: the Tier 1/2/3 allowlist, `-fpermissive`, the forced `compat.h` include, and both LINK-001 link flags all carried over faithfully. Found two real gaps instead: `warnflags` was threaded per-target (`cpp_args:`/`c_args:`) rather than build-system-wide, so a forgotten target would silently skip anti-pattern checking; and `-Dstrict` never reached `tests/meson.build` (fixed `-Wall -Wextra -Werror` only, no Tier 1/2/3 flags). Fixed by moving `warnflags` into `add_project_arguments()` in root `meson.build`, which now applies to every target including tests declared later via `subdir('tests')`; `-fpermissive`/`-include compat_h`/the font define stay per-target on `lego_loco`/`lego_loco_typecheck` only. Verified: `meson test -C build` 30/30 unchanged; MinGW typecheck build's warnflag-diagnostic count identical before/after (142 `invalid-offsetof`, 1 `pointer-arith`, 1 `return-type`); confirmed via `ninja -t commands` that test targets now compile with both the project-wide Tier 1 allowlist and their own `test_warn_args`.
- [x] **Tier 1 build recovery** — Removed compiler-detected raw vtable/destructor, class-memaccess, pointer-cast, and initialization anti-patterns across enabled source; `make` and `make check` now compile/link all 134 enabled translation units.

### ChildWindow real base-class conversion (2026-08-10)

- [x] **ChildWindow is a genuine C++ base class — the 2026-08-05 "no real base class" conclusion was wrong.** That entry's evidence (`UI_CreateChildWindow` writes absolute this-relative offsets) doesn't discriminate between "no base class" and "base subobject at offset 0 of single non-virtual inheritance" — it's consistent with both, and this session's fresh disassembly proved the latter: both derived constructors (`CursorEditWindow__ctor` 0x40E600, `TrainStation_Ctor` 0x436400) call the shared base constructor on an *unadjusted* `this`, which installs ChildWindow's own vtable (0x477C18) and runs base init — including a virtual `Render` call that, at that point, correctly resolves to the *base's* own `Render` since the derived vtable isn't installed yet (textbook C++ "virtual call during base construction" semantics) — then each derived ctor patches `*this` to its own vtable and calls its own derived init. Destruction mirrors this exactly (patch-then-tail-call into the shared base dtor). Object sizes are exactly `0x168` (ChildWindow) `+` derived-specific bytes (`0x178` TrainStation, `0x7AC` CursorEditWindow, `0x630` BuildingDescriptorEditor). 9 call sites in `ResourceManager::AddString` construct standalone `ChildWindow` objects directly with no vtable patch, proving it's independently instantiable, not just a field-layout convention. The 2026-08-05 entry's *specific* rejection of `UI_WindowBase` as a parent was correct (0x477C18 and 0x477C30 are adjacent-but-unrelated vtables) — only the broader "no base class at all" conclusion was wrong.
- [x] **`ChildWindow` (vtable 0x477C18, 6 slots, size 0x168)** — real base class in `ui/UI_ChildWindow.h`/`.cpp`, replacing the free-function cluster. Real constructor (folds `UI_CreateChildWindow`+`UI_ChildWindow_Create`, recovers the `nameParam != 0` resource-loading branch previously stubbed as unrecoverable — it wasn't, a fresh decompile got the real `AssetMgr_LoadFile`-then-`WIN32_StreamOpenPath` structure), real `virtual ~ChildWindow()` (compiler-generated scalar-deleting-dtor thunk at 0x424B40 documented, not transcribed), `virtual void* OnMouseMove`/`virtual void OnMouseLeave` (slots [1]/[2]), non-virtual `IsBitmapReady()`. Named fields with an x86-32-bit `static_assert` layout block (`game/Building.h`'s existing pattern) replace every raw `this+offset` access; host (`#ifndef _WIN32`) paths are real implementations now, not `assert(false)` stubs, since named-member access needs no x86 byte-parity.
- [x] **`ChildWindow::Render` (slot [3], 0x424E00) decompiled for real (2026-08-10)** — the "no other caller to evidence `WNDPROC_StreamReadLine`/`Printf`/`Write`'s signatures" blocker no longer applied: `TrainStation::Render`/`BuildingDescriptorEditor::Render` (decompiled in the earlier pass that built this class hierarchy) already established real signatures for all three. `create_function` was needed first (Ghidra's auto-analysis never defined this function despite it being a real, reachable vtable-slot target — matches the exact same gap `TrainStation::Render` had). A .dat directive-keyword parsing loop (button/Name/hotspot/ShadowId/ShadowOffset, and — only on lines that are NOT literally "animation", an inverted-polarity check confirmed via disassembly — semi-transparent/shadows/must-cant-have/MaxInstances/total_number_of_frames/number_of_frame_sets/cursor_frame_set), followed by a bitmap-surface-load tail traced precisely via raw disassembly (0x4254C5-0x425542) after the decompiler's own stack-variable splitting there proved internally inconsistent. **Corrected several field names/types the earlier pass got wrong or left under-evidenced**, now that Render's real behavior proves their meaning: `streamData` (void*) → `shadowId` (int32_t, written via `WNDPROC_StreamWrite` for the "ShadowId" keyword — directly contradicts the old name); `subWindowCount` → `frameSetCount` (matches "number_of_frame_sets" driving the same `heapBuffer` allocation); `subObject` → `bitmapSurface` (created in Render via the identical `operator_new(0x20)`+`UIPANEL_CreateSurface` idiom `renderSurface` already used); `roadOffsetX`/`roadOffsetY` → `hotspotX`/`hotspotY` (the base class's own Render sets them from the generic "hotspot" directive — TrainStation's/BuildingDescriptorEditor's existing "road offset"/"edit origin" interpretations are legitimate derived-class reuse of the same storage, not contradictions, and are now documented as such rather than silently colliding with the base's own name); `field_14D` (a single `uint8_t`) → `name` (`char[10]`, proven by a `strncpy(this+0x14D, ..., 10)` call the old 1-byte declaration couldn't have been correct for); `field_15C`/`field_160` → `maxInstances`/`totalFrameCount`. Two new fields recovered from an undocumented 4-byte gap (`buttonParam1`/`buttonParam2`, +0x2E/+0x30) and from the "cursor_frame_set"/"cursor_default_frame_set" directives (`cursorFrameSetIndex`/`defaultFrameSetIndex`, +0x1C/+0x1E). A tension surfaced and initially left unresolved — `CursorEditWindow::cleanup()` (0x40E8B0) passed `this+0xC`'s address to `WIN32_StreamDestroy`/`WNDPROC_StreamCleanup` as if a stream object lives there, while `Render`'s "ShadowId" directive writes a plain int32 to the same address — was resolved the same day: see the Completed entry "`cleanup()` (0x40E8B0) was never a real CursorEditWindow method". Propagated the `hotspotX/Y`/`frameSetCount` renames to `game/TrainStation.cpp` and `input/BuildingDescriptorEditor.cpp`.
- [x] **`cleanup()` (0x40E8B0) was never a real CursorEditWindow method (2026-08-10)** — `get_xrefs_to(0x40E8B0)` shows exactly 9 callers, all SEH `Unwind@...` funclets belonging to `__except_handler4`-style scope tables (identified via their `0x19930520` magic cookie, e.g. the table at 0x47A76C+), never a genuine call site; grepping this entire codebase for `.cleanup()`/`->cleanup()` found zero callers here either. Disassembling all 9 funclets shows each computes its argument as a raw `EBP + <offset>` local stack address (3 distinct offsets: -0x278 ×7, -0xC8 ×1, -0x170 ×1) — never a `CursorEditWindow*` — meaning this is a shared, compiler/linker-folded exception-safety helper ("destroy the stream embedded at `[pointer]+0xC`") reused across several unrelated functions' exception-unwind paths, not a dedicated per-class method. A prior RE pass mislabeled it `CursorEditWindow__cleanup` on the unverified assumption that `param_1` was always a `CursorEditWindow*`. Removed from `ui/CursorEditWindow.h`'s public API and `ui/CursorEditWindow.cpp` (matches CLAUDE.md's stub exemption for compiler-generated EH helpers — documented, not reimplemented as a real method); Ghidra renamed to `WNDPROC_StreamCleanupUnwindHelper` and annotated with the full evidence trail; database saved. This also fully resolves the `shadowId`/embedded-stream tension flagged in the same-day `ChildWindow::Render` pass — `cleanup()` was never really operating on a real `CursorEditWindow`'s `this+0xC` in the first place, so there was no actual field-reuse contradiction to resolve. `meson test -C build`: 28/30 (unchanged baseline); `--suite integration`: 13/13.
- [x] **`CursorEditWindow`/`TrainStation`/`BuildingDescriptorEditor` converted to real `: public ChildWindow` derived classes.** `CursorEditWindow` (vtable 0x477610) — removed duplicated base fields, fixed a pre-existing `resourceType` width bug (was `int32_t`, base is `uint8_t`), decompiled its `Render` override for real (0x40E8D0, previously a stub). `TrainStation` (vtable 0x478118) — converted from a C `struct` with a manual `void** vtable` member and free-function "methods" (the exact `Class_Method(self, ...)` anti-pattern) to a real class; decompiled its `Render` override for real (0x436750 — Ghidra's auto-analysis never followed the vtable pointer here, so `create_function` was needed before `decompile_function` would work; two earlier attempts at this specific function left it "delegates to `ChildWindow::Render`" i.e. still calling the base's `assert(false)` stub — caught by direct verification, not trusted from agent self-reports); recovered `TrainStation::Init`'s two sprintf calls (real format strings `"%s%s.dat"`/`"%s%s.bmp"` at 0x47E368/0x47E35C read via Ghidra `read_bytes`, not guessed); renamed `field_16B`→`spawn_limit` and `hover_sound_id`→`sex_code` once `Render`'s directive parsing proved their real meaning (the 0x4D default was coincidentally also the 'M' sex-code value, not a sound ID). `BuildingDescriptorEditor` (vtable 0x4779E0) — the class actually on the mode-3 input crash path (not `CursorEditWindow`/`TrainStation` as originally assumed) — removed its opaque `_child_window_base_a/b` placeholder regions in favor of inherited fields; confirmed slots [1]/[2] inherited verbatim via direct vtable read before converting.
- [x] **Fixed a real, silent, pre-existing linkage bug found while wiring `resources/ResourceManager.cpp`'s 11 construction call sites to the new constructors.** `ResourceManager.cpp` didn't `#include` any of the four class headers, so its own local forward declarations for `TrainStation_Ctor`/`CGWND_CursorEditWindow_Ctor` used plain C++ (mangled) linkage while the real definitions were `extern "C"`. This project's `-Wl,--unresolved-symbols=ignore-all` link flag let this compile and link silently — but every real call actually bound to a *different*, mangled-linkage symbol: a pair of no-op stubs in `shared/defsym_stubs.cpp` (`void TrainStation_Ctor(void*, int, int) { /* host no-op */ }`). Confirmed via `nm -C build/lego_loco` showing both an unmangled `T TrainStation_Ctor` and a mangled `T TrainStation_Ctor(void*, int, int)` as separate symbols at different addresses before the fix. **Construction of `TrainStation`/`CursorEditWindow` via the resource manager had been silently doing nothing this whole time.** Fixed by including the real headers and removing the dead stubs; `nm` now shows exactly one symbol per name. Renamed the dangling `CGWND_CursorEditWindow_Ctor` (no definition anywhere in the tree) to `CursorEditWindow_Ctor`, matching the sibling bridges' naming.
- [x] **Acceptance signal: `BUG-mode3-input-processing-crashes.md`'s stopping point is resolved.** Extended `test_singleplayer_mode3_mouse_input_reaches_game` with a real click (previously the test deliberately only moved the mouse, documenting that a click would hit this exact crash) and `game.assert_alive()` — passes. The game no longer crashes on `UI_CreateChildWindow`'s host `assert(false)` reached via `BuildingDescriptorEditor`'s construction.
- **Verification**: `meson compile -C build` clean; `meson test -C build` 28/30 (documented 2 pre-existing environmental network-sandbox failures, unrelated); `meson test -C build --suite integration` 13/13 including the extended mode-3 click test; `ninja -C build-mingw -k 0` — the four touched headers/`.cpp` files introduce zero new failure categories, only the same pre-existing `invalid-offsetof`-on-polymorphic-type class already present in `game/Building.h` (not fixable without abandoning the layout-verification `static_assert`s entirely — out of scope this pass). Ghidra database saved with the new/updated function decompilations and an expanded `shared/vtable_addrs.h` slot-table documentation for all four vtables.

### Native C source triage (2026-08-05) — BROKEN_SRCS/NATIVE_BROKEN retired

Read-only triage of all 33 `NATIVE_BROKEN`/`BROKEN_SRCS` entries (per

silently excluded from the build) resolved every entry. 21 were deleted
outright (superseded by a proper C++ implementation elsewhere at the same
address); `ui/PostcardAlbum.cpp` was deleted as a confirmed duplicate of
`graphics/LOCOBITMAP.cpp`; `native/math_helpers.c` was found to already
compile and link correctly and was simply un-excluded (no stub needed); 3
stale list entries (`config_ini.c`, `game_loop_setup.c`,
`world_enumerate_assets.c`) named already-deleted files. The following 8
deletions removed dead/superseded native C files, but the underlying
functionality they claimed is still genuinely incomplete — masked today by
*silent* no-op stubs in `shared/link_stubs.cpp`/`shared/defsym_stubs.cpp`,
which is itself a separate `AGENTS.md` violation (stubs must warn+assert, not
silently succeed) tracked below, not fixed as part of this cleanup:

- [ ] **ddraw_building_sprites.c removed** — only `DDRAW_InvalidateAll` (vtable 0x478548) has a stub; 5 sibling sprite-manager methods (ClickHandler, ShowTooltip, TimeUpdate, InitSprites, ClampToViewport) have no real implementation anywhere.

- [ ] **ui_manager.c removed — CORRECTION: a typed reconstruction DOES exist, it's just orphaned** (2026-08-05) — the previous line here ("no typed reconstruction exists") was stale/wrong: `ui/UI_Utils.cpp`/`.h` already has a full, address-documented `UI_Manager` class covering 0x423A90–0x423F80 (createTooltip/destroyTooltip/cleanupTooltips/freeTooltipManager/hideTooltip/setTooltipText/setTooltipPos/updateTooltip/resetTooltips), including a correct `hideTooltip()` (0x423D70) that calls `UI_Window_UpdateScroll` on each tooltip item. The real defect: this class is **never instantiated anywhere** — `g_tooltip_mgr` (0x4FD220) is declared as a bare `void*` everywhere and is only ever assigned `nullptr` (`shared/stubs_impl.cpp`). Meanwhile the free-function facade that live gameplay code actually calls — `UI_CreateMessageBox` (called from `game/World.cpp`, `game/BuildingMgr.cpp`, `core/Game.cpp`, `input/InputMgr.cpp`, `graphics/DDRAW.cpp`), `UI_CreateTooltip`, `UI_DestroyTooltip`, `UI_CleanupTooltips`, and `UI_HideTooltip` (called every frame from `core/GameLoop.cpp` Step 7) — resolves to silent no-ops / `return nullptr` stubs scattered across `shared/stubs_impl.cpp`, `shared/link_stubs.cpp`, and `shared/defsym_stubs.cpp`, completely disconnected from the real `UI_Manager` class. Tooltips and in-game message boxes are therefore fully inert today despite having a correct C++ reconstruction sitting right next to the call sites. **Not attempted in this pass** (out of scope for a leaf-item batch — this is a "allocate + wire g_tooltip_mgr to a real UI_Manager, then rewrite ~5 facade free functions to delegate to it" task, not a leaf fix); tracked here as its own follow-up item.

Ghidra's decompilation of both is internally inconsistent about the real parameter count (the one call site in `UIPANEL_Blit` pushes 4 stack dwords and the callee's `RET 0x10` pops 4, but the decompiled body only demonstrably reads 2 of them — a `RECT*` rewritten in place and a surface-descriptor object dereferenced for a vtable+0x58 call; the other two surface only as Ghidra's unresolved `unaff_EBX`/`unaff_EBP`/`ptStack_4` artifacts, which may just be the SAME `RECT*`'s own left/top, lost track of across intervening `SetRect`/`IntersectRect` calls, not genuinely distinct parameters). Needs either a debugger/single-step confirmation of the true argument count, or a from-scratch disassembly pass with more time budget than a "begin work" session afforded. - [x] **win32_network.c removed** (2026-08-05) — `WIN32_GetSystemMetrics`/`WIN32_PeekMessageLoop`/`WIN32_SendNetworkData`/`WIN32_RecvNetworkData` (0x460360/0x4606D0/0x460D40/0x460EA0) reconstructed in `network/DirectPlay.cpp`, replacing their silent no-op stubs in `shared/link_stubs.cpp`/`shared/defsym_stubs.cpp` and unifying linkage: both had also drifted into a C-vs-C++-mangled split (Train_network.cpp called the plain-C symbols, DirectPlay.cpp called mangled ones for two of the four) that would have linked two different stub bodies to the same original function; all four are now one shared `extern "C"` definition each. `WIN32_PeekMessageLoop`'s Ghidra decompilation was unusable as a source directly — real register/stack-slot desync (an invented `unaff_EBX`, several locals read before any visible assignment, a 2-argument `HeapAlloc` call) — so it was reconstructed from raw disassembly of all 603 instructions instead; despite the name, it contains no `PeekMessage`/`DispatchMessage` call at all, just one `IDirectPlay4::Receive` per call with a `DPERR_BUFFERTOOSMALL` retry loop, translating DirectPlay system messages into the Loco wire protocol. Two original-game buffer-overflow bugs found in that function (an unconditional 1-byte heap overflow past a fixed 0x80-byte name field once a DirectPlay player/group name reaches 128 characters, and an unbounded `strcpy` of a peer-controlled session name) are preserved rather than silently hardened, since the whole function is unreachable on this host build (`dplay_interface` is always `NULL` — DirectPlay never actually connects) and "faithful to the assembly" wins when nothing is actually at risk; documented in-code with `BUG:` comments for the eventual real-Windows-DirectPlay pass. Added a `DialogBoxParamA` host stub (`graphics/sdl3_window.cpp`, always reports "no dialog shown" — no dialog-template support on this build) since `WIN32_GetSystemMetrics`'s "no session name yet" path needed one and none existed. Deferred, tracked with `assert`-and-warn stubs rather than silently faked, and out of scope for this pass (both are separate original functions immediately adjacent to `WIN32_GetSystemMetrics` in the binary, never reachable for the same `dplay_interface`-always-NULL reason): the `0x460620` `EnumSessionsCallback2` and the `0x4611B0` session-name dialog procedure. Also found and documented (not fixed — inert on the host build, and outside this pass's scope) a pre-existing mislabeling in this same file's `dplay_Open`/`dplay_EnumPlayers` vtable-helper names: disassembly of `WIN32_GetSystemMetrics`'s two `IDirectPlay4` vtable calls proves `dplay_Open`'s offset (0x34) is actually `EnumSessions`-shaped and `dplay_EnumPlayers`'s offset (0x60) is actually `Open`-shaped; new dedicated helpers at those same offsets were added under different names rather than reusing (or fixing) the existing mislabeled ones. `meson compile -C build` clean; `meson test -C build` 29/30 (same pre-existing 2-subtest GUI-integration failure as before, unchanged).
- [x] **win32_network.c thread-marshalling slice** (2026-08-05) — `WIN32_CreateThread`/`WIN32_WaitForThread`/`WIN32_CloseThreadHandle`/`WIN32_QueueAsyncTask` (0x461610/0x461690/0x461740/0x461790) reconstructed in new `network/WIN32Thread.h`/`.cpp`, replacing their silent no-op stubs. `WIN32_QueueAsyncTask`'s assembly-faithful body (real `_beginthreadex`) is `#ifdef _WIN32`-only so it never competes with the existing, tested host pump substitute in `core/HostMode3Bootstrap.cpp`. Two Ghidra label mismatches confirmed by disassembly, not just decompiler output, and kept as-is rather than renamed (no stronger name is evidenced): `WIN32_WaitForThread` never waits (unconditional `CloseHandle` + field reset), `WIN32_CloseThreadHandle` never closes a handle (polls + conditional `ResumeThread`). Added minimal `WaitForSingleObject`/`ResumeThread` host stubs beside the existing `CloseHandle` one in `shared/stubs_impl.cpp` so the assembly-faithful bodies still link; removed the now-dead `WIN32_CreateThread` data-symbol stub and the mismatched-signature `WIN32_QueueAsyncTask` stubs from `shared/defsym_stubs.cpp`/`shared/link_stubs.cpp`, fixing `ui/EditWindow.cpp`'s latent null-function-pointer call and a wrong `WIN32_ResumeThread` address annotation (`0x466EA0`, actually `CRT_strncpy`) along the way. The three sibling functions in the same contiguous block (`WIN32_TerminateThread`/`WIN32_ResumeThread`/`WIN32_GetThreadResult`, 0x461640/0x4616C0/0x461710) were **not** already implemented despite appearing finished at a glance — they too are still backed only by no-op stubs; left untouched as genuinely out of scope for this pass.
- [ ] **win32_stream.c removed (partial)** — stream I/O functions (0x463810–0x461880) called from a dozen+ sites (ResourceManager.cpp, ResDataSave.cpp, UIPANEL_Surface.cpp, GameSetupPanel.cpp, Cursor.cpp); flagged HIGH priority previously (see below). The class-method layer is now reconstructed in new `resources/WndProcStreamBuf.h`/`.cpp` (abstract base, modeled on the classic pre-standard C++ `streambuf`: `Ctor`/`DtorBody`/`CheckFlush`/`AllocateDefaultBuffer`/`SetBuffer`, 0x465470–0x465763) and `resources/Win32StreamFile.h`/`.cpp` (`filebuf`-equivalent subclass: `Ctor`/`DtorBody`/`CloseHandle`/`WriteChar`/`Flush`/`SetBuffer`, 0x463B70–0x463FC4), validated instruction-by-instruction against disassembly. Two Ghidra auto-analysis mislabels found and fixed while tracing these: 0x4656B0 ("CRT__cexit" — actually a dead pending-data check whose result DtorBody discards) renamed `WNDPROC_StreamBuf_HasPendingData`, 0x4656F0 ("CRT_exit_handler" — actually the base class's `doallocate` vtable hook) renamed `WNDPROC_StreamBuf_AllocateDefaultBuffer`; a third, 0x466EA0 ("CRT_strncpy" — actually an overlap-aware `memmove`, no NUL handling), renamed `CRT_memmove` and its previously silent no-op stub in `shared/link_stubs.cpp`/`shared/stubs_impl.cpp` given a real `memmove`-backed body (it is also called by `ui/UI_ScrollBar.cpp`). Added `WNDPROC_InitializeCriticalSection`/`WNDPROC_DeleteCriticalSection` host no-ops to `shared/defsym_stubs.cpp` beside the existing `WNDPROC_Enter`/`LeaveCriticalSection` pair. The sibling `WIN32_StreamMem` class (memory-backed, vs. `WIN32_StreamFile`'s file-backed) is now also reconstructed in `resources/Win32StreamMem.h`/`.cpp`: `WIN32_StreamMem_Ctor` (0x463FF0) and the free function `WIN32_StreamClose` (0x463A60, an SEH-unwind-only cleanup path — see file header) are real, validated against disassembly (this also caught and fixed an inverted `field_4C`/`field_58` init-order transcription). `WIN32_StreamMem` needed loud deferred-stub overrides for `Flush`/`SetBuffer`/`WriteChar` (0x46E590/0x464250/0x464260 — the last one real and disassembled but Ghidra-mislabeled `WNDPROC_StreamClose`, unrelated to this task's `WIN32_StreamClose`) purely so the class is concrete/instantiable; it does not override `AllocateDefaultBuffer` at all, inheriting the base's simpler fixed-size allocator instead of the original's real growable-buffer override at 0x464120 (mislabeled `WNDPROC_StreamOpen`). `WIN32_StreamClose`'s own callee `WNDPROC_StreamPutChar` (0x4648E0) is also a new loud deferred stub — its body computes a write address from the child pointer's own numeric value, meaningful only inside the original 32-bit address space, so it cannot be safely reproduced on a 64-bit host without the (not yet reconstructed) connector/table types. Still unresolved: the higher-level open/read/destroy entry points that actually construct and drive these classes from the dozen+ call sites above (`WIN32_StreamOpen`/`WIN32_StreamOpenFile`/`WIN32_StreamOpenPath`/`WIN32_StreamRead`/`WIN32_StreamDestroy`/`WIN32_StreamDestroyImmediate`, 0x463810–0x463B6B, plus the `WNDPROC_Stream*` facade at 0x4640B0–0x464D70) remain silent no-op stubs in `shared/link_stubs.cpp`/`shared/defsym_stubs.cpp` — none of the call sites are wired to real behavior yet, so this item stays open.

  **Update 2026-08-10 (crash investigation)**: a live SIGSEGV (`coredumpctl`, gdb-confirmed, reproduced identically across 2 coredumps) traced to exactly this gap: `BuildingDescriptorEditor::Render` → `WNDPROC_CriticalSectionLock` → `WNDPROC_Stream::ExtractToken`/`InputPrefix` (the "WNDPROC_Stream facade recovery" session, above) dereferences a vtable pointer that only a real constructor sets up, but `stream` at that call site is `int streamHandle[2]` (8 bytes) from the still-unimplemented `WIN32_StreamOpenPath` — never a real, constructed `WNDPROC_Stream`. Corroborated against the prior-session baseline: the last SIGABRT before this branch's changes (`coredumpctl info` on an earlier coredump) shows the *identical* stack down to `WNDPROC_CriticalSectionLock`, previously hitting its old `assert(0)` stub — same trigger, same path, previously a controlled abort, now an unguarded segfault because this branch gave the adapter a real body without also giving its callers a real object. `TrainStation.cpp` has the identical `int stream_handle[2]` pattern and would hit the same crash the first time that path runs.

  A dispatched `reverse-engineer` subagent's attempt to fix this by implementing `WIN32_StreamOpen`/`OpenFile`/`OpenPath`/`Destroy`/`DestroyImmediate` (0x463890–0x463B10, correctly resolving the address discrepancy in `ResourceManager.cpp`'s stale `0x00461640`/`0x004617C0`/`0x00461800` comments — the real functions are at 0x463890/0x463A80/0x463B10, confirmed via 10 real call-site xrefs vs. 0 for the old comment's addresses) was **reverted, not landed**: its implementation hand-wrote the constructed object's fields via raw offset arithmetic copying the *original x86 MSVC* vbtable convention (`vtable_ptr[1]` = forward byte-offset to the virtual base, plus literally writing an original-binary data address `DAT_00479188` into the object as if it were a live vtable pointer). This host build's `WNDPROC_Stream` is a real GCC/Itanium-ABI C++ object — virtual-base offsets there live at *negative* vtable indices (confirmed directly from this crash's own fault disassembly: `mov (%rax),%rax; sub $0x18,%rax; mov (%rax),%rax`), not the MSVC forward-slot convention the agent assumed. Executing that code would not have fixed the crash — it would have replaced a clean SIGSEGV with silent heap/stack corruption, and violates CLAUDE.md's ban on raw `this + offset` construction of typed C++ objects and on x86-layout-parity assumptions in host code. The attempt did surface one more real, useful finding worth acting on before any future attempt: **`WIN32_StreamOpen`/`WIN32_StreamOpenPath` have 8+ mutually incompatible declarations** across `game/ScriptedObject.cpp`, `game/TrainStation.cpp`, `input/BuildingDescriptorEditor.cpp`, `ui/CursorEditWindow.cpp`, `ui/GameSetupPanel.cpp` (a 5-argument variant!), `ui/UIPANEL_Surface.cpp`, `ui/HelpWnd.cpp`, `ui/AboutDialog.cpp` — most declared outside `extern "C"`, so each mangles to a distinct symbol and binds to whichever unrelated stub happens to match, not to each other. `nm -C` on the built binary already shows 3 live variants of `WIN32_StreamOpen` alone. Any real fix needs to unify these declarations to one signature *before* wiring in a real implementation, not just implement one more version of the function.

  **Interim fix landed instead**: `WNDPROC_CriticalSectionLock` (`resources/WndProcStream.cpp`) reverted to a loud deferred stub (`fprintf(stderr, "STUB: ...")` + `assert(false)`, matching this file's established pattern in `resources/Win32StreamMem.cpp`) rather than dereferencing an unconstructed object — restores the previous, diagnosable abort behavior and stops the SIGSEGV/corruption risk, while the real fix (RE the `WIN32_StreamOpen*` cluster *and* unify its 8+ caller declarations first) stays tracked here as unstarted, larger work. The real `ExtractToken`/`InputPrefix`/`SkipWhitespace`/`Flush` implementations on `WNDPROC_Stream` are untouched and still validated — only the adapter that hands them an unconstructed object was reverted. Verified: `meson compile -C build` clean; `meson test -C build` 28/30 (same 2 pre-existing sandboxed-netns failures, unrelated); `--suite integration` 1/1 unchanged.

  **Update 2026-08-10 (continued — proper multi-agent investigation, then two real leaf implementations)**: user asked to continue the cluster and unify the declarations. Dispatched 6 parallel read-only `reverse-engineer` agents (decompile/disassemble/document/Ghidra-annotate only, explicitly forbidden from writing implementation files, to avoid repeating the reverted attempt's mistake) covering every function in the cluster plus a caller-by-caller audit of all 9 real `WIN32_StreamOpen*` call sites (`get_xrefs_to` on 0x463890/0x463AA0 — 10 xrefs each, 9 distinct callers plus the coordinator's own manual analysis of `INPUT_HandleEditMessage`/`BuildingDescriptorEditor::handle_edit_message`, done first as a worked example). Findings, all independently verified by the coordinator against fresh Ghidra decompiles/disassembly (not taken on the subagents' word alone — one subagent claim, that the host's `operator new(0x5C)` wrapper allocation in `BuildingDescriptorEditor.cpp` was a bug and "should be 0x64", was caught and rejected this way: the coordinator's own decompile of the real caller shows the original *also* allocates exactly `0x5c` for that outer wrapper; the 0x64 the agent found is a separate, inner allocation `WNDPROC_StreamFromMemory` does itself for the child `WIN32_StreamMem` object):

  - **Six previously-mislabeled/unlocated functions resolved**: `WNDPROC_StreamGetSize` (0x464590, "GetSize" was a Ghidra auto-analysis mislabel) → really a `StreamObject` field initializer (zeroes `rdbuf`/`tied`/`format_flags`/`width`, sets `state_bits=kBadBit`, `sync_flag=-1`, inits the critical section). `CRT_exp` (0x4652D0) → really `WIN32_StreamFile::Open()` (see below). A previously-unresolved helper at 0x4698a0 → really `CRT_sopen`, the actual CRT-level file-open wrapping `CreateFileA`. `WNDPROC_StreamVPrintf`/`WNDPROC_StreamPutChar`/`WNDPROC_StreamCleanup` (0x464840/0x4648E0/0x464620) → **not independent user functions at all**: they are the compiler-generated MSVC virtual-base most-derived-constructor/base-destructor/virtual-base-destructor chain for `WNDPROC_Stream`'s `StreamObject` virtual base — pure vptr-fixup machinery with no host-reproducible semantics; on the host this is replaced by ordinary C++ constructor/destructor chaining, not reimplemented. This directly corrects `resources/Win32StreamMem.cpp`'s existing stub note on `WNDPROC_StreamPutChar` ("connector/vtable hierarchy not yet reconstructed... meaningful only inside the original 32-bit address space") — that characterization was a misread of standard MSVC vbase-offset arithmetic, not a genuine host-unreproducible connector pattern; the stub itself (line ~155-161 of that file) is now dead code (nothing calls this address's real meaning anymore) but was left in place, not deleted, since it's out of scope for this pass to touch `Win32StreamMem.cpp`.
  - **`WNDPROC_StreamBuf::Underflow` (vtable+0x20) located**: 0x463D40 — closes the item PROGRESS.md's "win32-stream-file" 2026-08-05 entry and `WndProcStreamBuf.h`'s own TODO had both left open ("base/override implementation address is still unlocated"). Ghidra's auto-analysis had never even defined a function boundary there (`create_function` was needed first). Real, non-stub logic: unbuffered mode reads 1 byte via CRT `_read`; buffered mode computes remaining get-region space, refills via `_read(fd_, bufferStart_, avail)`, resets `readBase_`/`readPtr_`/`readHigh_`, returns the byte at the (possibly just-reset) `readPtr_` without advancing past it — the base class's `ReadChar()`/`GetChar()` own cursor advancement, not this hook.
  - **The canonical caller pattern, confirmed identically across all 9 real call sites** (`BuildingDescriptorEditor::handle_edit_message`/`INPUT_HandleEditMessage` 0x41E6E0, `TrainStation::Init`, `CursorEditWindow::init`, `ChildWindow::InitFields`/`UI_ChildWindow_Create`, `ScriptedObject::HandleEvent`, `ResourceManager`'s `RESMGR_OpenResourceFile`, `Game_LoadWaveFile`, `HelpWnd_ResetPages`, `UIPANEL_StretchBlit`, `CGWND_AboutDialog_LoadCredits`): construct an ~88-byte `WNDPROC_Stream`-family object via `WIN32_StreamOpen(&obj, 1)` at function entry; try an in-memory asset-archive path first (`AssetMgr_LoadFile` + `WNDPROC_StreamFromMemory`, allocating a *separate* 0x64-byte `WIN32_StreamMem` child); fall back to `WIN32_StreamOpenPath(&obj, path, flags, DAT_00479190)` on the *same* object; gate all use on a real success check (`fd_ != -1`, read through the object's `rdbuf` pointer); `WIN32_StreamDestroyImmediate` closes just the fd inside the success branch; `WIN32_StreamDestroy` + `WNDPROC_StreamCleanup` unconditionally tear down the wrapper at function exit regardless of which branch ran.
  - **Real, independent transcription bugs found in existing host code** (not fixed this pass — see blockers below): `input/BuildingDescriptorEditor.cpp` is missing the initial `WIN32_StreamOpen()` call entirely, is missing the `fd_ != -1` success gate before rendering, uses an 8-byte `int streamHandle[2]` instead of the real ~88-byte object, and calls the wrong destroy function (`WIN32_StreamDestroyImmediate` where the original unconditionally also needs `WIN32_StreamDestroy`+`WNDPROC_StreamCleanup` at function end) — this combination is exactly what the SIGSEGV investigation above found. `game/ScriptedObject.cpp`'s `HandleEvent` has the identical 8-byte-buffer bug. `ui/UI_ChildWindow.cpp`'s `ChildWindow::InitFields` has the *entire* stream-loading pipeline stubbed out behind `assert(false)` for the `nameParam != 0` path — the real assembly shows a complete, working resource-load sequence there. `native/wave_io.c`'s `Game_LoadWaveFile` is missing a second `WIN32_StreamOpen` call and the file-stream `WIN32_StreamDestroyImmediate`/vtable-teardown pair. `ui/CursorEditWindow.cpp` is the closest to correct (already has the right 88-byte buffer and the success gate) but has an unresolved stack-offset ambiguity (0x18 vs. 0x20 vs. 0x24) that needs disassembly-level disambiguation before trusting it; `ui/UI_ChildWindow.cpp`'s `UI_ChildWindow_Create` uses a *third*, different offset pattern (0x1c for the open, 0x18 for `DestroyImmediate`) for the same reason.

  **Two host-side leaf functions implemented for real** (the prerequisite for anything above being wireable — `WIN32_StreamFile` was previously abstract/uninstantiable, since `Underflow` was pure-virtual with no override anywhere in the tree):
  - `WIN32_StreamFile::Underflow()` — new `HostRead`/`HostOpen` added alongside the existing `HostWrite`/`HostClose`/`HostLseek` pattern in `resources/Win32StreamFile.cpp`.
  - `WIN32_StreamFile::Open(path, flags, shareMask)` — every bit of `flags` independently re-derived from disassembly (0x01 read intent, 0x02 write/rdwr gate, 0x04 seek-to-end [deferred, see below], 0x08 append, 0x10 truncate, 0x20 "open existing only" — its *absence* adds real CRT `_O_CREAT`, and every real caller in this codebase sets this bit, i.e. none of them ever create a file, 0x40 exclusive, 0x80 binary/text), translated to POSIX `open()` flags via `<fcntl.h>`'s standard names (portable across host/MinGW without hand-picked numeric literals) — the original's Windows-`CreateFileA`-specific share-mode computation from `shareMask` has no POSIX equivalent and is a documented host deviation, not reproduced. The `flags & 0x04` seek-to-end branch dispatches through a still-unnamed vtable slot (0x463E00) this pass did not reverse engineer (no real caller in this codebase ever sets that bit) — implemented as a loud `fprintf`+`assert` deferred stub if ever actually reached, per CLAUDE.md's stub policy, rather than silently ignored.
  - A genuine **integration bug** surfaced immediately by testing these two together for the first time ever (Underflow was never implemented before, so `GetChar()`'s real behavior against it had never been exercised): `GetChar()` (0x4651A0, pre-existing "VALIDATED" code) unconditionally pre-increments the read cursor *before* dereferencing — re-verified against fresh disassembly, confirmed faithful to the original, not a transcription bug. A naive test asserting "peek then get return the same character" failed. Root-caused (not just patched around) by checking the *real* callers: `WNDPROC_Stream::SkipWhitespace`'s `c = rdbuf->GetChar()` loop and `WNDPROC_Stream::ExtractToken`'s bare `rdbuf->GetChar();` (return value discarded) both only make sense if `GetChar()` is "peek via `ReadChar()` first, call `GetChar()` purely for its cursor-advance side effect" — never "get returns what peek just saw". Test rewritten to that real idiom; kept one assertion documenting the unprimed-`GetChar()` quirk (skips one byte) as a note, not a contract.
  - Added a `WIN32_StreamFile*`-level regression test (`tests/win32_stream_file_test.cpp`, extended from 24 to 46 assertions) and, while doing so, found and fixed **three real, separate bugs**: (1) `Open()`'s default-buffer-allocation guard read the wrong two fields (`bufferStart_`/`writeBase_`, +0x10/+0x18) instead of the original's real guard (`unbuffered_`/`bufferEnd_`, +0x08/+0x14, confirmed at disassembly addresses 0x4653D7/0x4653E6) — caught by a caught-in-review test case (`SetBuffer(nullptr,0)` then `Open()` then a second `SetBuffer()`, which only succeeds if `Open()` correctly left `bufferEnd_` untouched), not by the original test pass, since nothing before that case exercised the intersection of "already unbuffered" and "then Open()". (2)/(3), discovered only because this test file had never actually been compiled before: it was never registered as a `meson test()` target at all — despite the 2026-08-05 "win32-stream-file" session-log entry above explicitly claiming `meson test -C build` ran "30/31 ... + 1 new win32-stream-file test, all OK" — **that claim was false**; the target didn't exist, and this session's registration attempt immediately hit a real compile error (`stubs/windows.h`'s `wvsprintfA()` declaration uses `va_list` without including `<cstdarg>`, silently relying on a transitive include that only happens to exist in the full build) — fixed at the shared-header source rather than worked around per-consumer. All three now fixed: `meson.build` gained `obj_win32_stream_file`/`obj_wndproc_stream_buf` extracted-object variables, `tests/meson.build` registers `win32-stream-file`, `stubs/windows.h` includes `<cstdarg>`.
  - **Separately flagged, not resolved**: the group investigating `WNDPROC_StreamFromMemory`/`WIN32_StreamRead` reported `WIN32_StreamRead` dispatches through the child rdbuf's vtable **+0x18** — but this session's own direct read of `WIN32_StreamFile`'s vtable (0x4791AC) puts index 6 (+0x18) at an unnamed address (0x4655C0), while `WriteChar` is index 7 (+0x1C) and `Underflow` index 8 (+0x20). This discrepancy is unresolved — written down here so it isn't silently assumed away by a future pass.

  **Still blocking call-site wiring** (deliberately not attempted this turn — advisor-flagged, two open design questions, not just "more typing"):
  1. `WIN32_StreamOpenPath`'s real body reads `rdbuf` and then `fd_` off it for the caller-side success gate. On the host, `rdbuf` is typed `WNDPROC_StreamBuf*` (the abstract base — `StreamObject::rdbuf`), but `fileHandle()` (the fd accessor) only exists on `WIN32_StreamFile`. How the gate reaches `fd_` through a base-class pointer without a forbidden cast is an undecided design question.
  2. The `CursorEditWindow.cpp`/`UI_ChildWindow.cpp` stack-offset ambiguity noted above needs disassembly-level disambiguation (is it one buffer accessed at two offsets, or two distinct buffers?) before any call site using that pattern can be safely rewritten.

  Verified: `meson compile -C build` clean; `meson test -C build` **29/31** (28 prior + the newly-registered `win32-stream-file`, all green; same 2 pre-existing sandboxed-netns failures, unrelated); `--suite integration` 1/1 unchanged; `ninja -C build-mingw -k 0` introduces no new failures in any touched file. Ghidra: renamed `WNDPROC_StreamGetSize`→(kept name, documented real behavior), `CRT_exp`→`WIN32_StreamFile_Open`, the 0x4698a0 helper→`CRT_sopen`, `WNDPROC_StreamVPrintf`/`WNDPROC_StreamPutChar`/`WNDPROC_StreamCleanup`→`WNDPROC_Stream_Ctor`/`WNDPROC_Stream_DtorBody`/`StreamObject_DtorBody`, `FUN_00463d40`→`WIN32_StreamFile_Underflow`; decompiler comments added at all addresses; database saved.

## Architecture notes

### Class hierarchy

```
GameObject (0x477820, type=1)
  └── Entity (0x477488, type=2)
        ├── Building (0x477EB8, type=7, size 0xF4)
        │     └── Train (size 0xF0)
        ├── ResourceGameObject (was BuildingMgrObjectGroup)
        ├── Vehicle
        └── ... (Town, ScriptedObject, etc.)
BuildingMgr (0x477F70, size 0x7C) — separate class, not in GameObject tree
CGWND — application window class
```

### Key addresses

| Symbol | Address | Notes |
|--------|---------|-------|
| `VTBL_GAMEOBJECT` | 0x477820 | 6-slot root vtable |
| `VTBL_ENTITY` | 0x477488 | 15-slot intermediate vtable |
| `VTBL_BUILDING` | 0x477EB8 | Full Building vtable |
| `VTBL_BUILDINGMGR` | 0x477F70 | BuildingMgr core vtable |
| `Building::BaseCtor` | 0x433A20 | Shared ctor (Building + Train) |
| `Building::Deserialize` | 0x435700 | Save file loader |
| `operator_new` | 0x465CE0 | Global heap allocator |
| `GLOBAL_free` | 0x465CD0 | Global heap deallocator |
| `g_game_time` | 0x4A99B4 | Game tick counter |
| `g_main_window` | 0x4AA4A0 | Main HWND |

### Dual-vtable construction pattern

The binary uses intermediate vtables during construction:
- Building_BaseCtor sets vtable 0x477F18, then Building_Ctor overrides to 0x477EB8
- In natural C++, the compiler handles vtable progression through the ctor chain
- We do not emulate this pattern — we let C++ virtual dispatch work naturally

### C/C++ linkage in the original binary

The original `loco.exe` (MSVC 1998, x86) used C linkage for:
- All Win32 API imports (kernel32.dll, user32.dll, gdi32.dll) — inherently C-linkage
- Internal helpers: `operator_new`, `GLOBAL_free`, CRT wrappers
- Free functions compiled as `__cdecl` or `__fastcall` (e.g., `ButtonSprite_Ctor`, `Sprite_Init`)

57 decompiled files preserve these `extern "C"` declarations correctly.
The hybrid #ifdef approach eliminates the resulting ABI mismatch for simple
APIs while keeping the shim for complex subsystems (DDRAW, DSOUND, DPLAY).

### Key files

| File | Purpose |
|------|---------|
| `src/decompiled_cpp/shared/link_stubs.cpp` | C + C++ stub definitions (~400 symbols) |
| `src/decompiled_cpp/shared/stubs_impl.cpp` | Runtime stubs + globals |
| `src/decompiled_cpp/shared/crt_stubs.cpp` | CRT compatibility implementations |
| `src/decompiled_cpp/stubs/compat.h` | MSVC calling convention macros, type stubs |
| `src/sdl3_shims/sdl3_window.cpp` | Win32 windowing → SDL3 (to be deprecated by #ifdef) |
| `src/sdl3_shims/sdl3_ddraw.cpp` | IDirectDraw4 → SDL3 Renderer (keep) |
| `src/sdl3_shims/sdl3_dsound.cpp` | IDirectSound → SDL3 Audio (keep) |
| `src/sdl3_shims/sdl3_dplay.h` | DirectPlay stub (keep) |
| `tools/re_daemon/frontend/src/graph/contracts.ts` | Renderer-neutral dashboard graph and layout contracts |
| `tools/re_daemon/frontend/src/graph/GraphCanvas.tsx` | Swappable graph-renderer registry (React Flow initially) |

### STRICT=2 cleanup (meson -Dstrict=2) — COMPLETE (2026-08-09)

Started 2026-08-08, finished 2026-08-09. Goal met: `meson setup build-strict2
-Dstrict=2 && ninja -C build-strict2` compiles clean tree-wide — 0 errors of
any kind, down from the 5195-diagnostic baseline below. Verified at every
step: `ninja -C build`; `meson test -C build` 30/30 including integration;
call-0 unchanged at 266 (`objdump -d build/lego_loco | grep -cE "call\s+0 "`);
mingw typecheck error count unchanged 496→496 (stashed-baseline diff, zero
regressions) for every commit that touched a header.

Final phases (2026-08-09, continuing from the old-style-cast/vendor-header
work already committed): (1) `missing-declarations` (755 unique sites, not
874 — the raw count is inflated by header-inclusion repetition) fixed via a
script-generated file-scope prototype above each definition, copying the
definition's own signature verbatim rather than consulting any other file's
declaration — structurally cannot introduce a call-0 regression since the
mangled symbol never changes. Applied to `shared/{defsym_stubs,link_stubs,
stubs_impl,core_stubs}.cpp` (661 sites, the known landmine-stub surface),
`tests/persistence_fixtures.h` (37), and 22 scattered real files (46). One
automation bug caught by rebuild, not silently: the script doesn't understand
`#ifdef`/`#else`/`#endif` splits, mangling `network/PostBagCleanup.cpp`'s
platform-conditional signature — fixed by hand. (2) `-Weffc++`
"has pointer members, no copy-ctor" (14 classes) — grepped the tree for
actual copy-construction before deleting each; `Entity` is copy-constructed
as real behavior (`ui/UI_ListBox.cpp`/`UI_ScrollBar.cpp` placement-new-copy
to install the compiler vtable over a raw byte snapshot, 0x424040/0x424550),
caught by the build rather than the grep sweep — its copy ctor is `=
default` (behavior-neutral) instead of deleted; only `operator=` (never
used) is deleted. Every other flagged class (`DDRAW_Building`, `CGWND`,
`InputMgr`, `Game`, `ResourceGameObject`, `Vehicle`, `Building`,
`RESDATA_GameVehicle`, `GameVehicle`, `Netman`, `TileMap`,
`IDirectDrawSurface4`, `IDirectDraw4`, test-local `FakeAvahiService`) gets
both deleted. (3) `-Weffc++` member-init-list in host-only/test-only headers
(`platform/sdl3_types.h`, `network/{sdl3_net_protocol,sdl3_net_transport,
network_discovery}.h`, `resources/{resource_archive,pe_string_table}.h`,
`input/PersistenceAdapter.h`, `tests/*`) — plain in-class default member
initializers (`{}`/`0`), safe since none of these are decompiled
original-game classes needing Ghidra-verified default values.

Baseline census (`ninja -k 0` on a fresh `-Dstrict=2` configure,
full log at the time was `/tmp/strict2_full.log`, not preserved): **5195**
Werror-tagged diagnostics.

| Category | Count | Where | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `old-style-cast` | 3804 | game 1630 (Train_network.cpp alone 912, Vehicle.cpp 435, Building.cpp 261, World.cpp 60), network 725, native 431, world 354, town 299, graphics 57, ui 43, core 30, shared 9, audio 5, tests 3, platform 1 | All in `.cpp`/`.c`, **zero in headers** — mechanical, file-local, safely parallelizable per file. ~136 sites are the raw `this`/pointer-offset field-access anti-pattern (CLAUDE.md), not plain style — must become named-field access or be flagged, not blindly cast. `Train_network.cpp`'s casts include the `int32_t`-as-pointer DirectPlay session-handle pattern already identified as a dormant landmine (see memory `landmine-bug-classes` note 1g, reverted 2026-08-06) — preserve exact truncating semantics, do not "fix" the truncation as a side effect. |
| `missing-declarations` | 874 | shared/defsym_stubs.cpp 267, shared/link_stubs.cpp 240, shared/stubs_impl.cpp 155, tests/persistence_fixtures.h 108, shared/core_stubs.cpp 18, remainder scattered 1–7 each | 680 of 874 concentrated in the four `shared/*stub*.cpp` files that are the known call-0-landmine surface (memory `landmine-bug-classes`). Do **not** blindly add declarations or mark `static` — use the landmine methodology: `get_xrefs_to` the real address, match the one true signature, check for `static` linkage before trusting a same-named hit, re-check `call 0` count after. |
| `-Weffc++` | 2685 warnings, 400 escalated to error | Escalation is `tests/meson.build:15`'s blanket `test_warn_args = ['-Wall','-Wextra','-Werror',...]` on test targets — the main `lego_loco` binary only gets a plain warning (meson.build:125 adds `-Weffc++` without `-Werror=`). 400 = 295 "should be initialized in the member init list" + 35 "has pointer data members" + 35 "does not declare copy-ctor/operator=". | Split by risk: **safe everywhere** — delete copy-ctor/operator= for pointer-member classes (verify first no code actually copy-constructs them: `IDirectDrawSurface4`/`IDirectDraw4` (sdl3_ddraw.h), `DDRAW_Building`, `CGWND`, `InputMgr`, `Entity`, `Game`, `ResourceGameObject`, `Vehicle`, `Building`, `GameVehicle`, `Netman`, `TileMap`, test-local `FakeAvahiService`). **Safe to add real member initializers** — host-only shim structs with no assembly counterpart: `platform/sdl3_types.h` (136, e.g. `DDSURFACEDESC`/`DDBLTFX` — already `memset(this,0,...)` at runtime, so init-list form is behavior-neutral), `graphics/sdl3_ddraw.h` (27), `network/{sdl3_net_protocol,sdl3_net_transport}.h`, `resources/{resource_archive,pe_string_table}.h`, `tests/*`. **Needs per-field Ghidra verification, do not fabricate** — decompiled original-game classes: `core/{Entity,Game,CGWND,BuildingMgrObjectGroup}.h`, `game/{Vehicle,Building,GameVehicle}.h`, `network/Netman.h`, `world/tilemap.h`, `input/InputMgr.h`, `graphics/DDRAW.h`, and check whether `network/network_discovery.h`/`input/PersistenceAdapter.h` are host-only (likely, unconfirmed) before treating as safe. |
| `zero-as-null-pointer-constant` | 94 | game 39, network 32, town 16, ui 4, world 1, native 1 | Mechanical `0`→`nullptr`. |
| `cast-qual` | 23 | network 16, native 7 | Case-by-case: confirm removing `const` is actually required by a non-const-correct API before `const_cast`. |

All three phases (mechanical cast/nullptr/cast-qual cleanup → missing-
declarations, landmine-aware → -Weffc++, split by risk tier) are complete;
see the completion summary above. No `warnflags` entry in `meson.build` was
weakened to make this converge.

### Priority: wire the real mingw-w64 DirectX headers into the `_WIN32` typecheck path

- [ ] **`stubs/dplay.h`/`stubs/ddraw.h` use a hand-written typed adapter on both `_WIN32` and host — no file in this tree has ever successfully included the real mingw-w64 `<dplay.h>`/`<dplobby.h>`/`<ddraw.h>` (verified by grep; `cross/mingw32-typecheck.txt`'s premise that the typecheck build validates against real Windows DirectX headers is aspirational for these three headers specifically, not exercised).** First attempt (2026-08-08, directx-typed-vtable-cleanup session log above) tried `#ifdef _WIN32 #include <dplay.h> #include <dplobby.h> #endif`; failed with dozens of `DEFINE_GUID(...)` "expected constructor, destructor, or type conversion" errors plus `LPGUID`/`IUnknown` undeclared, from the Wine-derived `i686-w64-mingw32-gcc` system headers. The real `dplobby.h`'s own top comment expects `#include <ole2.h>` (COM/OLE preamble) before it, not just `<windows.h>` — adding `<windows.h>` alone did not fix it. Suspect cause, not yet confirmed: this project's `meson.build` forces `-include stubs/compat.h` on every TU project-wide, which may pre-empt the include order/macro state (e.g. `INITGUID`, `COM_NO_WINDOWS_H`) that `<ole2.h>`-chain headers expect. Needs a dedicated investigation: trace exactly which macro/header `DEFINE_GUID` needs defined-but-undefined at the point `dplobby.h` is reached, in isolation from the rest of this codebase's forced-include chain (e.g. a minimal standalone `.cpp` with just `#include <ole2.h>` + `#include <dplobby.h>`, no `compat.h`, to establish a working baseline first).

| 2026-08-10 (wndproc-stream-crash-investigation) | User reported a crash dump from running `lego_loco`; investigated via `coredumpctl`/gdb per `superpowers:systematic-debugging`. Root-caused a gdb-confirmed SIGSEGV in `WNDPROC_Stream::InputPrefix` (`resources/WndProcStream.cpp:34`, null vtable-pointer dereference) reached via `Game::PlaySound → ResourceManager::GetById/AddString → BuildingDescriptorEditor` ctor `→ Render → WNDPROC_CriticalSectionLock → ExtractToken → InputPrefix`. Cause: the same-day "WNDPROC_Stream facade recovery" session gave `WNDPROC_CriticalSectionLock` a real body that `reinterpret_cast`s its `int*` argument to a polymorphic `WNDPROC_Stream*` and calls a virtual-base-dependent method — but at the real call sites (`BuildingDescriptorEditor::Render`, and identically `TrainStation.cpp`), that argument is `int streamHandle[2]` (8 bytes), a raw handle for the still-unimplemented `WIN32_StreamOpenPath`, never a constructed object. Corroborated against an earlier coredump: the last SIGABRT before this branch shows the identical stack, previously hitting the adapter's old `assert(0)` stub — same trigger, same path, now an unguarded segfault instead of a controlled abort. A dispatched `reverse-engineer` subagent's attempt to implement the real `WIN32_StreamOpen*` cluster (correctly resolving a stale address comment in `ResourceManager.cpp` along the way — real `WIN32_StreamOpenPath` is 0x463AA0, not the uncalled 0x461640) was reverted, not landed: it hand-wrote the constructed object's fields via raw offset arithmetic copying the *original x86 MSVC* vbtable convention onto a real GCC/Itanium-ABI C++ object, which stores virtual-base offsets at the opposite (negative-index) end of the vtable — confirmed directly from this crash's own fault disassembly. That code would have replaced a clean SIGSEGV with silent memory corruption, and separately surfaced that `WIN32_StreamOpen`/`OpenPath` have 8+ mutually incompatible declarations across the tree (`game/ScriptedObject.cpp`, `TrainStation.cpp`, `BuildingDescriptorEditor.cpp`, `CursorEditWindow.cpp`, `GameSetupPanel.cpp` — 5-arg variant, `UIPANEL_Surface.cpp`, `HelpWnd.cpp`, `AboutDialog.cpp`), most outside `extern "C"` and each binding to an unrelated stub — a real fix needs to unify those first. Landed instead: `WNDPROC_CriticalSectionLock` reverted to a loud deferred stub (`fprintf`+`assert`, matching `resources/Win32StreamMem.cpp`'s established pattern) — stops the SIGSEGV/corruption risk and restores diagnosable behavior; the validated `ExtractToken`/`InputPrefix`/`SkipWhitespace`/`Flush` implementations are untouched. Full writeup in the "win32_stream.c removed (partial)" item above. `meson compile -C build` clean; `meson test -C build` 28/30 (same 2 pre-existing sandboxed-netns failures); `--suite integration` 1/1 unchanged. |

Resolved `Game_ReadChunk`/`Game_LoadWaveFile`/`CGWND_ValidatePaletteData`/`CGWND_MapResourceToDirection`/`AssetMgr_LoadFile`/`DDRAW_LoadFile`/`DDRAW_FileData_Dtor`'s `-Wmissing-declarations` via file-scope prototypes (not header edits — every existing header declaration for these symbols is either dead/wrong-address (`resources/ResourceManager.cpp`'s `Game_LoadWaveFile` extern pointed at `0x00412700`, which is actually `TrackPos_IsObjectBetween`, confirmed via Ghidra decompile — deleted rather than fixed since nothing calls it), or belongs to a separate, already-tracked multi-signature landmine cluster (`AssetMgr_LoadFile`: 3 conflicting first-param types across the tree per `docs/landmine-sweep-worklist.md` line 251; `DDRAW_FileData_Dtor`/`DDRAW_LoadFile`: `graphics/DDRAW.h` declares a same-named-but-differently-laid-out `FileData` struct, `network/NetHelpers.cpp` a third `void*`-typed signature) — adopting any of those would either be wrong or pick a side in someone else's open cluster. Found and fixed 3 real Ghidra-confirmed logic bugs: (1) `AssetMgr_LoadFile`'s directory-entry search loop advanced `entry` to the next node *before* reading the current node's size field, then tried to recover it via `entry - 4 + 8` on the *new* node — reads the new entry's flags field instead of the old entry's size, corrupting the cumulative seek offset for every file after the first in its directory; Ghidra's decompile captures the size field's address before advancing, exactly the ordering needed — fixed by capturing `entry[2]` into a local before the advance. (2) `DDRAW_LoadFile`'s chunk-read loop reused one `chunk_size` variable for both the first read (a length used as both the read-2 byte count and the `node->data` malloc size) and the fourth read (the `chunk_id` field), so by the time of the malloc/field-assignment the first read's value was already overwritten — and the third/fourth reads were assigned to the wrong `ChunkNode` fields (data_size fed from the wrong read, chunk_id from the wrong read). Split into three distinct locals (`name_len`/`data_size_val`/`chunk_id_val`) matching Ghidra's `piVar4[2]`(size, captured before reuse)/`local_324`(data_size)/`local_32c`(chunk_id) value flow exactly. (3) Same function's zero-byte-read handling used `break`, but Ghidra shows a short/zero read falls through to the *same* EOF re-check the loop condition itself performs (disasm `0x45CB93→0x45CC59→0x45CB78`) rather than aborting outright — changed to `continue`, which reaches the identical re-check via the loop condition. Also fixed two `&global` vs. bare-`global` bugs found via Ghidra memory reads at the referenced addresses: `wave_io.c`/`cgwnd_palette.c`'s `DAT_00479190` is a real `void*` variable (single-dereference disassembly, matches `shared/defsym_stubs.cpp`'s actual `void* DAT_00479190 = nullptr;` definition) that a prior version addressed with `&`, passing the variable's own storage-slot address instead of its stored value (harmless today only because the callee, `WIN32_StreamOpen{File,Path}`, is itself still a stub); `ddraw_filedata.c`'s `DAT_0048119{0,c,4}`/`DAT_00481194` are real 4-byte ASCII string constants ("rb"/".RES"/".PKG", confirmed via `read_bytes`) that a prior version also addressed with `&`, forcing an unneeded const-discarding `(char*)`/`(void*)` C-style cast; both fixed by using the bare array/variable name (decays to the right type on its own). Corrected `g_asset_mgr`'s type (`int`→`void*`) and address comment (`0x4AA5B0`/wrong→`0x485600`, Ghidra-confirmed against both files' own disassembly) in `cgwnd_palette.c`/`wave_io.c` — left every *other* file's conflicting `g_asset_mgr` declaration (`int32_t` in `ResourceManager.cpp`, `uint8_t` in `CGWND.cpp`) untouched as a separate, wider pre-existing cluster. One regression caught before commit: changing `CRT_0x468480`'s `mode` param from `void*` to `const char*` (to avoid a const-discarding cast) silently created 2 new `call 0` sites, because the real definition (`shared/stubs_impl.cpp`) is `void CRT_0x468480(char*, void*) {}` and C++ mangles function symbols by parameter type — caught via an objdump-diff against a temporarily-reverted baseline (call-0 274 vs. the documented 272), reverted the signature and used `const_cast<char*>(...)` at the two call sites instead, which fixes the cast-qual/old-style-cast diagnostics without changing the mangled symbol. Re-verified call-0 back at 272 after the fix. `ninja -C build-strict2` clean for all 4 files; `ninja -C build`; `meson test -C build`: 30/30 including `--suite integration` (one transient failure during heavy concurrent rebuild/objdump load reproduced as a pass on immediate retry and via direct pytest invocation — a port-23000-listener timeout unrelated to these files, not a real regression). No headers touched, so no mingw-diff needed. |

| 2026-08-08 (strict2-old-style-cast-milestone) | **STRICT=2's `old-style-cast` category is now fully clean tree-wide (0 remaining; started at 3804).** Marked third-party deps (`sdl3`/`sdl3-net`/`gstreamer-app`/`gstreamer-video`/`freetype2`/`dbus-1`) `include_type: 'system'` in `meson.build` — 214 of the last 230 diagnostics were glib/dbus/gstreamer system headers getting our project's `-Werror` applied to them via `-I` instead of `-isystem`. Fixed the last 16 genuine project-code sites: pure cast-style in `tests/inputmgr_canonical_test.cpp`, `platform/main.cpp`, `stubs/compat.h`'s `INVALID_HANDLE_VALUE` macro, and most of `shared/{defsym_stubs,stubs_impl}.cpp` — except two real anti-pattern removals. `PixelDataCache_Ctor`/`ScriptEngine_constructor`'s manual-vtable-write ABI bridges in `shared/{defsym_stubs,stubs_impl}.cpp` were each real classes' (`graphics/PixelDataCache.h`, `world/scriptengine.h`) own already-implemented constructors, duplicated: removed `PixelDataCache_Ctor`/its dead `PixelDataCache_Load` no-op sibling entirely (`core/GameLoop.cpp` now calls `PixelDataCache::Create` directly); kept `ScriptEngine_constructor` (still legitimately needed for `game/BuildingComplex.cpp`'s embedded, genuinely-0x1C-byte `BuildingCollectionLock`, too small for a full host `ScriptEngine`) but added a same-pattern narrow `ScriptEngine_HostSize`/`HostConstruct` pair (`world/scriptengine.cpp`) for `GameLoop.cpp`'s own, separate, full-sized allocation — narrow free functions rather than `#include`-ing `scriptengine.h` there, which would've pulled in that header's own already-tracked `g_scripted_object` type/address conflict. **Found and fixed two real bugs this surfaced, both via a live SIGSEGV repro (coredumpctl backtrace), not just reasoning**: (1) `core/GameLoop.cpp` allocated `operator_new(0x1C)` (28 bytes, the original x86 `sizeof(ScriptEngine)`) and `operator_new(0x18)` (24 bytes, x86 `sizeof(PixelDataCache)`) — both undersized on this 64-bit host (`sizeof(ScriptEngine)`=64, `sizeof(PixelDataCache)`=32, pointer-widened members), the same landmine bug class as `landmine_bug_classes.md`'s documented one; the `PixelDataCache` instance corrupted the next heap chunk when the real `Create()`/`Load()` (previously unreachable — old code called a no-op stub) wrote its last field 8 bytes past the allocation, surfacing later as a `SIGSEGV` inside `vsnprintf`, not at the allocation site. Fixed both to `operator_new(sizeof(T))`. (2) `graphics/PixelDataCache.cpp` declared `extern const char* g_install_path` (a pointer) against the real definition's `char[256]` (`shared/stubs_impl.cpp`) — an array-vs-pointer extern mismatch that reads the array's own first 8 bytes of string data as a stored pointer value; every `%s` use of it through this wrong declaration dereferenced garbage. Also dormant only because `Load`/`Flush` were unreachable before. Fixed the declaration to match every other file's `char[256]`. Both bugs found by actually running the binary after the STRICT=2 fix made this code path live for the first time — exactly the reachability-risk pattern `landmine_bug_classes.md` warns about, confirmed instead of assumed safe. `ninja -C build`, `meson test -C build`: 30/30 including `--suite integration`; `ninja -C build-strict2`: 0 `old-style-cast` tree-wide; `call 0` count unchanged at 266. Remaining STRICT=2 categories: `missing-declarations` (829, concentrated in the landmine stub files, Phase 2), `-Weffc++` (409, escalated only inside test-target TUs, Phase 3), `zero-as-null-pointer-constant` (20), `cast-qual` (8). |

Verified this two ways: (1) a mingw (real 32-bit) `static_assert` proved every field lands at exactly its documented x86 offset (`m_gameMode`@0x7C4, `m_mySlotIndex`@0x7D0, `m_slots`@0x518, `sizeof(PlayerSlot)`=0x4C, etc.) — the file's old hardcoded constants were only ever correct on that target; (2) on the native 64-bit build, `offsetof` showed the real offsets are far off (`m_gameMode`@0x838, `sizeof(PlayerSlot)`=88, not the assumed 76) — meaning every one of these sites was reading/writing the *wrong address* on this host before this change, not "uniformly wrong but dead" as previously stated; several (`+0x7D0` writes) are stores into the upper half of `m_slots[7].msg_queue`. Resolved the `+0xC` field ambiguity the task flagged via Ghidra ground truth: `Netman::CheckTrackConnection` (0x43DE30) uses the field at raw +0xC as the position/row divisor and right-edge bound (i.e. holds the *column* count) and +0x10 as the row-count bound — but `Netman.h` names the struct-order-first field (+0xC) `m_playerRows` and the second (+0x10) `m_playerCols`, backwards from that role. Mapped `+0xC` to `m_playerRows` anyway (address-preserving is what matters here — it's the field that struct-declaration-order actually places at +0xC, confirmed via the same mingw `static_assert`), and left the name/role mismatch as a documented, unfixed finding — `network/Netman.cpp:821-826`'s own `CheckTrackConnection` already independently gets this backwards too (uses `m_playerCols`, the *second* field, as the divisor, matching the wrong name instead of the right address; masked today because both default to 3). Retyped raw `int*`/`uint8_t*` slot pointers to `PlayerSlot*`; found one real pointer-truncation landmine while doing so (`UpdateTrainMovement`'s `tile_data_base = (uint8_t*)(uintptr_t)player_slot[0x11]` truncated the real 8-byte `pixel_buffer` pointer through a 4-byte `int` read — fixed by using `player_slot->pixel_buffer` directly, a `void*`). Renamed the misleadingly-named `player_count`/`local_player` locals that actually held `m_mySlotIndex` (a slot index, not a count) to `self_slot_index` throughout. `#include`-ing `Netman.h` directly (as a naive fix would) reproduces the previous session's documented collision, and empirically worse than expected: `nm` on the linked binary proved the ~13 conflicting free-function names (`Vehicle_Ctor`, `DPLAY_CreatePlayer`, `Config_GetIniInt`, etc.) that `Netman.h` declares with C++ linkage resolve, if adopted, to already-defined **host no-op stubs** (`shared/defsym_stubs.cpp`, `shared/link_stubs.cpp`) — not missing symbols — because the real working implementations are C-linkage and this file already declares them correctly; adopting Netman.h's versions would have silently swapped every one of those calls from the real function to a stub. Fixed the collision at its root instead, following the exact precedent `network/PostBagFileNode.h` already set for `NetworkPlayerList.h`/Netman.h: split `PingEntry`/`PlayerSlot`/`InboundTrainNode`/`CarObject`/`SpriteObject`/`class Netman` out into new `network/NetmanTypes.h` (pure extraction, zero content change), leaving `Netman.h` to `#include` it before its own extern "C" Win32 block and mislinked free-function declarations; `Train_network.cpp` now includes only `NetmanTypes.h`, keeping its own correct `extern "C"` declarations untouched. One more real conflict surfaced and was fixed the same way: `g_resmgr`, transitively visible for the first time via `ResourceManager.h`, is a real `ResourceManager` object (`extern ResourceManager g_resmgr`) — this file's own `extern void* g_resmgr` was simply wrong (matching a widespread but incorrect pattern used by ~20 other files tree-wide); removed the local wrong-typed redeclaration. Verification: a normalized mingw objdump diff of just `Train_network.cpp.obj` (32-bit, where the hardcoded constants were already correct) isolated exactly 22 real instruction-count-changing hunks out of 749 total — every one is the expected 2-instruction `add $0xNN,%reg; mov (%reg),%reg` folding into a single `mov 0xNN(%reg),%reg` at exactly the intended displacements (0x7c4 ×8, 0x7d0 ×2, 0xc ×4, 0x44 ×3, 0x7d4, 0x40/0x42, 0x4), plus one trailing alignment-`nop` hunk; all other hunks are pure address/label renumbering from those 22 sites shrinking by one instruction each — no unexpected offset ever appears. The native-build diff is intentionally large (every corrected site now compiles to the real 64-bit offset instead of the stale x86 one; spot-checked `m_gameMode`: `add $0x7c4,%rax` → `mov 0x838(%rax),%eax`). `ninja -C build`; `meson test -C build`: 30/30 including `--suite integration`. `call 0` count unchanged at 272 (baseline and after). Full-tree mingw typecheck error census (stashed-baseline diff): byte-for-byte identical 504 pre-existing errors before and after (unrelated `uint`/`ushort`/`offsetof`-on-non-standard-layout issues across `Building.h`/`GameVehicle.h`/`tilemap.h`/etc.) — zero new, zero fixed. Grepped every other `extern .* g_netman` declaration tree-wide: already a pre-existing, tolerated mix of `void*`/`Netman*` (harmless — a global's extern type annotation isn't part of the linker symbol) and one unrelated incomplete `NetMan*` (typo'd class name) in `ui/HelpWnd.cpp`, reported but not touched. Closed two gaps a first-pass review flagged: (1) the mingw diff's naive per-offset grep undercounted `0x518`/`0x51d` because the compiler sometimes splits a slot-array address into two immediates (e.g. `add $0x510` + a later `add $0x8`, summing to the same 0x518) — manually reconstructed every split site by hand and confirmed each nets to the intended address; the array-indexing itself (`&g_netman->m_slots[idx]`) is guaranteed correct by the language once `offsetof(Netman, m_slots)`==0x518 is `static_assert`-verified, which it is; (2) an exhaustive scan of all 727 "same instruction count" hunks (not just a random sample) found exactly the expected 6 register-swapped/reordered byte-store sites for the `m_mySlotIndex` byte-truncation sites (functionally identical — same value stored to the same address, just via a reordered load/compute-address sequence) and confirmed `movzwl 0x40/0x42(%eax); cwtl` correctly sign-extends the narrowed `pixel_width`/`pixel_height` reads, matching the intended `static_cast<int16_t>` semantics; zero unexplained differences remain.
