Fixed by moving the declaration out of `extern "C"` with the correct `UIPANEL_Surface*` signature. `game/BuildingPanel.cpp:96` has the identical mislabel (confirmed via the same objdump trace, same 0x4ba150 stub) — not fixed, different file, flagged for a follow-up session. **Second bug found and fixed, Ghidra-confirmed**: `track_building()`'s `children_list` traversal read `TrackPiece::sub_resource` (+0x28) as a full 8-byte pointer via `*(void**)(child+0x28)`, but `game/TrackPiece.h` declares that field `int32_t` (4 bytes) — on this 64-bit host the 8-byte read pulled in 4 bytes of the adjacent `flags`/`_pad_2E` fields as garbage upper address bits, corrupting multi-child traversal. `RESDATA_CreateChildSprite`'s disassembly (0x4546D0) confirms +0x28 really is a linked-list "next" pointer (`*(void**)(prev+0x28) = new_child`) — the field's declared type in `TrackPiece.h` is the actual bug, but fixing that header is out of scope here (it also affects `game/ScriptedObject.cpp`'s own +0x28 use, and `TrackPiece.cpp`'s destructor already has its own `uint32_t` truncate-then-widen workaround for the identical mismatch) — matched that existing workaround at this call site rather than widening the read, and flagged the real fix (retype `TrackPiece::sub_resource` to a pointer) as a follow-up. **Bulk cast-modernization** for the remaining ~200 sites, biased toward named-field access wherever a canonical header already had the field: `entity->resource`/`building->resource`/`overlay_resource`/`background_resource`/`button_strip_resource`/`sprite->pixelData` now go through `shared/types.h`'s canonical `RESDATA` struct (`object_type`/`resource_id`/`frame_width`/`frame_height`) instead of raw byte offsets; `child_panel`'s two remaining raw vtable[1] dispatches → `child_panel->hide()`; `overlay_panel`/`panel_graphics` raw reads in `deselect_building` now go through the `UIPANEL_SurfaceView`/`PanelGraphicsView` bridge helpers already introduced by the patch; added a `resdata_get_surface()` helper (mirroring the file's existing `panel_load_resource()` convention) for RESDATA's vtable[1] Lock/GetSurface dispatch (kept raw — RESDATA has no C++ vtable on the host side, same justification as `panel_load_resource`); `postcard_command_handler`'s raw vtable[2] dispatch on a `TrackPiece*` → `control->PtInRect(...)` (`GameObject::PtInRect`, confirmed at slot 2 by every class in this hierarchy's own vtable doc comments); `ButtonSprite`'s `x`/`y`/`sourceX`/`sourceY` (4 consecutive int32 fields, RECT-shaped) now viewed via `reinterpret_cast<RECT*>(&sprite->x)` instead of raw `sprite+4` arithmetic. Two sites left as raw offset reads with explanatory comments rather than forced through a too-small/host-mismatched type: `is_valid_placement`'s tile_type==4 check reads +0x62C, past `RESDATA`'s own asserted 0x1D8 size; and a `PtInRect` on `render_param_x/_y/render_extra/render_rect_ptr`, whose 4th field is a real 8-byte pointer on this host despite forming a valid 16-byte RECT on the original x86 layout (a pre-existing, host-only layout discrepancy needing its own Ghidra-verified fix, not a guess). Also rewrote `Train_HandleTrackBuild` (bottom of file, the orchestrator's other named target) to use `TrainSubsystem::sprite_list_1`/`request_count`, `Vehicle::next`/`tunnel_angle`/`field_76`/`field_7E`/`field_80`/`field_82`/`field_84`/`field_86`/`init_flag`, and `DPlayManager::m_sessionBlk1`/`m_wordValue` instead of raw offset math — all already-named fields in their canonical headers, just not previously used here; this also fixed a second scaled-pointer-arithmetic bug (`vehicle+0x14+index*4` assumed the original x86 4-byte `VehicleEditor*` stride for `Vehicle::editors[4]`, but each pointer is 8 bytes on this host, so any `index > 0` landed inside the wrong slot — fixed by indexing the real typed array) and converted a raw vtable[13] dispatch to `vehicle->editors[0]->SetName(...)` (`Entity::SetName`, confirmed at slot 13 independently by `core/VehicleEditor.h`'s own vtable doc). **Unrelated, pre-existing build blocker found and fixed first** (separate commit, so it can be dropped independently): `ui/CursorEditWindow.cpp::init` dereferenced a `int localStream[22]` through `const int**` to read its vtable pointer, which GCC 15.3's strict-aliasing analysis flags as a hard `-Werror=strict-aliasing` error — reproduced on a clean stashed `HEAD` rebuild with zero Town.cpp changes, so it predates this session and blocked the whole binary from linking in this environment; fixed with a `memcpy`-based read (aliasing-safe, byte-identical semantics). **Verification**: `build-strict2` `town/Town.cpp` error count 246 → 0 (full `ninja -k 0` rerun; confirmed none of the other STRICT=2-failing files in the tree reference any of Town's retyped fields — all pre-existing, unrelated to this pass). **Note on buildtype**: mid-session verification (`ninja -C build`/`meson test -C build`/`objdump` `call 0` counts) was run under an explicit `-Dbuildtype=debugoptimized` configure, chosen without checking the project default first — this understated `call 0` to 243 and produced 2 apparently-pre-existing test failures (`host-menu-renderer-linkage`, `test_singleplayer_accept_reaches_mode3_without_crashing`), both confirmed at the time to reproduce identically on a stashed clean-`HEAD` rebuild under the *same* (wrong) buildtype, so nothing was misattributed to this session's changes — but the numbers themselves didn't match this project's actual defaults. Caught before finishing: a final from-scratch `rm -rf build && meson setup build` (no buildtype override, i.e. the project default `debug`) reproduces the orchestrator-stated baseline exactly — `call 0` count **272, unchanged** both before (`git checkout <pre-session-commit> -- town/Town.cpp town/Town.h`, rebuilt, counted) and after this session's full changeset — and `meson test -C build` is a clean **30/30**, including `--suite integration`, with zero failures of any kind. The two "pre-existing" failures seen under `debugoptimized` do not reproduce under the default `debug` buildtype either before or after this session's changes; they were an optimization-level artifact of this session's own environment misstep, not a real, tracked pre-existing issue — noted here so a future session doesn't go looking for them. `ninja -C build-mingw -k 0` diffed before/after via `git checkout <pre-session-commit> -- <files>`/restore on the full `town/Town.cpp`/`Town.h`/`CursorEditWindow.cpp` changeset: byte-for-byte identical error message set for every touched file (13 pre-existing `Town.cpp` errors — `uint`/`assert`/`NET_GetFilePath` mingw-only gaps, unrelated) — zero new mingw-side breakage from any of this session's retyping or cast changes. **Found, documented, deliberately not fixed** (each is its own cluster, out of scope for a Town.cpp-focused pass): `game/BuildingPanel.cpp:96`'s identical `UIPANEL_CreateSurface` extern-"C" mislabel (above); `game/World.h`'s `World_GetObjectAt`/`World_RenderAll` both declared taking `Vehicle*` when the one call site in `Town::send_postcard` passes the `World` singleton (read from `Building+0x44C`) — cast locally with a comment rather than touching the shared declaration; `NET_ResolveAddress` has at least 4 conflicting declarations/addresses tree-wide (`Town.cpp`'s own 1-arg `void*(const char*)`, `network/Netman.h`'s 2-arg `void*(void*, const char*)`, `native/ddraw_init.c`'s C-linkage 1-arg version, `graphics/PixelDataCache.cpp`'s `void*(char*)`) — left Town.cpp's own declaration untouched, just added `static_cast<DPlayManager*>(...)` at each of its 6 call sites to type the result instead; `DPLAY_GetMessageCount` similarly has an incompatible `network/Netman.h` declaration (`int16_t(int32_t)` vs. this file's local `int(void*)` stub) — resolved by marking this file's own definition `static` rather than touching the cross-file conflict. Worktree: `.claude/worktrees/agent-a6adacb4df9151b91`, branch `worktree-agent-a6adacb4df9151b91`; commits `47c860a` (CursorEditWindow fix), `841f8f6` (five field resolutions + UIPANEL_CreateSurface bug), `bac7fa3` (zero-as-null-pointer-constant + missing-declarations), `3f7e85d` (Train_HandleTrackBuild rewrite), `f42d74b` (bulk cast conversion, 228→0) — ready to merge back to the main tree. |

**Session 2026-08-05: Build system migration — Make → Meson, one unified source tree**

- [x] **Layout flatten** — `src/decompiled_cpp/*` and `src/sdl3_shims/*` moved to the git root, one subsystem-directory tree (`core/`, `game/`, `graphics/`, `audio/`, `input/`, `network/`, `resources/`, `town/`, `ui/`, `world/`, `native/`, `shared/`, `stubs/`, `platform/`). Every host-only shim file now sits beside the decompiled classes of its subsystem, whole-body wrapped in `#ifndef _WIN32` (most already were; the outliers — `sdl3_ddraw`, `sdl3_dsound`, `sdl3_window`, the `resources/` trio, `sdl3_dplay.h`, `sdl3_directplay_train_bridge.cpp`, `platform/sdl3_types.h`, `platform/stub_func.c` — were fixed). `src/decompiled_cpp/.pi/` (unrelated tracked daemon session state, pre-existing duplicate of the root-level `.pi/`) was deliberately left untouched, out of scope.
- [x] **Dead code removed** — `src/network/`, `src/world/`, `src/port/` (confirmed unbuilt, unreferenced, superseded by the real decompiled+SDL3 versions), `src/sdl3_shims/link.sh` (hardcoded absolute path, never invoked), the abandoned partial `src/sdl3_shims/CMakeLists.txt`, and `platform/weak_stubs.c` (malformed syntax — `Type Foo(args)()` — never compiled, not referenced by any Makefile).
- [x] **`BROKEN_SRCS`/`NATIVE_BROKEN` retired** — all 33 entries triaged: 21 deleted outright (superseded), 8 deleted with a `PROGRESS.md` TODO (see "Native C source triage" above), `math_helpers.c` un-excluded after confirming it already compiles/links correctly (no stub needed — it was simply never wired into the build), `ui/PostcardAlbum.cpp` deleted as a confirmed duplicate, 3 stale entries named already-deleted files. The Meson source glob is now unconditional — no exclusion list of any kind survives.
- [x] **Meson project authored** — root `meson.build` + `meson_options.txt` (STRICT tier via `-Dstrict=0|1|2`) + `cross/mingw32-typecheck.txt`. Dependencies resolve via the Nix dev shell's already-propagated pkg-config path (confirmed: `NIX_CFLAGS_COMPILE`/`NIX_LDFLAGS` auto-inject every `buildInputs` package's include/lib paths — no more manual `/nix/store/*sdl3-*` globbing or pkg-config fallback shells). `native/*.c` and `platform/*.c` are compiled as C++ (`-x c++`) matching the old Makefile's use of `$(CXX)` for `.c` sources, preserving C++ name-mangling compatibility with their callers.
- [x] **Found and fixed a real nondeterminism bug during final verification** — `shared/stubs_impl.cpp` and `shared/link_stubs.cpp` both define `DDRAW_Init` (a pre-existing ODR violation masked by `-Wl,--allow-multiple-definition`, exactly the class of bug tracked as TODO LINK-001): one asserts (`"stub reached"`), the other returns success. Which one wins the link depends on which object file comes first on the link line, which depended on `find`'s directory-listing order for `shared/*.cpp` — not POSIX-guaranteed stable, and perturbed by this migration's `git mv` operations. `meson test -C build --suite integration` intermittently regressed from 10/12 to all-12-erroring (`DDRAW_Init` hitting the assert) across repeated full rebuilds, with the exact same source producing different binaries depending on incidental filesystem entry order. Fixed by piping every configure-time `find` call in `meson.build` through `sort`, making source (and therefore link) order deterministic and reproducible; confirmed stable across multiple clean rebuilds afterward. The underlying `DDRAW_Init` duplicate-definition itself is unfixed — still tracked as LINK-001 — this only makes the build's *outcome* deterministic rather than filesystem-order-dependent.
- [x] **Full test suite ported** — all ~30 Make test targets → `meson test`: plain unit tests, `nm`/`objdump` linkage checks, source-grep regressions, object-extraction isolation tests (via `extract_objects()` on the main `lego_loco` target — no redundant recompilation), and the network-namespace/private-dbus tests (their `unshare`/`dbus-daemon` setup extracted from Make recipe syntax into standalone `tests/env/with-dummy-netns.sh` / `tests/env/with-private-dbus.sh` wrappers). `29/29` real tests pass (matching the pre-migration baseline exactly; `menu-sprite-viewer` was already broken before this migration — `tools/menu_sprite_viewer.cpp` doesn't exist anywhere in the repo or git history — and is not ported).
- [x] **`test-integration` ported, two real bugs fixed** — `conftest.py`'s `build_game` fixture shelled out to `make build` directly; now the suite is a real `meson test(..., suite: 'integration')` entry so `LEGO_LOCO_BUILD_ROOT` is passed explicitly via `env:` and used for `meson compile -C <root>`, no hardcoded build-directory name. `test_game_gui.py` hardcoded the literal `"build/sdl3_net_transport_test"`, silently relying on the pytest process's CWD being the repo root (only true because the old Makefile happened to invoke pytest without `cd`ing) — now resolved via the same `LEGO_LOCO_BUILD_ROOT` env var. Side effect of the source-tree unification: `test-integration` improved from the previously-tracked 8 failed/4 passed to 2 failed/10 passed (see "Restore the current GUI integration gate" above) — network/discovery shim code that was previously compiled only into standalone test binaries is now linked into the real game binary for the first time.
- [x] **MinGW typecheck build wired** — `meson setup build-mingw --cross-file cross/mingw32-typecheck.txt` compiles only the real decompiled classes (`core/`, `game/`, `graphics/`, `audio/`, `input/`, `network/`, `resources/`, `town/`, `ui/`, `world/`, `shared/` — matching the old nested Makefile's actual scope, which never included `native/`, `stubs/`, or `sdl3_shims/` either) against real MinGW Windows headers; not linked, not a shippable target. Confirmed host-only guarded files (`sdl3_ddraw.cpp`, `sdl3_net_protocol.cpp`, `resource_archive.cpp`, etc.) compile down to empty translation units under `_WIN32` for free, exactly as designed. `ninja -C build-mingw -k 0` reports a real per-file census (58/104 succeed) — the 46 failures are genuine pre-existing decompiled-class Windows-header incompatibilities (`-fpermissive` void*-cast issues, matching the spirit of the Tier 2/3 census numbers above), not migration defects; fixing them is ongoing RE work, out of scope here.
- [x] **Old Makefiles removed** — root `Makefile`, `src/decompiled_cpp/Makefile`, `src/sdl3_shims/Makefile` all deleted. `src/` now holds only the untouched `.pi/` daemon state. `build-meson/` renamed to `build/` (Meson's build directory is a fresh `meson setup build`, not a rename-in-place — Meson build dirs embed absolute paths and don't tolerate being moved).
- [x] **Docs updated** — `AGENTS.md`'s "Build and tests", "Host deviations and SDL3", "Stubs", and "Code organization" sections rewritten for the flattened layout and Meson commands (including the retired-exclusion-list rule and the palette-handling note folded in from the now-deleted `src/sdl3_shims/README.md`); `.claude/skills/re-with-subagents/SKILL.md`'s ~14 hardcoded `src/decompiled_cpp/` paths updated; `flake.nix` `buildInputs`/shellHook updated (dropped unused `cmake` and `gnumake`, added `meson`/`ninja`); `.gitignore` updated for the new `build/`/`build-mingw/` directories.
- **Net result**: one source tree, no exclusion lists, `meson test -C build` reproduces the exact pre-migration pass/fail shape (29/29 unit, improved GUI integration), `ninja -C build-mingw -k 0` gives an honest per-file Windows-header-compatibility census. `AGENTS.md`'s Stubs policy (loud stub or real code, never silent build exclusion) is now actually enforced by the build, not just documented.

Caught by review and fixed: (1) `WIN32_QueueAsyncTask` had 4 different mangled signatures scattered across the tree; consolidated `core/HostMode3Bootstrap.cpp`/`core/CGWND.cpp`/`core/InitMode1.cpp` onto the assembly-faithful `(void*, void*, void*)` so `ui/EditWindow.cpp`'s (previously dead-code-eliminated, so silently unverified) call site has a real symbol to bind to; (2) `WaitForSingleObject`/`ResumeThread` host stubs need `extern "C"` linkage to match `WIN32Thread.cpp`'s declarations — moved from a plain-C++ (mangled, non-matching) definition in `shared/stubs_impl.cpp` to `shared/link_stubs.cpp`'s existing `extern "C"` block beside `CloseHandle`; the mismatch was invisible because this project's `-Wl,--unresolved-symbols=ignore-all` link flag doesn't fail on it. Confirmed via `nm` that every one of the 4 functions now has exactly one defined symbol and zero dangling undefined references; `EditWindow.cpp`'s dead allocation now sizes from `sizeof(WIN32Thread)` instead of a duplicated `0x41C` literal. `meson compile -C build` clean, `meson test -C build` 30/31 (same pre-existing 2-subtest GUI-integration failure as before, unchanged; the +1 test count is unrelated concurrent work in this tree).

**2026-08-05 (win32-stream-file)**: Reconstructed `WIN32_StreamFile_Ctor`/`DtorBody`/`WriteChar`/`Flush`/`SetBuffer` (0x463B70-0x463FC4) plus their required base-class callees (`WNDPROC_StreamBuf_Ctor`/`DtorBody`/`CheckFlush`/`AllocateDefaultBuffer`/`SetBuffer`, 0x465470-0x465763, and `WIN32_StreamFile_CloseHandle`, 0x463C30) as a real `WNDPROC_StreamBuf`/`WIN32_StreamFile` class hierarchy in new `resources/WndProcStreamBuf.{h,cpp}` and `resources/Win32StreamFile.{h,cpp}`, validated instruction-by-instruction against disassembly. Corrected 3 Ghidra auto-analysis mislabels found while tracing these (`WNDPROC_StreamBuf_HasPendingData`, `WNDPROC_StreamBuf_AllocateDefaultBuffer`, `CRT_memmove` — the last was masquerading as `CRT_strncpy` and is also called by `ui/UI_ScrollBar.cpp`, whose previously-silent no-op stub in `shared/link_stubs.cpp`/`shared/stubs_impl.cpp` now has a real `memmove`-backed body). **2026-08-05 (link-gap-fix)**: Root-caused and fixed the two multiplayer integration regressions flagged at the end of the previous entry. They were **not** caused by that entry's INPUT-cluster work, nor by concurrent `Netman.cpp`/`NetworkPlayerList.*` edits as guessed there — both guesses were wrong. Actual cause: an earlier batch-6 pass (WIN32/WNDPROC stream+thread+network cluster) removed the no-op stubs for `WIN32_SendNetworkData`, `WIN32_PeekMessageLoop`, `WIN32_GetSystemMetrics`, `WIN32_RecvNetworkData`, and `WIN32_PostQuit` from `shared/link_stubs.cpp`/`shared/defsym_stubs.cpp`, on the belief that real bodies already existed in `network/DirectPlay.cpp`/`core/CGWND.cpp`. That belief was false for all five: `DirectPlay.cpp` only *forward-declared* `WIN32_SendNetworkData`/`WIN32_GetSystemMetrics` (never defined them, and declared two of them as C++-mangled overloads distinct from the extern-"C" symbols `game/Train_network.cpp` actually calls); `core/CGWND.cpp` never defined `WIN32_PostQuit` at all. With `-Wl,--unresolved-symbols=ignore-all` (tracked as LINK-001) already in the link flags, removing the stubs turned a would-be link error into a silent runtime null-pointer call — confirmed via `journalctl`/`systemd-coredump`: `segfault at 0 ip 0000000000000000`, hit inside `TrainSubsystem::DownloadMissingAssets`'s `WIN32_SendNetworkData` call the first time a multiplayer host session had a missing asset to request. This is exactly the failure mode LINK-001 predicts once a masked duplicate/missing symbol's "winning" definition is removed.
Fix: restored all five as documented, non-crashing, warn-once stubs (`fprintf` once + safe default return; no `assert(0)`, since all five are proven reachable from ordinary multiplayer/UI flows — `WIN32_PostQuit` alone has 3 real callers in `ui/HelpWnd.cpp`/`game/BuildingPanel.cpp`/`input/Cursor_new_impls.cpp`) — matching the caller's actual linked signature in each case, not the previous (also-wrong) stub signatures. Also removed `DirectPlay.cpp`'s now-confirmed-dead, wrong-signature `WIN32_SendNetworkData` forward declaration. `meson test -C build`: 30/30. `meson test -C build --suite integration`: 11/12 (only the long-tracked `test_singleplayer_accept_reaches_mode3_without_crashing` remains failing). Real DirectPlay send/recv bodies are still unimplemented — tracked via the `// TODO: decompile 0x...` comments left on each stub — this is a masking fix, not the real implementation.

**2026-08-05 (train-connect-removealltracks)**: Fixed the two free functions declared/called in `game/Train_network.cpp` (`Train_ConnectToServer`, `Train_RemoveAllTracks`) that had no body anywhere in the tree — `nm` showed both undefined and their real call sites compiled to `call 0` (masked by `-Wl,--unresolved-symbols=ignore-all`, LINK-001's exact failure mode again). Both are unreachable today (`TrainSubsystem::ProcessMessages`'s and `TrainSubsystem::UpdateTrainMovement`'s caller chains are dead on the host build), but fully decompiled/implemented rather than stubbed, per this project's practice for evidenced-but-not-yet-wired functions.

Host-mode path re-hosts DirectPlay and connects to the ini-configured train server (retrying once); browser-mode path builds `"http:\\<payload>"` (literal double backslash, verified against raw `.data` bytes at 0x47EAF8 — not a typo) and validates it character-by-character before `ShellExecuteA` "open" (verified the literal at 0x47EAF0 is `"open"` by computing its PE file offset and reading the raw bytes directly, since Ghidra's string scanner hadn't flagged it). Both paths share a cleanup tail (peer teardown, error-notify message, placeholder `DPlayManager` player via the real `operator_new(sizeof(DPlayManager))` + placement-`new` + `CreatePlayer()` idiom already established at `network/Netman.cpp:2333-2336` — not the differently-addressed, still-undefined `DPLAY_CreatePlayer` free function this same file also declares — and an `sprite_list_1` flush matching `TrainSubsystem::RemoveAllCars`). Along the way, found and fixed a **3rd wrong-address/dropped-argument bug**, not one of the two originally reported: `DirectPlay_HandleMessages` (0x45F390) was declared `void(void)` and its own decompilation silently dropped 3 real stack arguments (protocol int, server-address string, flags) that `Train_ConnectToServer`'s real disassembly passes at two call sites — fixed the shared declaration in `network/DirectPlay.h`/`.cpp` to the real 3-arg signature (its own two internal call sites, inside `DirectPlay_ConnectToSession`, pass `(0, nullptr, 0)`, now explicit instead of implicit); the ~2076-byte body itself remains the same pre-existing `// TODO: decompile 0x45F390` stub, just no longer silently discarding arguments. `ShellExecuteA` had no host implementation anywhere in the tree and no established URL-open helper to reuse, so it's a new loud deferred stub in `shared/link_stubs.cpp` (`fprintf`+`assert`, `#ifndef _WIN32`) rather than inventing an `xdg-open`/subprocess-spawn path for a currently-dead caller.
- **`Train_RemoveAllTracks`**: real address is 0x43CC40-0x43CCB2, `__fastcall` (unchanged) — the old address comment (0x0043CA50) was mid-body of `Train_ConnectToServer` itself. Operates on `sprite_list_2` (Train.h's canonical "dead/orphaned train car list" field), not the "track_list" name in Ghidra's own auto-comment. Verified Ghidra's flagged bug by disassembly, not just trusting its comment: 0x43CC8D clears the node's `next` (+0x70) *before* 0x43CC96-0x43CC99 re-reads that same field to advance the list head, so only the first node is ever removed per call and the rest of the list leaks — preserved as-is, matching `TrainSubsystem::RemoveAllCars`'s established style of documenting a kept original bug.
- Verification: `objdump -d -C build/lego_loco` confirms both call sites now resolve to real addresses (`Train_ConnectToServer`→`0x42a32c`, `Train_RemoveAllTracks`→`0x42a99b`, both `nm`-visible as `T`), not `call 0`. Build was intermittently blocked mid-session by concurrent unrelated edits to `town/Town.cpp`/`network/NetworkPlayerList.cpp`/`network/Netman.cpp` from another session in the same tree (same recurring condition logged in the previous two entries) — verified this change's own 3 touched TUs compiled clean in isolation while that was in flight, then re-ran the full build once the tree quieted. `meson compile -C build` clean. `meson test -C build`: 29 Ok/1 Fail — the 1 failure is the integration-suite meson test node itself (it wraps all GUI pytest cases and fails if any one does); its own breakdown is `meson test -C build --suite integration`: 11/12, and the single failing case is the same long-tracked `test_singleplayer_accept_reaches_mode3_without_crashing`, unrelated to this change (both touched functions stay dead code). No new failures introduced.
