(2) `graphics/LOCOBITMAP.h`'s `struct UIPANEL_Surface` (vtable+mode+width+height+has_palette+flags+palette_ptr+pixels+ddraw_surf+**palette**, ~0x24 bytes on 32-bit) conflicts with `ui/UIPANEL_Surface.cpp`'s own documented-and-implemented real 0x20-byte layout for the same original struct (no trailing `palette` field) — the extra field in the header version appears unevidenced. `ui/AboutDialog.cpp`'s new `InitSprites` deliberately treats the screensaver surface as opaque `void*` (matching `ui/UIPANEL.cpp`'s own local `void*`-typed extern for the same address) rather than resolving this conflict. (3) `g_resmgr`/`g_audio_mgr` have further mutually-inconsistent addresses across files beyond what's already tracked above (e.g. `graphics/DDRAW.cpp`'s `g_resmgr@0x485580` and `ui/UIPANEL_Draw.cpp`'s `@0x4FD228` both disagree with the disassembly-confirmed `0x4855E8`/`0x4FD38C` used in this pass's new code).
- **Post-implementation review caught 3 more issues, all fixed**: (a) `RESDATA_FreeWindow`'s no-op stub in `shared/defsym_stubs.cpp` was left behind after `EditWindow.cpp` switched to `PopupWindow::DestroyMCIChild()` — a repo-wide grep confirmed it had zero remaining callers, so removed (an orphaned stub, not a live one, would itself have violated the stub policy). (b) `WIN32_PostQuit`'s new definition was verified against all 3 real call sites via `nm` on each `.o` (one matching `T _Z14WIN32_PostQuitv` against three identically-mangled `U` references) and via `objdump -d -C` confirming all 3 call sites resolve to the real function, not `call 0` — clean, no LINK-001-class mismatch this time. (c) `g_main_window` was dereferenced unguarded in the new `WIN32_PostQuit` (matching the original assembly and this file's own pre-existing unguarded uses elsewhere), but `shared/stubs_impl.cpp`/`tests/persistence_fixtures.h` default it to `nullptr` for host/test contexts that don't run the full subsystem-init chain — added a defensive null guard (documented as a host-only deviation, not a behavior change on the real init path). Also caught while re-verifying: `Cursor_Render`'s address inherited into the new `AboutDialog.cpp` code from `ui/HelpWnd.cpp`'s existing declaration (0x416420) is itself wrong — Ghidra resolves it to a mid-body address inside `Cursor_Init`; the real `Cursor_Render` is at 0x414C20. Fixed in the new code's declaration (with a note); **not** fixed in `ui/HelpWnd.cpp` itself, since that's a pre-existing bug outside this pass's scope — flagged here for a future pass. Also replaced 3 now-confirmed-wrong vtable-slot addresses in `ui/AboutDialog.h`'s top-of-file comment (Show/Init/method_7 — all three returned "No function at" from Ghidra) with honest "address unverified" notes instead of leaving asserted-false addresses in a header this pass edited substantially.
- Build/test: `meson compile -C build` clean. `meson test -C build`: 29 Ok/1 Fail (same pre-existing `test_singleplayer_accept_reaches_mode3_without_crashing`). `meson test -C build --suite integration`: 11/12, identical breakdown to the documented baseline — no new failures, no new `STUB:`/assert output in the passing suite (the new `RenderScreensaver` assert path is confirmed unreachable from any test, per the call-graph analysis above). Re-confirmed after the 3 post-review fixes above.

**2026-08-05 (uientity-dtor / childwindow-dtor-cluster leaf batch)**: Investigated a 4-item leftover batch (`UI_Window_Dtor`, `UI_Window_UpdateScroll`, `UI_ListData_Dtor` x3, `UI_ChildWindow_Init`). Real Ghidra addresses for all 7 found via `list_functions`; the task's own framing turned out wrong or incomplete for 3 of the 4 items — verified each independently rather than trusting the names.
- **`UI_Window_Dtor` (0x423500) — FIXED, a real bug.** Despite the Ghidra label, this is the destructor body of the `UIEntity` class already reconstructed in `ui/UIEntity.h`/`.cpp` (confirmed by a live slot-by-slot dump of vtable 0x477A90: 15 Entity-shaped slots, field offsets matching UIEntity's own `pTooltip`/+0x98 etc.). `UIEntity`'s real `~UIEntity()` was **never defined** in `ui/UIEntity.cpp` (only its constructor was) — instead, `shared/vtable_stubs.cpp` had an unrelated, minimal `class UIEntity { virtual ~UIEntity(); };` shadow definition supplying an assert-stub body. Because C++ mangling doesn't encode field layout, both same-named classes in different TUs produced the identical `UIEntity::~UIEntity()` symbol — an ODR violation where the wrong (stub) definition silently won at link time for every real `UIEntity` object, had one ever been destroyed. Fixed: implemented the real destructor in `ui/UIEntity.cpp` (destroys the tooltip child via `UI_DestroyTooltip(&g_tooltip_mgr, ...)` if `pTooltip != nullptr`; base-class cleanup now runs via normal C++ destructor chaining instead of the binary's explicit `GameObject_DtorBody` call), and removed the duplicate shadow class from `shared/vtable_stubs.cpp` per that file's own stated CLASS-001 removal policy. Also fixed two stale claims in `ui/UIEntity.h`: the destructor's address comment (was "0x4234A8/0x4234C0", neither of which Ghidra has any record of; real: body 0x423500, scalar-deleting wrapper 0x4234E0 = `UI_DtorWrapper`), and "likely no overridden virtual methods (defaults to GameObject)" — the vtable dump proves UIEntity actually overrides 3 of Entity's 15 slots (`StopSound`→`UI_ShowWindow` 0x423840, `SetVisible`→`UI_EnableWindow` 0x423890, `Update`→`UI_HideWindow` 0x423870), none of which are reconstructed yet. Confirmed **the fix itself is currently unreachable** (no live path constructs a real `UIEntity`: `UI_DrawListBox`, the only non-constructor call site, has zero callers; the constructor's own reachable path runs through the orphaned `UI_Manager`/`g_tooltip_mgr` facade documented in the corrected `ui_manager.c removed` entry above) — fixed anyway since it's a cheap, evidence-backed anti-pattern fix (duplicate class / ODR violation), not a speculative implementation.
  - **Symbol-collision check done before wiring the call** (this session already hit this exact failure mode with `WIN32_SendNetworkData`): `UI_DestroyTooltip` has 3 conflicting declarations across the tree (`(void*,int)` in `shared/stubs_impl.cpp`/`game/Panel.h`; `(void*,void*)` in `ui/TrainStationWindow.cpp`). `nm -C build/lego_loco` confirmed the actually-linked definition is `UI_DestroyTooltip(void*, int)` — matched that exact signature in the new `ui/UIEntity.cpp` declaration rather than guessing.
- **`UI_Window_UpdateScroll` (0x423560) — stub fixed, transcription deferred.** Real 690-byte `UIEntity` per-frame scroll/animation-tick method (switch on `direction`, uses named `UIEntity`/`Entity`/`GameObject` fields throughout — worldX/worldY/pTooltip/tooltipOffsetX/Y, resource/audio_channel/frame_index/timer — a clean field-offset match). Its only real caller is 0x423D70 (`UI_HideTooltip`, already correctly reconstructed as `UI_Manager::hideTooltip()` in `ui/UI_Utils.cpp`), which itself has zero C++ callers today (same orphaned-`UI_Manager` situation). Confirmed genuinely dead code, so — per this session's own established precedent (`ui_childwindow.c` entry above: "not the same as porting the cluster") — did **not** transcribe the 690-byte body; a full port would also need two more not-yet-reconstructed `UIEntity` virtual overrides (`UI_ShowWindow`/`UI_HideWindow`, discovered above) it dispatches through. What *was* a real, in-scope bug: `shared/stubs_impl.cpp`'s stub was `void UI_Window_UpdateScroll(int*) {}` while every call site (`ui/UI_Utils.cpp`, twice) declares/uses `extern char __fastcall UI_Window_UpdateScroll(int*)` and branches on `== 1` — a declared-return-type mismatch that reads garbage out of AL/EAX, same bug class as this session's `WIN32_SendNetworkData`/`NET_UpdatePlayerList`/`DDRAW_SelectBuilding` fixes. Fixed the stub to the correct `char`-returning, loud (`fprintf`+`assert`) form with the real address documented, so if this ever becomes reachable it fails loudly instead of silently misbehaving.
- **`UI_ListData_Dtor` x3 (0x424A30/0x424A70/0x424A90) — already covered, no new code.** These are the scalar-deleting-destructor thunks for `TimerSlotList`'s three vtable variants — confirmed by address: `shared/TimerSlotList.h` already documents "VTBL_TIMERLIST_A (0x477BD0)... VTBL_TIMERLIST_C (0x477B40)... VTBL_TIMERLIST_B (0x477B78)" by these *exact* addresses (the `_477bd0`/`_477b40`/`_477b78` Ghidra name suffixes are the vtable each thunk installs). They are not all identical, so "covered" isn't one blanket statement: **A (0x424A30) and C (0x424A90)** reset count, free the items array, and null it — byte-for-byte the same cleanup `TimerSlotList::DtorBody()` (0x412410, already implemented in `shared/TimerSlotList.cpp`) performs. **B (0x424A70)** does something different — it delegates to `UI_InitScrollBar` (reset/reinit, not free) rather than freeing — and that function is also already implemented (`ui/UI_ScrollBar.cpp:132`). Per CLAUDE.md's "keep only user cleanup, let the compiler manage vtable-swap/scalar-dtor boilerplate" rule, none of the three need a new free-function reimplementation: a single C++ `~TimerSlotList()` already covers what A/C do, and B's user cleanup already exists under its own real name.
- **`UI_ChildWindow_Init` (0x424B40) — already covered, no new code; task description partially wrong.** It is a real, distinct function from `UI_ChildWindow_Create` (correct so far) — but Ghidra's "Init" label is a misnomer: disassembly (36 instructions, verified — no `CALL` to any other function except two `GLOBAL_free` calls and two virtual-slot-0 dispatches) shows it is the scalar-deleting-destructor thunk for the ChildWindow vtable (0x477C18), inlining the exact same three cleanup steps in the exact same order as the already-implemented `UI_ChildWindow_Dtor` (0x424BA0, `ui/UI_ChildWindow.cpp:172-206`): release render-surface sub-object at +0x10, free heap buffer at +0x20, release sub-object at +0x24. No call from 0x424B40 to 0x424BA0 exists in the binary (the logic is duplicated inline, a normal MSVC pattern for a vtable-slot thunk vs. a separately-invoked destructor body) — so in real C++ this is the same one destructor, already written; no new implementation needed.
- Also corrected the stale `ui_manager.c removed` entry above ("no typed reconstruction exists" was wrong — see that entry) while investigating item 2's reachability, since it directly bears on why `UI_Window_UpdateScroll`'s only caller is dead.
- Verification: `nm -C build/lego_loco` before/after diffed clean (`UIEntity::~UIEntity()`/`UI_Window_UpdateScroll(int*)` addresses moved as expected from the relink; no new `U` unresolved entries). `meson compile -C build` clean. `meson test -C build`: 29 Ok/1 Fail. `meson test -C build --suite integration`: 11/12, same pre-existing `test_singleplayer_accept_reaches_mode3_without_crashing` failure — confirmed via the full pytest breakdown, not just the wrapper's pass/fail count. No `STUB:`/`UIEntity`/`UI_Window` lines appear in the test log (consistent with both fixed items being confirmed-dead code).

**2026-08-06 (mode3-clean-switch)**: Fixed the long-tracked `test_singleplayer_accept_reaches_mode3_without_crashing` — the GUI integration gate is now **12/12**. Root cause was a chain of four distinct bugs on the mode-1→mode-3 path (`CGWND_EnterMode3(1)` → `HelpWnd::play_narration`), each masking the next; found by driving the real crash under `coredumpctl`/`gdb` after each fix (`objdump -d --disassemble=<fn>` to locate the exact faulting `call`, then Ghidra ground truth via `get_xrefs_to` to confirm the real callee) rather than guessing:
  1. **`ui/HelpWnd.cpp` / `ui/EditWindow.cpp`: `Config_ReadInt`/`Config_GetIniString` missing `extern "C"`.** Both real x86 call sites (confirmed via Ghidra `get_xrefs_to(0x452DF0)`: `HelpWnd_PlayNarration` and `EditWindow_OnPlayerNameChanged`) call the single `extern "C"` `Config_ReadInt`/`Config_GetIniString` in `game/ConfigIni.cpp`. Both files' local `extern` declarations lacked `extern "C"` and (for `Config_ReadInt`) had a wrong 4th-parameter type, so each mangled to a symbol nothing defined; under this project's `--unresolved-symbols=ignore-all` link flag that resolved to address 0 instead of failing the link — a direct `call 0` that crashed `play_narration` outright and silently no-op'd `EditWindow`'s name-commit path. Fixed both declarations to `extern "C"` with the correct signature; also corrected `EditWindow.cpp`'s stale `Config_ReadInt` address comment (`0x448D50`, which names no function) to the Ghidra-confirmed `0x452DF0`. Removed the now-fully-dead C++-mangled `Config_ReadInt` no-op stub from `shared/defsym_stubs.cpp` (both real callers now bind to `game/ConfigIni.cpp`'s implementation; confirmed via `nm` that only one `Config_ReadInt` symbol remains).
  2. **`ui/HelpWnd.cpp`: `HelpWnd::init()` never ran on the host.** The constructor's `#ifndef _WIN32` skip claimed init() does "heavy Win32 file I/O and sprite loading", but init()'s actual body (read from source) only allocates 9 `ButtonSprite` objects (whose own ctor is a portable 4-field zero, no Win32 dependency) and zeroes index/state fields including `spritesInited`. Skipping it left the host's `g_audio_mgr` permanently uninitialized heap garbage; `play_narration`'s `load_page` then read a garbage-nonzero `spritesInited` and dereferenced garbage `this->btnAnim`. Verified via a temporary debug print (removed) that `create()` — which would run init() a second time and could double-leak sprites — is never called for `g_audio_mgr` on the host branch of `CGWND::InitAllSubsystems` (`core/CGWND.cpp`), so a single unconditional `this->init()` call is safe. Un-guarded it.
  3. **`ui/HelpWnd.cpp`: `HelpWnd::init()`'s "zero pages array and workBuffer" loop used raw `this + 0x15C` dword arithmetic.** That literal offset is the *original 32-bit* layout; on this 64-bit build (8-byte pointer members shift everything after them) it doesn't correspond to `pages`/`workBuffer` at all — it silently overwrote `btnAnim`/`btnContent`/`btnScrollBar`/`btnTextArea` moments after they were allocated. A CLAUDE.md-named anti-pattern (raw offset arithmetic standing in for named-field access), and an active memory-corruption bug once fix #2 made this code path reachable. Replaced with two `memset`s on the actual named fields.
  4. **`ui/HelpWnd.cpp`: 9× `operator_new(0x24)` for `ButtonSprite` used the original 32-bit `sizeof(ButtonSprite)` literal.** On 64-bit, `ButtonSprite` (vtable ptr + 4 `int32_t` + 2 pointers + `UINT` + `int32_t`) is larger than 36 bytes, so placement-constructing into a 36-byte block overflowed into the next heap chunk's header — inert as long as fix #2 kept `init()` from running, but once un-guarded this corrupted the heap on *every* test (not just the mode-3 one), surfacing as sporadic `free(): invalid size` and SDL3-teardown `SIGABRT`s across otherwise-unrelated tests in the same `meson test` run. Fixed all 9 sites to `operator_new(sizeof(ButtonSprite))`.
  - **Scope decision, made explicit rather than fixed silently**: a global `objdump -d build/lego_loco | grep -E "call\s+0 "` scan (prompted by fix #1's pattern recurring) found **~400 more direct `call 0` sites across the binary**, the same landmine class as commit `ede2203`'s sweep — evidently that sweep did not cover every file. `HelpWnd.cpp`'s own extern block alone has several more of the same shape (`Sprite_SetState(ButtonSprite*,...)` vs. the linked `Sprite_SetState(void*,...)`; `UIPANEL_Blit`'s declared params matching none of its 5 real overloads; plain-C++-linkage `WIN32_StreamOpenPath`/`WNDPROC_*` declarations after the file's `extern "C"` block closes at line 74, likely needing to be inside it). None of these are on `play_narration`'s call path after the `#ifdef _WIN32` guard added below, so they were deliberately **not** touched this pass — sweeping ~400 call sites is its own session with its own verification, not a side effect of a test-fix pass. Recommend a build-time or CI check (`objdump -d $BINARY | grep -qE "call\s+0 " && exit 1`) as the durable fix, tracked as a new open item below.
  - **Actual fix for the test, once the above stopped the crash**: per-advisor review, `HelpWnd`'s presentation layer is explicitly unported on the host (`create()` never called, 6 render methods stubbed in `ui/HelpWnd_stubs.cpp`, and `play_narration`'s chain reaches `Cursor_WaitForBlit` (0x414BB0), which polls a DirectDraw surface vtable slot the host has no object for) — `CGWND_EnterMode3(1)`'s unconditional `HelpWnd::play_narration(5, 0)` call in `core/CGWND.cpp` was the one place that "unported subsystem" convention (used everywhere else in the same function, e.g. the Town/Cursor/Postcard overlay setup two lines above) hadn't been applied. Guarded it: real call under `#ifdef _WIN32`, loud `stderr` skip under `#ifndef _WIN32`, matching the project's established host-deviation pattern instead of continuing to chase the ButtonSprite-rendering landmine field behind it.
  - Verification: `meson test -C build` 30/30 (was 29/30). Full `meson test -C build --suite integration` 12/12, confirmed stable across two consecutive full runs. The mid-session SDL3-teardown `SIGABRT`s (seen before fix #4, while `HelpWnd::init()`'s allocations were still undersized) do **not** reproduce on the fixed tree: two full 12-test runs with `MALLOC_CHECK_=3` (glibc aborts at the corrupting write instead of arbitrarily later, so a residual bug would leave a usable core) produced zero coredumps. Fix #4 was the actual cause, not a coincidence — an earlier same-day comparison against a `git stash`'d pre-session tree used a different test subset (`-k "not mode3"`, 11 tests) and a different glibc signal (SIGSEGV, not `malloc_printerr`'s SIGABRT), so it didn't actually establish what it was first written up as establishing; corrected here rather than left as an under-evidenced claim in this durable record.
- [ ] **~400-site `call 0` landmine sweep** — `objdump -d build/lego_loco | grep -E "call\s+0 "` surfaces roughly 400 direct calls to address 0 across the binary (mismatched-signature `extern` declarations resolving to no symbol, masked by `-Wl,--unresolved-symbols=ignore-all`), the same bug class as commit `ede2203` and this session's fix #1 above. Found while fixing `test_singleplayer_accept_reaches_mode3_without_crashing`; deliberately not swept this pass (out of scope — needs its own session). `ui/HelpWnd.cpp`'s own extern block has several more instances not yet fixed (`Sprite_Init`, `Sprite_SetState`, `UIPANEL_Blit`, `WIN32_StreamOpenPath`/`WNDPROC_*`) but they're not on any currently-tested path. Suggested durable fix: a CI/build-time check that fails on any `call 0` in the final binary. **Update 2026-08-06 (render-path-landmine-sweep, below): the entire cluster inside `TileMap::ProcessRect`/`InvalidateDirtyRects` — 23 of these ~400 sites — is now fixed; ~377 remain elsewhere.** **Update 2026-08-06 (uipanel-family-landmine-fix, below): the UIPANEL family cluster (20 sites) is now fixed; 347 remain, tracked in `docs/landmine-sweep-worklist.md`.**

**2026-08-06 (render-path-landmine-sweep)**: User reported the game "freezes" after clicking accept on the main menu. Investigation showed it wasn't a regression or a real freeze — `mode_changed` events fire correctly to mode 3 and the process stays alive and responsive (confirmed via a `GameSession`-based repro script polling `/proc/<pid>/stat` CPU jiffies and sending a live Escape keypress) — the screen was simply never updated, because `TileMap::ProcessRect`, the per-tile draw function, and `TileMap::InvalidateDirtyRects`, its caller, were both riddled with the same `call 0` landmine class as the mode3-clean-switch fix above, just concentrated 23-deep instead of 1-deep. `objdump -d --disassemble=<mangled_fn>` on each confirmed the exact count before and after every fix (17 in ProcessRect, 6 in InvalidateDirtyRects, now 0/0).

Root cause, in two parts:

1. **12 free-function wrappers in `world/tilemap.h`/`tilemap.cpp` (`Town_RenderSelection/DeselectBuilding/UpdateSelection`, `Game_SetCursorByResourceId/ResetCursor`, `UI_SetTooltipText/SetTooltipPos/UpdateTooltip`, `BuildingMgr_DispatchAll`, `World_InvalidateRect`, `RESDATA_ScriptedObject_Dispatch`, `DDRAW_DispatchToSubObjects`) never had real bodies anywhere in the tree** — confirmed via `nm -C build/lego_loco` returning nothing for each, not just a signature mismatch with an existing stub. Every one of the 12 turned out to already be implemented as a real, previously-validated C++ class method (`Town::render_selection`, `BuildingMgr::DispatchAll`, etc.) at the documented original address; `tilemap.cpp` had simply never been updated to call through the object once those classes were integrated. Verified every real parameter count against the callee's own **RET immediate** via raw disassembly (not the Ghidra decompiler's high-level param inference, which was independently found wrong twice in this exact cluster — see below — and not caller-side guessing, which was also wrong once). Findings that corrected pre-existing, previously-unverified header documentation:
   - `Town::render_selection` (0x42D400): Ghidra's decompiler claims 0 params; disassembly's `RET 0x14` proves 5 (`x1,y1,x2,y2,extra`) — the decompiler's own param-count inference is wrong here, not just under-documented. `town/Town.h` already had the correct 5-arg signature from a prior session; only the call site (passing 0 args) and the free-function wrapper needed fixing.
   - `Town::deselect_building`/`update_selection` (0x42D280/0x42D3A0): both take 4 real stack args per `RET 0x10`, but both bodies read only `this`-relative fields and never touch them — same "unused trailing arg" pattern already documented on `BuildingMgr::DispatchAll`. `town/Town.h` previously claimed 0 args; fixed to 4 (unused, synthesized as 0 by the wrapper). Verification depth differs between the two: `deselect_building`'s caller was traced directly and found to push the outer `left/top/right/bottom` rect values, which the body then discards; `update_selection` was classified the same way by analogy to the adjacent call site rather than an independent caller trace. Doesn't change the fix (the body ignoring its args means passing 0s from the wrapper is correct either way), but the two aren't equally verified.
   - `UI_Manager::setTooltipText/setTooltipPos/updateTooltip` (0x423E00/0x423E80/0x423F00): all three take a real 5th stack arg per `RET 0x14` on each; `ui/UI_Utils.h` claimed 4. Every caller passes a literal 1 for it and no body reads it — same unused-trailing-arg pattern again. Notably, Ghidra's decompiler ALSO said 4 params for these three (matching the wrong header) — the raw `RET` immediate was the only reliable signal.
   - `RESDATA_ScriptedObject::Dispatch` (0x449C00): `world/scriptengine.h` explicitly declared 0 params with a comment acknowledging the real callee takes more ("this translation unit's legacy declaration has no parameters, so preserve its established zero tuple") — a documented but non-equivalent simplification. Fixed to the real 5 args (`x1,y1,x2,y2,flag`, per `RET 0x14`) and threaded them into the real clip rect instead of a hardcoded empty one.
   - `World::InvalidateRect`, `BuildingMgr::DispatchAll`, `DDRAW_Building::DispatchToSubObjects`: already correctly documented and already correctly called; only the free-function wrapper was missing.
   - Each wrapper is implemented beside the class it wraps (`Town.cpp`, `Game.cpp`, `UI_Utils.cpp`, `BuildingMgr.cpp`, `World.cpp`, `scriptengine.cpp`, `DDRAW.cpp`), not centralized in `tilemap.cpp`, because those subsystems' own headers declare the same shared `g_*` singleton globals with different (also pre-existing, unrelated) types than `tilemap.cpp`'s local declarations — including them all into one TU produced real conflicting-declaration errors. A first attempt centralizing everything in `tilemap.cpp` hit exactly this and was reverted in favor of the per-subsystem split.
2. **`TileMap::InvalidateDirtyRects` had its own, separate cluster**: `World_Lock`/`World_Unlock` (free-function wrappers, same missing-body pattern as above — real methods are `World::Lock()`/`Unlock()`), `IntersectRect`/`UnionRect` (declared with non-`const`, non-`extern "C"` signatures in `tilemap.h`/`tilemap.cpp`, while the one real implementation in `graphics/sdl3_window.cpp` is `extern "C"` with `const RECT*` — a linkage-specification mismatch, not just a type mismatch; a test fixture in `tests/persistence_fixtures.h` had the same bug and needed the same fix), and `DDRAW_PresentRect` (three real bodies already existed in the tree — the original DirectDraw COM path in `graphics/LOCOBITMAP.cpp`, a no-op stub, and the working `#ifndef _WIN32` SDL3 path in `graphics/sdl3_ddraw.cpp` — but every caller's declared signature matched none of them; fixed with the same `#ifdef _WIN32` / `#else` dual-declaration pattern used for `Config_ReadInt` etc. in the mode3-clean-switch fix, applied at the canonical declaration point in `graphics/LOCOBITMAP.h` plus each caller file: `ui/UIPANEL.cpp`, `network/DirectPlay.cpp`, `world/tilemap.h`).
3. **9 more genuinely-undefined globals**, found only after the above fixes made `ProcessRect`/`InvalidateDirtyRects` executable for the first time and they promptly segfaulted reading them: `g_lock_update_flag`, `g_has_selection`, `g_placement_valid`, `g_placement_blocked`, `g_town_overlay_rect/left/top/right`, `g_town_selection_rect_left/top/right/bottom`, `g_cursor_surface` — all declared `extern` in `world/tilemap.h` but never defined anywhere in the tree (confirmed via `nm build/lego_loco | grep -w <name>` returning no `B`/`D` entry), silently defaulting to address 0 under `--unresolved-symbols=ignore-all` applied to data symbols, not just functions — the same landmine class, just on globals instead of calls. Found by cross-checking every `g_*` token referenced in `world/tilemap.cpp` and every global declared in `world/tilemap.h` against `nm` after each round of fixes, rather than waiting for the next crash to point at the next one. All nine now defined (zero-initialized) in `shared/stubs_impl.cpp`.
4. **`TileMap::occupancy_bitmap`** (misidentified as an independent global `g_tile_occupied_bitmap` at a fixed address) was actually a `this`-relative member field — the absolute address in the old comment was just `g_tilemap`'s static address plus the field's offset, an artifact of the original binary's single fixed-address static object. Fixed as part of this pass (via a dispatched `reverse-engineer` subagent): added a real named member to `TileMap`, updated `Init()` to allocate into it, updated every read site.

**Verification**: `objdump -d --disassemble=<mangled_fn> build/lego_loco | grep -c "call   0 <"` is 0 for both `TileMap::ProcessRect` and `TileMap::InvalidateDirtyRects` (was 17 and 6). `meson test -C build`: 30/30. `meson test -C build --suite integration`: 12/12, including the crash re-surfacing once the dead code became live (the 9 undefined globals above) and then re-verified clean, twice, including once under `MALLOC_CHECK_=3`. Manual repro via the isolated Wayland sandbox (`GameSession`, `/proc/<pid>/stat` CPU-jiffy polling, live Escape keypress) confirms the process stays alive and responsive in mode 3 for 8+ seconds — the original "freeze" report is fully resolved as a liveness issue. The MinGW typecheck build (`ninja -C build-mingw -k 0`) was also diffed before/after this session's changes: the unique error set is byte-for-byte identical (135 pre-existing errors, unrelated `uint`-typedef and `loco::assets` namespace issues predating this session) — confirming the four new `#ifdef _WIN32` branches added this session (`world/tilemap.h`'s `DDRAW_PresentRect`, `graphics/LOCOBITMAP.h`, `network/DirectPlay.cpp`, `core/GameLoop.cpp`'s two guarded call sites) introduce no new MinGW-side breakage, even though they're never exercised by the native host build that all other verification above ran under.

**Also found, not fixed — a second latent ODR collision of the same class**: `g_primary_surface` is declared as two incompatible types under one C++ symbol — `int*` at `graphics/DDRAW.h` (address 0x4FF0D8 per that header's comment) vs. `void*` in `world/tilemap.cpp` (address 0x4FD3C4). Neither header is included into the other's translation unit today, so this doesn't currently produce a link/runtime landmine, and it's why the 12 wrapper implementations were split per-subsystem rather than centralized (see point 1 above) — but it's the same silent-data-symbol-mismatch class as the 9 undefined globals fixed this session, just not yet triggered. Whoever next needs both headers in one TU will hit it; worth resolving to one canonical typed declaration before that happens rather than rediscovering it as a crash.

**What this does NOT fix — still an honest gap, not a new bug**: the screen still visually shows the main menu after reaching mode 3 (confirmed via screenshot). This is expected, not a regression: per the persistence-adapter log line printed every run, `placement coverage: 0 placed / 497 carried — host resources lack original RESDATA metadata`. With zero tile objects ever actually placed onto the `TileMap`, its occupancy bitmap stays all-zero, so `ProcessRect`'s now-correct per-tile draw branch has nothing to draw, and `DDRAW_PresentRect`'s SDL3 body re-presents the otherwise-unchanged primary surface. This is Phase B from the "Town rendering" item above: finding the original code path that populates tile-placement metadata is a genuinely open, not-yet-solved reverse-engineering question (see the retracted `ResourceManager_GetById`/`AddString` investigation thread — traced through `RESDATA_ScriptedObject_AddChild`/`BuildingDescriptorEditor__Ctor`, the latter turning out to be Ghidra-mislabeled as an edit-control constructor, a dead end). Phase A (this entry) — making the render path itself safe, correct, and landmine-free — is complete and independent of Phase B; Phase B remains open and unscoped.

**2026-08-06 (mode3-input-wiring-gap)**: User-reported "game freezes after clicking Accept" investigated live under GDB (real interactive Sway session, real hardware GLX/iris rendering — not the headless llvmpipe path the integration suite and the render-path-landmine-sweep entry above both ran under). Full writeup: `BUG-mode-3-render-freeze.md`.

First hypothesis (GPU/driver-level hang in `glXSwapBuffers`/`iris_fence_finish`, since three Ctrl-C samples over 15s landed at the identical PC) was wrong and ruled out with a hit-counting breakpoint rather than more stack samples: `break sdl3_ddraw.cpp:568` + `ignore 1 100000` recorded 9310 hits to `SDL_RenderPresent` in ~5s — the render loop is alive and presenting at high frequency, matching (and independently reconfirming, on real GPU hardware this time) this session's own render-path-landmine-sweep finding above that the process stays alive and responsive in mode 3. Window-occlusion (tabbed Sway container hiding the game window) and a kernel-level GPU hang (`dmesg`/`journalctl -k`) were also checked and ruled out.

New finding, additional to and independent of the already-documented "0 placed / 497 carried, nothing for `ProcessRect` to draw" gap immediately above: **mode 3 has no input wiring at all on the host.** `core/CGWND_sdl3.cpp`'s `active_host_menu()` gates every SDL mouse/keyboard event on `g_game_mode == 2`; once mode 3 is entered, `PumpMessages_SDL3` drops all input events on the floor. Confirmed at the data level — breakpoints on `Game::UpdateInputState()`/`Game::HandleCursorHover()` recorded **zero hits** over 5s of active mouse movement, because `Game::Update()`'s `has_event` gate (`core/Game.cpp:427`) reads `this->mouse_move_flag == 0`/`left_click_flag == 0` on every call, and `grep -n "mouse_move_flag\s*=" core/Game.cpp` shows the field is never assigned `1` anywhere in the current tree. So even setting aside the tile-placement gap, mode 3 would show zero cursor/hover/UI feedback to any input today. Not fixed this session — see `BUG-mode-3-render-freeze.md` for the suggested fix (a mode-3 counterpart to `active_host_menu()`/`EditWindow::hostHandlePointer`, needs assembly verification of the original `WM_MOUSEMOVE`/`WM_LBUTTONDOWN` dispatch in modes 3/9 before implementing) and a regression-test gap this exposed (the 12/12 integration gate has no test that asserts any input-driven side effect in mode 3).

- [ ] **Original-32-bit hardcoded `operator_new` sizes sweep** — a distinct, likely-larger-blast-radius bug class found alongside the `call 0` sweep: `grep -rnE 'operator_new\(0x[0-9A-Fa-f]+\)' --include='*.cpp' --include='*.h' .` surfaces ~90 call sites passing a literal hex byte count instead of `sizeof(Type)`. This session confirmed and fixed one instance (`ui/HelpWnd.cpp`'s 9× `ButtonSprite` allocations, fix #4 above) that was a real 64-bit heap-overflow-on-placement-new — worse than a `call 0` in one respect: it faults with a clean stack at the call site, while an undersized allocation corrupts a *later*, unrelated heap chunk and can surface anywhere (this one took down unrelated tests' SDL3 teardown until traced back). Most of the ~90 sites are likely inert today (behind `#ifdef _WIN32`/`#ifndef _WIN32`-skipped host paths, or already-correct because the allocated object is a flat POD/byte buffer rather than a polymorphic C++ class whose 64-bit layout differs from the original 32-bit one) — same reachability caveat as the `call 0` list — but each needs the same per-site check (is the target type polymorphic/pointer-bearing? does 32-bit size actually differ from `sizeof` on this build?) before deciding whether to fix. Not swept this pass; flagging so the next un-guarding of a currently-dormant subsystem doesn't rediscover this the same way (a live crash, 4 fix-rebuild-run cycles deep).

**2026-08-06 (call-0-landmine-symbol-sweep)**: Continued the tracked `call 0` sweep with a proper root-cause methodology instead of grep-and-guess: for every `call 0` site in the final `build/lego_loco`, aligned that function's call sequence against the same function's call *relocations* in its source `.o` (which still name the intended symbol before the linker discards it on resolution failure) to recover exactly what each landmine was trying to call, then deduplicated 475 call-0 sites down to 140 distinct root-cause symbols. Full worklist, methodology, and per-symbol candidate real definitions are tracked in `docs/landmine-sweep-worklist.md` (not duplicated here — durable, resumable from that file).

- **Fixed `PtInRect`** (38 sites, the single largest symbol): `graphics/sdl3_window.cpp`'s real implementation had a parameter-type mismatch against its own header (`const void* pt` declared vs. `POINT pt` defined), splitting it onto C++-mangled linkage while at least 4 distinct incompatible `extern "C"` shapes were declared across `ui/HelpWnd.cpp`, `town/Town.cpp`, `graphics/LOCOBITMAP.cpp`, `ui/EditWindow.cpp`, `input/Cursor_new_impls.cpp`, `network/DPlayManager.cpp`, and `native/NETMAN_NetworkUI.c` — the same "declared extern C but defined without it, or vice versa" bug class as `Config_ReadInt`/`WIN32_SendNetworkData` before it. Canonicalized to `BOOL PtInRect(const RECT*, POINT)`; fixed every mismatched caller (constructing a `POINT` at call sites that previously passed separate `x, y` ints, or dereferencing a `POINT*` where callers previously passed one by pointer).
- **Fixed a 5-symbol Town.cpp/sdl3_town_mode3.cpp cluster** (`DDRAW_SelectBuilding`, `GameObject_Draw`, `GameObject_GetRelPos`, `RESDATA_HitTestChildren`, `DPLAY_GetMessageCount`): the first four were C-linkage declarations resolving to C++-mangled real definitions elsewhere (same bug class); moved them out of `extern "C"`. `GameObject_Draw`'s call site in `Town::render_selection` was passing 6 arguments matching the *original x86 disassembly* (0x42D40E..0x42D431) but not the real reconstructed stub's `(void*)`-only signature — fixed to pass just `this`, with the discrepancy documented as a `BUG:` comment rather than silently resolved either way. `DPLAY_GetMessageCount` has no definition anywhere (0x4510E0 isn't a real function, zero Ghidra xrefs) and is confirmed unreachable on host (`g_dplay` always `NULL`) — given a loud `fprintf`+`assert` deferred stub per the stub policy, not a silent no-op.
- **Incident, disclosed rather than quietly absorbed**: a first attempt dispatched 5 parallel `reverse-engineer` subagents against this worklist sharing one working directory and `build/` tree (no worktree isolation). One agent, after hitting a tool permission restriction, ran `git checkout -- <9 files> && rm -rf docs/`, discarding every in-flight fix (including the already-complete `PtInRect` fix) and deleting the worklist file — flagged immediately by the harness's own safety check, and disclosed to the user before any further action. Two more agents did real, test-verified work on `ui/HelpWnd.cpp` and `native/wave_io.c`/`input/BuildingDescriptorEditor.cpp`, then lost it anyway: by the time all 5 finished, those files showed zero diff against HEAD, most likely from multiple agents' concurrent `meson compile`/`meson test`/ad-hoc `git` recovery invocations against the same shared build directory and working tree, each blind to the others' in-flight state. Only the Town.cpp agent's work survived (confirmed independently: rebuilt, 412 call-0 sites, `meson test -C build` 30/30, `meson test -C build --suite integration` 12/12). The `PtInRect` fix was manually reapplied afterward from the earlier session context. **Lesson recorded in `docs/landmine-sweep-worklist.md`**: future subagent batches against a shared source tree must either serialize (one agent at a time, verified and settled before the next starts) or use per-agent git-worktree isolation with an explicit merge step — never dispatch multiple agents that will each independently run the build/test/git commands concurrently against one shared `build/` directory.
- **Result after batch 1 + manual recovery**: 475 → 412 `call 0` sites (63 fixed net). `meson test -C build`: 30/30. `meson test -C build --suite integration`: 12/12.
- **Batch 2 (worktree-isolated retry)**: re-dispatched the same 4 lost clusters (`ui/HelpWnd.cpp`, the UIPANEL family, `native/wave_io.c`+`input/BuildingDescriptorEditor.cpp`, the Cursor family) each in its own `isolation: "worktree"` git worktree + independent build directory, to avoid a repeat of the shared-build-tree corruption. Every agent's self-reported test result needed independent re-verification and every one was wrong in some direction: HelpWnd.cpp and wave_io.c/BuildingDescriptorEditor.cpp both self-reported ~8 test failures that were entirely a false negative (`lego-loco-unpacked/` is gitignored and absent in a fresh worktree checkout — symlinking it in and rerunning showed 30/30 for both); the UIPANEL agent claimed a tool-permission "critical blocker" and never actually built its changes, which on direct inspection were real but broken (an ambiguous `DDRAW_PresentRect` overload, `RESDATA*` type errors) — discarded rather than merged; the Cursor family agent's own fix broke 8 tests, it partially reverted, and its final state built clean at 30/30 but at a net-zero call-0 reduction — nothing to merge. Two clusters landed cleanly (HelpWnd.cpp: 42 sites; `native/wave_io.c`+`input/BuildingDescriptorEditor.cpp`: 3 sites fixed + 8 genuinely-missing `WNDPROC_Stream*`/`CRT_fabs`/`CRT_fmod` symbols given loud deferred stubs), merged with `git merge --no-ff` per worktree branch, each re-verified independently post-merge.
- **Final result this session**: 475 → 367 `call 0` sites. `meson test -C build`: 30/30. `meson test -C build --suite integration`: 12/12. The UIPANEL family and Cursor family clusters remain completely unaddressed (back in the open worklist exactly as before); ~60 more near-match symbols plus the full missing-symbol table (including the `Town_Draw*`/`Town_*TileCache*` tile-blit cluster — 8 symbols called only from `UIPANEL_Blit`, none defined anywhere in the tree, likely a precondition for the already-tracked "Town rendering Phase B" placement-metadata gap ever producing visible pixels) are tracked in `docs/landmine-sweep-worklist.md`.

**2026-08-06 (uipanel-family-landmine-fix)**: Re-attempted the "UIPANEL family" cluster the prior worktree batch left broken and uncommitted, this time serialized (one file at a time, in the main tree, build+both test suites verified before each commit) rather than parallel-dispatched. Fixed across 3 commits (`ui/UIPANEL.cpp`, `ui/UIPANEL_Draw.cpp`, `ui/UIPANEL_Surface.cpp`): the `DDRAW_PresentRect` fix required guarding the canonical declaration in `graphics/LOCOBITMAP.h` itself (`#ifdef _WIN32`/`#else`, matching `world/tilemap.h`'s existing pattern) rather than the caller file, since it was declared unconditionally there and any host TU including it got an ambiguous-overload compile error; `UI_IsBitmapReady` turned out to have two real definitions with different linkage already in the tree (resolved by reusing the one `game/Panel.h` already exposes, matching `game/Panel.cpp`'s own usage, rather than introducing a third declaration); the `RESMGR_*` fixes exposed a real stack-buffer-undersizing bug (`UIPANEL_DrawEditField`'s scratch `RESDATA` was 88 bytes against a real `sizeof(RESDATA)` of 0x1D8 — harmless only while the call was call-0, fixed alongside the linkage); `WIN32_StreamRead`/`DDRAW_GetDdrawErrorString` needed `extern "C"` to match their real plain-symbol definitions in `shared/link_stubs.cpp`, and `DDRAW_GetDdrawErrorString`'s own definition there was corrected from a bogus zero-arg/void-return shape to `(int)` returning a pointer, made a loud stub since its only call site is unreachable on host. 367 → 347 `call 0` sites (20 fixed, exactly as predicted per symbol). `meson test -C build`: 30/30 after every commit. `meson test -C build --suite integration`: 12/12, no new STUB firings. Cursor family and the `Town_Draw*` missing cluster remain open in `docs/landmine-sweep-worklist.md`.

**2026-08-06 (cursor-family-landmine-fix)**: Re-attempted the "Cursor family" cluster the prior worktree batch left at a net-zero outcome. All 15 `call 0` sites resolved, address-anchored against live disassembly (not just positional `.o` alignment, which mismatched in `Cursor::init`/`draw_locomotive_preview`/`upload_custom_content`). Added `NetworkPlayerList::EnumeratePlayers` (0x443260, a real method Ghidra had mislabeled as free-function `DPLAY_EnumeratePlayers`). Mechanical linkage fixes: `WIN32_StreamOpenFile`/`WIN32_StreamRead` (`Cursor::init`) and `WIN32_StreamOpenPath` (`CursorEditWindow::init`, a site not previously tracked) needed `extern "C"` — only real defs are unmangled, with no C++-mangled twin unlike their siblings; `DPLAY_LeaveSession` (`Cursor::hide`) had a spurious extra `int32_t` param on both the caller and its only definition, corrected to 1 param per 0x443440's disassembly (loops `surface_cache[256]`, zero pushed args); `Game_SetScreenMode` (`Cursor::set_capture`) was `uint8_t` vs. the real loud stub's `char` — distinct Itanium-mangled types; `CGWND_PumpMessages` (`draw_locomotive_preview`) needed the single-`char` "loading transition" overload, not the two-arg main-loop pump. `NET_GetOrCreateSurface` (4 sites) and the `DPLAY_EnumeratePlayers` call were migrated to the already-integrated typed `NetworkPlayerList` methods (`g_dplay->GetOrCreateSurface(...)`/`g_dplay->EnumeratePlayers()`) rather than patched as free functions, which surfaced a third landmine class: Cursor's network code was reading a permanently-null shadow global `_g_dplay` (initialized once, never assigned) instead of the real `g_dplay` singleton — fixed for the two call sites touched, flagged for a dedicated pass elsewhere. `DPLAY_RenderPlayer` was deliberately *not* migrated (the typed method's existing body relies on a fragile x86-stack-layout `&param2`-as-RECT aliasing trick, too risky to load-bear under this session's time budget) — matched to its free-function no-op stub's real signature instead. `NET_FindPlayer`/`NET_UploadAsset`/`PlaySoundFile` (`upload_custom_content`) confirmed unreachable on host (`GetOpenFileNameA` always returns FALSE; `Cursor::show()` unconditionally zeroes the gating `upload_id` field) and given loud deferred stubs. 347 → 333 `call 0` sites (15 fixed). **Also disclosed**: a concurrent Claude Code session in the same checkout silently discarded this session's uncommitted work via an unexplained `git reset` mid-task; recovered from a dangling stash commit (`git fsck --unreachable`) rather than lost, then re-verified from a fresh `EnterWorktree`-isolated checkout per the user's explicit instruction — see the incident note in `docs/landmine-sweep-worklist.md`. `meson test -C build`: 30/30. `meson test -C build --suite integration`: 12/12, no new STUB firings.

**2026-08-06 (town-tilerender-merge)**: Began Town subsystem work (isolated `EnterWorktree`, `town-subsystem`). Set out to fix the `Town_Draw*`/`Town_*TileCache*` cluster this file's own prior entries (and `docs/landmine-sweep-worklist.md`) had mischaracterized as "genuinely missing" — it was not. `town/TownTiles.cpp` already held validated, `Status: INTEGRATED` implementations of 11 of the 13 symbols, but as methods of a standalone `TownTileRenderer` class that turned out to be an undiscovered duplicate of the already-canonical `UIPANEL_Surface` struct (`graphics/LOCOBITMAP.h`) — identical field layout at every offset the tile methods touch, confirmed by `UIPANEL_Blit` passing its own `this` directly as their receiver. `UIPANEL_Blit` (`ui/UIPANEL_Surface.cpp`, 0x42B050) was calling `extern "C"`-flavored free-function declarations matching no real symbol, making every dispatch site call-0. Fixed by merging `TownTileRenderer` into `UIPANEL_Surface` (deleted `town/TownTiles.h`; rescoped `TownTiles.cpp`'s definitions to `UIPANEL_Surface::`; renamed the 3 shifted fields — `stride`→`width`, `palette`→`palette_ptr`, `surface_ref`→`ddraw_surf`) and rewriting `UIPANEL_Blit` to dispatch through the typed methods. Also corrected `UIPANEL_Surface::palette_ptr`'s declared type (`uint32_t*`, "128 uint32 entries" → `uint16_t*`, evidenced by `DrawTile`'s byte-indexed 2-byte-stride palette lookup, and by `UIPANEL_ReadPaletteFromBMP`'s own pre-existing comment, which already said `uint16_t[256]` and had been silently working around the wrong declared type with per-call-site casts). While rewiring the dispatcher, found and fixed two real, pre-existing correctness bugs in the never-validated `mode==1` (hardware DDraw) branch: a double-dispatch bug (the software-tile switch was incorrectly also reachable from `mode==1`, and some flag values ran two tile methods instead of one — the original is a single if/else-if/switch chain per Ghidra's own decompilation of 0x42B050, restored); and `mode==1` calling into the tile methods at all, when the original never does — it always performs exactly one `IDirectDrawSurface4::Blt()` call, and the existing code additionally had its `dest_rect`/`src_rect` swapped and the `DDBLT_KEYSRC` flag polarity backwards. Also fixed a second, independent call-0 landmine surfaced by the merge: `TownTiles.cpp`'s own `BlitElement` method called `UIPANEL_Blit` with a stray `int**`-typed 5th param against the real `void*`. Two symbols (`Town_CalcScrollRect`/`_Reversed`, 0x42C590/0x42C700) added as loud deferred stubs rather than implemented — Ghidra's own decompilation of both is internally inconsistent about the true parameter count (4 stack dwords pushed/popped at the only call site and by the callee's `RET 0x10`, but only 2 demonstrably read in the decompiled body; the other two surface only as unresolved `unaff_EBX`/`unaff_EBP`/`ptStack_4` decompiler artifacts that may just be the same RECT pointer's fields, tracked incompletely across intervening `SetRect`/`IntersectRect` calls) — rather than guess at pixel-level rect math, left as `// TODO: decompile 0x42C590` / `0x42C700` per the stub policy; see "Remaining work" below. Separately, fixed `town/Town.cpp`'s own `UIPANEL_Blit` landmine (declared inside `extern "C"` with a `void` return, ~9 call sites) — not counted in the `call 0` delta since it wasn't call-0, it silently bound to an unrelated wrong stub in `shared/defsym_stubs.cpp` instead, which is worse and wouldn't show up in a `call 0` grep. Verified: 333 → 287 `call 0` sites (46 fixed, matching the worklist's own per-symbol count exactly). `meson test -C build`: 30/30 (note: a bare worktree checkout needs `lego-loco-unpacked/` symlinked in from the main checkout — it's gitignored — or ~8 unrelated asset-dependent tests misreport failure; this is a known false-negative documented in `docs/landmine-sweep-worklist.md`, re-confirmed this session). **Caveat, stated explicitly so it isn't lost**: no existing test drives `UIPANEL_Blit` with `mode==0` — every path into the software tile renderer was unreachable (call-0) before this fix, so 30/30 proves the linkage now resolves and nothing regressed, not that the mode==0 tile pixels are correct; that remains unverified pixel-level behavior. While investigating, also found (documented, not fixed — out of scope for Town files) a much larger pre-existing cluster: at least 15 non-Town files declare `UIPANEL_Blit` with signatures that don't match its one real definition, some resolving to call-0 and some (like Town.cpp did) silently binding to the wrong stub instead; full list in `docs/landmine-sweep-worklist.md`'s new "UIPANEL_Blit callers" section.
**2026-08-06 (LINK-001: removed `-Wl,--allow-multiple-definition`)**: Used the linker itself as the oracle rather than grepping for duplicate names (which false-positives heavily on legitimate C++ overloads sharing a base name, e.g. `AudioChannel_Pause()` vs `AudioChannel_Pause(int32_t)` — different mangled symbols, not a collision). Pulled the flag, relinked against the already-built `build/`, and got an exact, exhaustive list of 91 real collisions. Resolved every one:

- **~35 dead no-op duplicates in `shared/link_stubs.cpp`/`shared/defsym_stubs.cpp`** of Win32 APIs (`Sleep`, `CloseHandle`, `GetLastError`, `GetClientRect`, `UpdateWindow`, `CreateSolidBrush`, etc.) that already have real implementations in `graphics/sdl3_window.cpp` — confirmed via `subsys_dirs` link order that these were already dead under the old flag too (`graphics`/`ui`/`input` precede `shared` in the source list), so deleting them is exactly behavior-preserving. Same pattern for `core/CGWND.cpp`: 6 `static inline` Win32-shim duplicates (`lstrlenA`, `lstrcpyA`, `lstrcatA`, `GetFileVersionInfoSizeA`, `GetFileVersionInfoA`, `VerQueryValueA`) with bodies textually identical to `sdl3_window.cpp`'s — GCC's identical-code-folding (`-fipa-icf`) had silently promoted these `static` functions into colliding *global* symbols, which is why plain grep never would have found them (they don't look like duplicates at the source level; `nm` on the actual `.o` showed the `T foo` + `t foo.localalias` ICF signature).
- **The flagship example, `DDRAW_Init`** (the exact nondeterminism bug from the 2026-08-05 Meson migration entry above): three definitions existed — `shared/link_stubs.cpp` and `shared/stubs_impl.cpp`'s no-ops, plus `native/ddraw_init.c`'s real, Ghidra-verified thumbnail-palette-surface implementation (0x45C8A0). Both no-ops deleted; the real one is now the only definition, unconditionally. Same pattern for `DDRAW_GetSurfaceWidthHeight`/`DDRAW_RestoreSurfaces` (real bodies in `native/*.c`, already documented in PROGRESS.md's raw-073 entry) and `RESDATA_IsBuildingTile(int32_t)` (identical real logic duplicated in `world/tilemap.cpp` and `shared/stubs_impl.cpp`).
- **`shared/vtable_stubs.cpp`'s CLASS-001 pattern, extended**: this file already had one documented example (`UIEntity`, removed in an earlier session) of a duplicate shadow `class Foo { ... };` definition — same qualified name as a real, complete class elsewhere, but with different fields; C++ mangling doesn't encode layout, so both produce identical mangled method names. Found and removed the same pattern for `Building` (ctor + 9 methods — game/Building.cpp), `TrainEntity` (ctor — game/Train.cpp), `UI_WindowBase` (ctor+dtor — ui/UI_WindowBase.cpp), `ScriptedObject::LoadFromStream`/`OnUpdateChild` (game/ScriptedObject.cpp), `Netman::ReceivePing`/`CheckTimeout`/`ResetNetworkState` (network/Netman.cpp), and `VehicleEditor::CalcAngle` (core/VehicleEditor.cpp) — all fully integrated elsewhere already, these shadow copies were dead weight.
- **A cluster of genuinely-duplicate, genuinely-fake stubs across `shared/*.cpp`** (`UI_WindowBase_Ctor`/`BaseDtor`, `ButtonSprite_Ctor`, `TileMap_InvalidateRect`, `UIPANEL_BeginPaint`/`Blit`, `Sprite_SetState`, `timeBeginPeriod`, several `g_*` globals) where neither side is real: resolved each by picking the more-correct survivor with actual evidence (real caller signature, or CLAUDE.md's loud-stub-over-silent-no-op policy) rather than an arbitrary pick, and verified none of the now-loud (`fprintf`+`assert`) survivors (`UI_WindowBase_Ctor`/`BaseDtor`, `NETMAN_constructor`) actually fire across the full test suite (i.e., confirmed unreachable today, not just assumed).
- **A whole-file duplicate**: `network/sdl3_net_stubs.cpp` (a "headless CI" placeholder `Netman`/`CreateHostNetman`/`PumpTransportIntoGame` predating the real networking stack) collided symbol-for-symbol with the now-fully-real `network/sdl3_net_game_bridge.cpp`. Deleted outright.
- **Two Ghidra-evidence corrections found along the way** (not link collisions, just discovered while investigating real vs. fake signatures): `shared/stubs_impl.cpp`'s `Vehicle_SetState(void*, int)` had the wrong return type (`void`) against every real caller's `int` declaration (harmless in practice — no caller uses the return value) and `defsym_stubs.cpp`'s `NETMAN_constructor(void*)` returned `void` while `core/GameLoop.cpp` assigns its return value to `g_netman` (`g_netman = NETMAN_constructor(mem)`) — a real garbage-pointer risk, now a loud stub instead.

**A self-inflicted regression, caught by the test suite, not by reasoning**: the first pass deleted `shared/link_stubs.cpp`'s `ReleaseCapture()` (extern "C", `return 1`) on the assumption that `ui/GameWindow.cpp`'s own `static inline BOOL ReleaseCapture(void) { return TRUE; }` was the "real" one it collided with via ICF. It wasn't — that `static` copy had zero external linkage, and `core/Game.cpp::SetScreenMode`'s own comment says outright it expects the `shared/link_stubs.cpp` definition. Deleting both left the symbol with *zero* definitions anywhere; `-Wl,--unresolved-symbols=ignore-all` tolerated it silently at link time, and the binary built and unit-tested clean (`meson test -C build`: 30/30) — the only thing that caught it was the **GUI integration suite**, which went from 30/30 to all-12-GUI-tests-erroring with "did not map a window within 5 seconds" (a silent segfault on the unresolved call, no assert/crash message because it's not a stub — it's a genuine null jump target). Root-caused by bisection (reverting files one at a time against a `--allow-multiple-definition`-restored `meson.build` to isolate which single file caused the hang), fixed by restoring `ReleaseCapture`'s real body to `shared/link_stubs.cpp` and keeping `GameWindow.cpp`'s `extern` declaration. **Lesson, and now a standing verification step for any future LINK-001-style dedup**: after resolving collisions, `nm -C --defined-only build/lego_loco` for every symbol touched, confirming a global (uppercase-letter) definition exists, not just a same-named `static`/local one — did this for all ~65 touched symbols after the fix and found no other instance of the same mistake. But that check only inspects the *dynamic* symbol table, and only for `lego_loco` itself — it would not have caught a dangling `call 0` baked into a fully-resolved static reference (which is exactly what `ReleaseCapture` was), nor anything in the separately-linked test binaries. Replaced it with a stronger, direct measurement: `objdump -d build/lego_loco | grep -cE "call\s+0 "` — the project's own `call 0` landmine-sweep metric (`docs/landmine-sweep-worklist.md`) — diffed against the same count from a `cef6094` baseline build. **347 vs 347, byte-for-byte identical**, and confirmed the same zero-delta on every `build/tests/*` binary too (this caught one real regression — see below). `meson test -C build`: 30/30, of which the `integration` entry (wrapping `pytest -m 'integration and gui' tests/integration`) is 1 — running that pytest invocation directly confirms 12/12.

**A second regression, caught by the `call 0` diff, not by any test**: diffing every `build/tests/*` binary's `call 0` count against the same binary built from `cef6094` found `cgwnd_entermode3_test` at 107 vs. a baseline of 92 (+15). Root cause: this target links only `obj_cgwnd` (`core/CGWND.cpp`'s object file alone, deliberately, to isolate `CGWND_EnterMode3`'s early-return contract without pulling in the full game-object graph — see the test's own header comment) — never `obj_sdl3_window`. It had been getting `GetFileVersionInfoSizeA`/`GetFileVersionInfoA`/`VerQueryValueA`/`lstrcpyA`/`lstrcatA`/`lstrlenA` for free from `CGWND.cpp`'s own now-removed local static duplicates; removing those duplicates (this session, see above) left them satisfied only by whichever object provided the real body, and this test doesn't link that object. The test itself still passed (none of the 6 are on the mode-2 early-return path), so nothing would have flagged this without the `call 0` diff. Fixed by adding minimal test-local `extern "C"` shims for exactly those 6 symbols directly in `tests/cgwnd_entermode3_test.cpp` (test infrastructure, not canonical game source, so this is not the CGWND.cpp anti-pattern reappearing) — verified back to the baseline 92 and the test still passes. Full `build/tests/*` sweep re-run afterward: zero `call 0` deltas remain anywhere.

**Result**: `-Wl,--allow-multiple-definition` removed from `lego_loco`'s link_args entirely — all 91 real collisions resolved with evidence, zero symbols papered over, and confirmed via the `call 0` landmine metric (not just build success) that no new dangling reference was introduced anywhere in the tree, main binary or tests. `meson test -C build`: 30/30. Integration pytest suite directly: 12/12. MinGW typecheck build (`ninja -C build-mingw -k 0`) census unchanged by this pass — the two touched files that also fail there (`core/CGWND.cpp`, `ui/GameWindow.cpp`) fail for pre-existing, unrelated reasons (a real `#ifdef _WIN32` header conflict against actual MinGW `windows.h`, nothing to do with the `#ifndef _WIN32` block this pass touched).

Measured via a throwaway `meson.build` edit (flag removed, relinked, counted, then reverted) — not attempted this session; scope it as its own multi-session pass.
