**2026-08-06 (directplay-cluster-investigation)**: Attempted the `DirectPlay_*` cluster (7 symbols, `game/Train_network.cpp`) as a mechanical linkage fix following the `UIPANEL_Blit`/`CGWND_SetMode` pattern; it isn't one. Confirmed the linkage mismatch is real via Ghidra and found 2 bogus address annotations along the way, but fixing the linkage made `TrainSubsystem`'s constructor's DirectPlay init reachable for the first time and crashed on a pre-existing 32-to-64-bit pointer-truncation bug in `network/DirectPlay.h`/`.cpp` (session handle typed `int32_t`, cast back to a pointer). Widening the two crashing functions' `session` param to `void*` fixed the crash but exposed a second, distinct regression on multiplayer re-entry (7/12 GUI integration tests hang, non-crashing) — the connection-list traversal inside `DestroyPeer` still round-trips node pointers through `int32_t`, so the field-width problem runs deeper than the two widened parameters. Rather than keep chasing this (3 direction changes already: widen → partial revert → widen 2-of-7), reverted all of it via `git stash` (kept on the stash stack, not committed) and rebuilt/retested at the pre-investigation baseline (`41846f6`): `meson test -C build` 30/30, `--suite integration` 12/12, both back to normal ~46s runtime (confirming the hang was caused by the investigation's changes, not a flake). Full findings — bogus addresses, the two-stage regression, the bridge-overload interaction — written to `docs/landmine-sweep-worklist.md`'s "DirectPlay_* cluster" section so a future session doesn't redo this work. Also used this to correct the census methodology: `FormatResourceString` (previously listed as a near-mechanical fix) is a false positive — the "real implementation elsewhere" the census pointed to is `static` (internal linkage), so it can't be what these callers actually bind to; both genuinely-real overloads are themselves no-ops. Added both lessons (linkage-blindness, load-bearing-wrong-stub risk) to the worklist's census-methodology section.
- [ ] **`DirectPlay_*` cluster in `game/Train_network.cpp` (7 symbols) — investigated 2026-08-06, blocked on a prerequisite bug, not fixed.** The linkage mismatch itself is real (confirmed via Ghidra) and mechanically fixable like `UIPANEL_Blit`/`CGWND_SetMode`, but fixing it makes `TrainSubsystem::TrainSubsystem`'s constructor's DirectPlay init reachable for the first time, which crashes: `network/DirectPlay.h`/`.cpp` type the connection-list session handle as `int32_t` and cast it back to a pointer internally — a 32-to-64-bit truncation bug, dormant only because every caller was previously call-0. Widening `DirectPlay_Close`/`DirectPlay_DestroyPeer`'s `session` param to `void*` fixes that crash (verified: single-player and first multiplayer-menu entry pass, 30/30 unit tests) but exposes a **second** regression: re-entering multiplayer (which tears down and recreates the DirectPlay peer) hangs — 7/12 GUI integration tests time out non-crashing. The connection-list traversal inside `DestroyPeer` still round-trips every node pointer through `int32_t`/`uintptr_t`, so the field-width problem isn't limited to the one parameter that got widened. **Needs a full pointer-width audit of `network/DirectPlay.h`/`.cpp`'s connection-list fields (every `int32_t` that ever holds a pointer) before this cluster's linkage can be fixed safely** — reverted via `git stash` rather than committed. Full writeup, bogus-address findings (0x461990/0x461A00 have no function; 0x45EDE0 is actually `EnumConnections`; 0x45F050 is actually `GetSessionDesc`), and the bridge-overload interaction with `network/sdl3_directplay_train_bridge.cpp` are in `docs/landmine-sweep-worklist.md`'s "DirectPlay_* cluster" section.
- [x] **`network/DirectPlay.cpp`'s `IDirectPlay4A`/`IDirectPlayLobby3A` vtable-helper table mismatches — RESOLVED (2026-08-08).** All offsets now match the real DX6 SDK slot order; `dplay_Open`→renamed to real `dplay_EnumSessions` (offset 0x34) with its call site's wrong 3rd argument (`&GUID_SessionDesc`, a GUID pointer) fixed to the real callback function pointer; `dplay_EnumPlayers`→renamed to real `dplay_Open` (offset 0x60); `dplay_GetSessionDesc`→renamed to real `dplay_GetPlayerAddress` (offset 0x48); `dpaddr2_EnumPlayers`→renamed to `dplobby3a_EnumAddress`; the dead, separately-mislabeled old `dplay_EnumSessions` (offset 0x44)→renamed to `dplay_GetMessageCount`; `dplay_ClosePlayer`→renamed to `dplay_CancelMessage`. `DirectPlay_CreatePlayer`'s real callback signature was re-derived from disassembly (0x45FBD0, `RET 0x10`) and found to be a `LPDPENUMADDRESSCALLBACK` that looks for the `DPAID_Modem` GUID chunk — renamed to `DirectPlay_FindModemNameCallback`. The two higher-level callers were renamed to match what they actually do: `DirectPlay_GetSessionDesc`→`DirectPlay_FindLocalModemName` (0x45EEC0, queries `GetPlayerAddress`+`EnumAddress`, no session desc involved at all), `DirectPlay_EnumPlayers`→`DirectPlay_OpenSession` (0x45FD80, calls the real `Open`, not `EnumPlayers`). **Still open, tracked separately**: `DirectPlay_SetSessionDesc` (0x45F090) itself was *not* renamed — it calls the real `EnumSessions` with a genuine callback at 0x45F2B0 that turned out to be substantial, previously entirely unexamined code (builds a session-list linked list conditionally on `connection_type`), left as a documented `DirectPlay_EnumSessionsCallback` stub (`// TODO: decompile 0x45F2B0`) rather than guessed at from a partial `decode_instructions` read — its full body, and therefore `DirectPlay_SetSessionDesc`'s true higher-level purpose (join-precheck vs. host-precheck), needs its own dedicated pass. Also open: why `DirectPlay_FindLocalModemName` calls `GetPlayerAddress` with `idPlayer=0` (`DPID_ALLPLAYERS`) rather than the local player's own DPID — functionally plausible (no player may be created yet at that point) but not confirmed against real DirectPlay runtime docs. Verified: `meson compile -C build` clean, `meson test -C build` unchanged at 22/30 (same 8 pre-existing environment failures), full-suite diff confirms zero new regressions. **Superseded (2026-08-08, typed-vtable-cleanup below)**: every `dplay_*`/`dplobby3a_*`/`com_QueryInterface`/`ddraw_surface_*` raw vtable-offset helper named in this entry was deleted outright, not just relabeled — the corrected offsets captured here were the input to that pass, not its output. `network/DirectPlay.cpp` now calls real typed C++ virtual methods (`IDirectPlay4A`/`IDirectPlayLobby3A`) instead of any vtable-offset helper.

**2026-08-06 (getresourcetype-cluster)**: Fixed `GetResourceType(int)` silent-wrong-stub cluster (`e114a0b`). `game/BuildingMgr.cpp` and `core/BuildingMgrObjectGroup.cpp` declared `GetResourceType(int)` instead of the real `GetResourceType(unsigned int)` (0x446030, Ghidra-verified: `(id>>10)&0xF`, capped at 0x10) — since C++ mangling doesn't encode return type, both bound clean but silently to `shared/defsym_stubs.cpp`'s void-returning `(int)` no-op instead of the real implementation, reading garbage resource types. `ui/UI_ChildWindow.cpp` had the same wrong param type *and* had it declared inside an `extern "C"` block (dead on host via `#ifdef _WIN32`, but wrong for the mingw typecheck target). Fixed all 3 to `unsigned int`, C++ linkage. Also fixed a bogus address in `world/EditorState.cpp` (0x45AAA0 — Ghidra confirms no function there — should be 0x446030) and removed the two now-dead stubs (`GetResourceType(int)` and a zero-arg `GetResourceType()`, zero referrers confirmed via `nm`). `call 0` count unchanged (287 — silent-stub binding, not a call-0 site). `meson test -C build`: 30/30. `--suite integration`: 12/12.

**2026-08-06 (resdata-tile-predicate-cluster)**: Fixed `RESDATA_IsBuildingTile`/`RESDATA_IsRoadTile` cluster (4 files). `town/Town.cpp` declared `RESDATA_IsBuildingTile(void*)` with a bogus address (0x44C4E0 — Ghidra confirms that's mid-body of `VehicleEditor_Update`, not this function); `game/Vehicle.cpp` declared both predicates correctly-typed but wrongly inside an `extern "C"` block. Both silently bound to `shared/defsym_stubs.cpp`'s plain no-ops instead of the real C++-linkage `(int32_t)` implementations (`RESDATA_IsBuildingTile` 0x44BD30 in `world/tilemap.cpp`; `RESDATA_IsRoadTile` 0x44BD10 in `shared/stubs_impl.cpp` — also corrected a wrong address comment in `input/InputMgr.cpp` that cited 0x44BD50 for `RESDATA_IsRoadTile`, which Ghidra shows is actually `RESDATA_IsWaterTile`). `game/ResdataGameVehicle.cpp` declared `RESDATA_IsRoadTile(void*)` — a **genuine call-0** (no definition anywhere matches that mangled name) — fixed by correcting the param to `int32_t`; `call 0` dropped 287→286, confirming it. Removed 6 now-dead decoy/no-op artifacts (`shared/link_stubs.cpp`'s `__pv`/`__i`-suffixed renamed placeholders plus misleading extern redeclarations claiming the wrong return type; `shared/defsym_stubs.cpp`'s two zero-arg no-ops). **Reachability caveat, checked before committing** (this session's DirectPlay investigation, immediately above, made exactly this kind of check standard practice): temporarily instrumented the two call sites that cast a live pointer through the real function's `int32_t` param (`Town.cpp`'s building-tile check, `ResdataGameVehicle.cpp`'s road-tile check) with an `fprintf`, ran the full GUI integration suite, and got zero hits — neither branch is exercised by any current test, so this fix's behavior on a real pointer is unverified, not proven safe by a passing test run. Proceeded anyway based on stronger evidence: `readelf -h build/lego_loco` confirms `EXEC` (non-PIE, not `DYN`), so the heap sits well under 4GB and the `int32_t`↔pointer round-trip the real function does is very unlikely to lose bits in practice; and `input/InputMgr.cpp` already calls this identical real function with an identical truncating cast, live, inside currently-passing tests (`inputmgr_canonical_test`, `input_world_test`), with no observed corruption. Instrumentation removed before commit. Verified the zero-delta call-0 bar (memory lesson 1c) against the pre-fix baseline (`e114a0b`) on every test binary, not just `lego_loco`: `cgwnd_entermode3_test` 92, `host_mode3_bootstrap_test` 21, all others 0 — unchanged except `lego_loco` itself (287→286, expected). `meson test -C build`: 30/30. `--suite integration`: 12/12.

**2026-08-08 (editorstate-cpp-cast-cluster / building-retype-resolution)**: Fixed `world/EditorState.cpp`'s STRICT=2 old-style-cast cluster and resolved the three-time-deferred `EditorState::building` retype the `Vehicle.cpp` (`fffbbe5`) and `World.cpp` (`9626012`) clusters both left open. **Retype decision**: `EditorState::building` (`world/EditorState.h`) is `GameVehicle*`, not `Building*` — single consistent type, no union/runtime-variant. Evidence: (1) every offset this file and its callers read through the field — `+0x18` (`GameObject::initialized`), `+0x40` (`Entity::resource`), `+0x88`/`+0x8A` (`ResourceGameObject::sub_pos_x`/`sub_pos_y`), `+0x10C`/`+0x110`/`+0x114`/`+0x118` (`RESDATA_GameVehicle::vehicle_kind`/`init_state`/`counter_timer`/what was mislabeled `reserved`), and `+0x11C` (`GameVehicle::occupant_state`) — line up exactly with named fields already documented in `game/GameVehicle.h`/`ResdataGameVehicle.h`; Building tops out at 0xF4 bytes, so everything from `+0x10C` up doesn't exist on it, and Building's own `+0x88` is a 1-byte `occupation_level`, not a 16-bit coordinate. (2) The `+0x11C` read specifically requires the *full* `GameVehicle` (0x12C bytes) — `occupant_state` is GameVehicle-specific, not present on the smaller `RESDATA_GameVehicle` base (0x11C bytes) — ruling out the weaker "maybe just RESDATA_GameVehicle" alternative. (3) `game/Vehicle.cpp`'s cluster already carried `reinterpret_cast<GameVehicle*>(...->building)` workarounds at every touch point with comments flagging this exact mistyping; `core/VehicleEditor.cpp`'s `MoveAlongTrack` had an open TODO wondering whether `end_a/end_b->building`'s `+0x88`/`+0x8A` reads were "Building or a track subtype" — they're `ResourceGameObject::sub_pos_x`/`sub_pos_y`, i.e. the same GameVehicle-family node, closing that TODO the same direction. Not fresh Ghidra reads this session — the type conclusion is drawn from convergent evidence already sitting in the tree (prior Ghidra-confirmed commits' comments, matching header field docs), cross-checked for consistency rather than re-verified against fresh disassembly. Retyped `EditorState::building` (`GameVehicle*`), `EditorState::FindAdjacentTrack()`'s return type, and `EditorState::HandleDirection`'s `train` param; converted every raw-offset read through it in `world/EditorState.cpp` to named-field access, removing the `reinterpret_cast<GameVehicle*>` workarounds and stale "declared Building* but real object is..." comments in `game/Vehicle.cpp`, `game/World.cpp`, and `core/VehicleEditor.cpp`'s `ProcessMove`/`MoveAlongTrack`. **Two bugs found and fixed along the way, both Ghidra-adjacent (inferred from the binary's own field layout, not a fresh disassembly read this session)**: (a) `RESDATA_GameVehicle`'s `+0x118` field was documented `reserved`/"unused" — it's a `Vehicle*` (the vehicle currently boarding/attached at this track node), corroborated independently by `game/Building.cpp`'s own pre-existing, separately-Ghidra-confirmed `FindPathToTarget` comment reading the identical offset on a different object as a `Vehicle*`; renamed to `boarding_vehicle` (`Vehicle*`), safe on any host width since it's a live C++ field, not an on-disk resource blob. (b) `EditorState::UpdateEditMode` had cached `this->building` into a frozen local and reused it after calling `FindAdjacentTrack()` (which can reassign `this->building` to an adjacent node on success) — the original disassembly re-dereferences `this+0x14` fresh at each of four sites after that call; fixed to match (caught by review, not by a failing test — no test currently exercises the reassignment path). **A real, host-only bug found in `world/tilemap.h`, left unfixed but worked around**: `TileMapResource::expected_ids` (+0x564) is declared `int32_t*` — a native, host-width pointer inside a struct that otherwise mirrors the original 32-bit on-disk resource blob byte-for-byte — so on this 64-bit host it's 8 bytes instead of 4, and every member declared after it (`state_63A` included) lands 4 bytes further out than the real asset data (verified directly: `static_assert(offsetof(TileMapResource, state_63A) == 0x63A)` fails, computes 0x642). This also affects `game/Building.cpp`'s and `world/tilemap.cpp`'s own pre-existing `->state_63A` reads through the same struct — not introduced here. Rather than widen the fix into rewriting `TileMapResource`'s on-disk-vs-host pointer representation (a separate, larger cluster — candidate follow-up: unify it with `shared/types.h`'s `RESDATA`, which already documents the same `+0x08 object_type` field independently), added a local `ReadTileTypeByte()` helper in `EditorState.cpp` that reads the fixed 32-bit byte offset directly, bypassing the struct's drifted member; used it at all 13 sites that previously read `+0x63A`. Fields before `+0x564` (`resource_id` +0x04, `grid_span_y` +0x16B, `original_span` +0x16C) are unaffected and read through the struct normally. Also renamed `GameVehicle::occupant_state`'s doc comment: this file's road-tile branch of `UpdateVehiclePlacement` sets/checks it against `1`, a third value beyond the class's documented `0`=free/`2`=occupied (a narrower "claimed by an in-progress editor placement" state). **Verification**: `ninja -C build-strict2` targeted grep for `world/EditorState.cpp` — clean (was the file's own 276-site old-style-cast list). All 5 touched compile units (`world_EditorState.cpp.o`, `core_VehicleEditor.cpp.o`, `game_Vehicle.cpp.o`, `game_World.cpp.o`, `game_ResdataGameVehicle.cpp.o`) compile clean under strict2 (only pre-existing `-Weffc++`/`-Wall` warnings, no errors). `ninja -C build`, `meson test -C build`: 30/30 including `--suite integration`. `call 0` count: **272, unchanged** (`objdump -d build/lego_loco | grep -cE "call\s+0 "`) — no new landmines from the retype or the renamed `HandleDirection`/`FindAdjacentTrack` signatures (grepped tree-wide for stale local declarations, found none). mingw typecheck diff against an isolated `git worktree` baseline at the same commit: identical error signatures/counts for every touched file (only line-number churn from added comments); the handful of additional pre-existing `-Werror=invalid-offsetof` (`GameVehicle`/`RESDATA_GameVehicle`) hits now also surfacing via `core/VehicleEditor.cpp`'s TU (which didn't previously include `GameVehicle.h`) are the same already-broken headers appearing through one more translation unit, not a new failure category — confirmed via the same worktree diff. Adding the new `Vehicle* boarding_vehicle` pointer member to `RESDATA_GameVehicle` does not regress any strict=2 test target: `-Weffc++`'s "has pointer data members" diagnostic is a plain warning project-wide (`meson.build`'s `warnflags` only adds bare `-Weffc++`) and only becomes a hard error under `tests/meson.build`'s `test_warn_args` blanket `-Werror` — every test TU that transitively includes `GameVehicle.h` (`input_world_test.cpp`, `inputmgr_canonical_test.cpp`, `persistence_adapter_test.cpp`) was already failing to compile under strict=2 for unrelated pre-existing reasons (verified against the isolated worktree baseline), so none of them regressed from passing to failing. **Incidental discovery, not this session's work**: a concurrent, unrelated agent session was live-editing `town/Town.cpp` (and had already touched `game/BuildingMgr.cpp`, `graphics/LOCOBITMAP.h`, `town/Town.h`, `town/TownTiles.cpp`, `world/scriptengine.cpp`, `world/scriptengine.h`) in the same working tree throughout this session; handled by using isolated `git worktree`s for baseline comparisons rather than `git stash` (an earlier `git stash`/`pop` round-trip on those 7 files raced with the other session's concurrent edit to `town/Town.cpp` — recovered cleanly via targeted `git checkout stash@{0} -- <file>` on the 6 non-conflicting files, left `town/Town.cpp` on its current, newer, unstashed content, stash entry left in the stack rather than dropped).

**2026-08-08 (directx-sdk-header-audit)**: Cross-checked `network/DirectPlay.{h,cpp}`, `graphics/DDRAW.h`, `native/DDRAW_GetSurfaceWidthHeight.c`, and `stubs/{dplay,ddraw}.h` against real Microsoft DirectX headers, per the user's request to compare against https://github.com/Olde-Skuul/directdraw and https://github.com/Olde-Skuul/directplay (modern republished DirectDraw 7.0/DirectPlay-Aug-2007 SDK reconstructions), then upgraded to the period-accurate primary source once the user located it: the genuine DirectX 6.0 SDK ISO (archive.org `dx-6-sdk`, MD5 `563fad13207f30c0435a19aff4a46cea`, verified against archive.org's own checksum) — see `NOTE-directx-sdk.md` for the independent PE-timestamp/import-table evidence that pins loco.exe to DX6.0 specifically. All findings below are confirmed against the real DX6 `DPLAY.H`/`DPLOBBY.H`/`DDRAW.H` (extracted to a scratchpad, not committed), not just the modern mirror, and cross-checked against Ghidra disassembly (not decompiler pseudocode, which was independently shown to fabricate a stale-register 4th argument on one call site) via `search_bytes`/`read_bytes`/`disassemble_function` on the `locoaudit` database.

**Fixed (safe, no behavior change on any linked path — `stubs/ddraw.h` and `stubs/dplay.h` are never linked to real ddraw.lib/dplayx.lib; the `network/DirectPlay.cpp` struct/comment edits touch a documentation-only, uninstantiated `DirectPlaySession` struct and dead-code comments)**:
- Two GUID mislabels in `network/DirectPlay.h`/`.cpp`, both byte-verified via `read_bytes` at the binary address then matched against `DEFINE_GUID` entries in the real SDK: the GUID at 0x478F88 (previously "`CLSID_DirectPlay`") is actually `IID_IDirectPlay4A`; the real `CLSID_DirectPlay` sits 16 bytes later at 0x478F98 (confirmed via `search_bytes` for its real byte pattern, xref'd only from the still-deferred body of `DirectPlay_HandleMessages`). The GUID at 0x479048 (previously "`IID_IDirectPlayAddr2`") is actually `IID_IDirectPlayLobby3A` — no `IDirectPlayAddress`/`IDirectPlayAddress2` COM interface exists anywhere in classic DirectPlay (grepped the entire real SDK, confirmed absent); `DirectPlay_CreateAddress`'s (0x45E4B0) `Ordinal_4(...)` → `QueryInterface(&DAT_00479048, ...)` → `Release` sequence is a lobby-object-to-Lobby3A upgrade, not an address-interface query. Renamed the C++ symbol `CLSID_DirectPlay` → `IID_IDirectPlay4A` at all 6 usage sites (it was already being used correctly as a QueryInterface argument — a pure misnomer, not a bug, confirmed by checking what it's bound to before renaming per reviewer advice).
- `DirectPlay.h`'s struct-layout doc block had two more field-purpose errors that fall out of the same root cause: `+0x15E4` was documented "DP Address structure (0x28 bytes)" — it's a `DPCAPS` (10 dwords, `dwSize=0x28`), confirmed via disassembly of `DirectPlay_ConnectToSession`/0x45EA84-0x45EA90 (`CALL [ECX+0x38]` = real `IDirectPlay4A::GetCaps`, 2-push arity matching `GetCaps(LPDPCAPS,DWORD)` exactly); `+0x1584` was documented "DirectPlay DLL handle" — it's a transient COM object pointer from `Ordinal_1`, always dereferenced through a vtable and `Release`d, never passed to `FreeLibrary`.
- `network/DirectPlay.cpp`'s `dplay_GetCaps` helper used vtable offset `0x3C` (real slot 15, `GetGroupData`) instead of the correct `0x38` (slot 14, `GetCaps`) — a plain transcription slip, not an original-binary ambiguity: disassembly of its one real call site (`DirectPlay_ConnectToSession`/0x45EA90) shows `CALL [ECX+0x38]`, i.e. the original code already uses the *correct* offset; only this file's C++ reconstruction had the typo. Fixed.
- `IDirectDrawSurface7`/`IDirectDraw7*` doc-comment references across `graphics/DDRAW.h`, `native/DDRAW_GetSurfaceWidthHeight.c`, and `network/DirectPlay.cpp` corrected to `IDirectDrawSurface4`/`IDirectDraw4*`: `search_bytes` against loco.exe finds a contiguous GUID table (0x4785C8-0x478628) matching Microsoft's `ddraw.h` declaration order for `IID_IDirectDraw`/`IDirectDraw2`/`IDirectDraw4`/`IDirectDrawSurface`/`Surface2`/`Surface3`/`Surface4`, but the two 7-series GUIDs (`IID_IDirectDraw7`, `IID_IDirectDrawSurface7`) are absent from the entire binary — consistent with `NOTE-directx-sdk.md`'s independent DX6.0 determination (DirectDraw7 didn't exist until DX7, Sept 1999). The two `IDirectDrawSurface4` vtable helpers already in `network/DirectPlay.cpp` (`ddraw_surface_GetDC`/`ReleaseDC`, offsets `[17]`/`[26]`) were independently checked against the real `IDirectDrawSurface4` method order and are already correct — no fix needed there.
- `stubs/dplay.h`'s `DPSESSIONDESC2` stub had three fields that don't exist in Microsoft's real struct (`dwSession`, `lpszReserved`, `lpszPlayerName`) and put `lpszSessionName`/`lpszPassword` after `dwReserved1`/`dwReserved2` instead of before — computed to 0x5C bytes instead of the real/documented 0x50. Replaced with the real 14-field layout (byte-identical to the DX6 SDK's), using `LPSTR`/`...A`-suffixed field names instead of Microsoft's anonymous `LPWSTR`/`LPSTR` union, since loco.exe is confirmed (via the `IID_IDirectPlay4A` finding) to use the ANSI interface exclusively, and this header is reached from `native/*.c`. `network/DirectPlay.cpp`'s actual runtime code (`DirectPlay_SetSessionDesc`/0x45F090, `DirectPlay_EnumPlayers`/0x45FD80) already builds this structure correctly via raw `desc[N]` dword-indexed writes matching the *real* layout — confirmed via disassembly (`desc[12]`=`lpszSessionName`, `desc[13]`=`lpszPassword`, `desc[6..9]`=`guidApplication`) — so this was a stub-header/comment bug, not a functional one; also fixed a leftover misleading `/* dwSession? */` comment on the equivalent raw-offset write in `DirectPlay_HostSession`.
- `stubs/ddraw.h`'s file comment referenced the pre-migration `LOCO_SDL3` macro and `src/sdl3_shims/sdl3_ddraw.h` path, both anti-patterns per `AGENTS.md`/`CLAUDE.md` (exact guard is `#ifndef _WIN32`; no more `sdl3_shims/` split) that predated this session's edit but were fixed on sight while already touching the file.

**Investigated and confirmed, but deliberately NOT fixed (would change which real DirectPlay operation several functions perform, not just their name — needs a dedicated VALIDATED pass, tracked as its own remaining-work item above)**: regenerated the full real `IDirectPlay4`/`IDirectPlay4A` vtable slot table from the DX6 SDK's `DPLAY.H` (`awk`+`nl`, sanity-checked against the disassembly-confirmed `GetPlayerAddress`@0x48 entry) and checked every hardcoded offset in `network/DirectPlay.cpp`'s helper block against it, using each call site's real push-instruction arity (not decompiler pseudocode — one site, `DirectPlay_GetSessionDesc`/0x45EEC0's `EnumAddress` call, was shown via `disassemble_function` to push a literal `0` where Ghidra's decompiler had fabricated a stale-register `piVar5` as the 4th argument, so pseudocode-only argument counts are not trustworthy here) as the discriminator. Confirmed real mismatches: `dplay_Open` (labeled Open, offset `0x34` is really `EnumSessions` — its one real call site in `DirectPlay_SetSessionDesc`/0x45F090 pushes 5 args including a real callback function pointer at 0x45F2B0, matching `EnumSessions`'s 5-param signature exactly, not `Open`'s 2); `dplay_EnumPlayers` (labeled EnumPlayers, offset `0x60` is really `Open` — its real call site in `DirectPlay_EnumPlayers`/0x45FD80 pushes exactly 2 args, matching `Open(LPDPSESSIONDESC2,DWORD)`, not `EnumPlayers`'s 4); `dplay_GetSessionDesc` (labeled GetSessionDesc, offset `0x48` is really `GetPlayerAddress` — 3-push arity confirmed at its call site in `DirectPlay_GetSessionDesc`/0x45EEC0); `dpaddr2_EnumPlayers` on the (correctly renamed) Lobby3A interface (labeled EnumPlayers, offset `0x14` is really `EnumAddress` — 4-push arity confirmed, callback is `DirectPlay_CreatePlayer`/0x45FBD0, but `EnumAddress`'s real callback type is `LPDPENUMADDRESSCALLBACK` = `(REFGUID,DWORD,LPCVOID,LPVOID)`, not `DirectPlay_CreatePlayer`'s current `(const char*,uint32_t,const char*)` — its body needs re-decompiling against the real signature before anything downstream can be fixed safely); and `dplay_ClosePlayer` (offset `0xCC` is really `CancelMessage(DWORD,DWORD)`, arity-matches the caller's `(pid,flags)` exactly, name-only mismatch, already flagged safe-to-relabel in a code comment but not renamed since the calling function's semantics under the new name are unexamined). Net picture: `DirectPlay_SetSessionDesc` and `DirectPlay_EnumPlayers` appear to have functionally swapped names relative to what they actually invoke (EnumSessions-with-a-callback vs. a plain Open), which — combined with the pre-existing `docs/landmine-sweep-worklist.md` finding that "0x45F050 is actually `GetSessionDesc`" from the 2026-08-06 investigation above — suggests this whole address range's function names were assigned by guessing from surrounding context rather than checking the real DirectPlay vtable, the same landmine bug class as `[[vtable_byte_offset_misalignment]]` in memory, just manifesting as wrong-slot-number rather than wrong-entry-width. Added a full corrected slot-order comment block above the helper table in `network/DirectPlay.cpp` so a future session doesn't have to re-derive it. Also noted, not investigated further: `ui/UIPANEL_Surface.cpp` and `platform/sdl3_types.h` each define their own separate, differently-sized local `DDBLTFX` struct (3 and 18 fields respectively) alongside `stubs/ddraw.h`'s 2-field stub version — three definitions of the same DirectX struct name across the tree, worth a follow-up consolidation pass but out of scope here since none of the three are ODR-conflicting (each is scoped to its own TU/host-only path) and `stubs/ddraw.h`'s copy has zero real callers.

**Build/test verification**: `meson setup build && meson compile -C build` — clean, 189/189 targets including `tests/dplay_config_test` (which links `network/DirectPlay.cpp`). `meson test -C build dplay-config`: pass. Full `meson test -C build`: 22/30 pass; the 8 failures (`resource-archive`, `resource-manager-sdl3`, `sdl3-game-audio`, `mode2-menu-backdrop`, `mode2-multiplayer-menu`, `input-world`, `persistence-adapter`, GUI `integration`) are pre-existing sandbox/fixture issues unrelated to this session's 6 touched files (confirmed: `sdl3_game_audio_test` fails with `"could not open game assets"` — missing `lego-loco-unpacked/art-res` fixtures in this environment, not a code regression; `git diff --stat` confirms only `graphics/DDRAW.h`, `native/DDRAW_GetSurfaceWidthHeight.c`, `network/DirectPlay.{h,cpp}`, `stubs/{ddraw,dplay}.h` were touched, none of which the failing tests exercise).

**2026-08-08 (directx-typed-vtable-cleanup)**: Per the user's request ("we should not have direct v-table interaction in the C++ code, but rather call the proper functions... limited to DirectX related work"), removed every raw vtable-offset dispatch pattern from `network/DirectPlay.cpp` — the ~15-function helper block (`dplay_Release`, `dplay_Close`, `dplay_CreatePlayer`, `dplay_EnumSessions`, `dplay_GetCaps`, `dplay_GetMessageCount`, `dplay_GetPlayerAddress`, `dplay_Open`, `dplay_CancelMessage`, `com_QueryInterface`, `dplobby3a_Release`, `dplobby3a_EnumAddress`, `ddraw_surface_GetDC`, `ddraw_surface_ReleaseDC`) that called through `(*(void***)iface)[N/4]`-style raw pointer casts — replaced with real typed C++ COM interfaces in `stubs/dplay.h`: `IDirectPlay4A`/`IDirectPlayLobby3A`, declared as ordinary C++ abstract classes with virtual methods in the exact real vtable slot order (cross-checked against the genuine DX6 SDK `DPLAY.H`/`DPLOBBY.H`, the same source used for the previous session's offset-mismatch fixes). Every call site in `DirectPlay.cpp` now reads as ordinary method calls (`dplay->GetCaps(caps, 0)`, `lobby3a->EnumAddress(&callback, ...)`) instead of vtable-index arithmetic — exactly CLAUDE.md's "do not manually read or write VTBL_*... C++ manages vtables" rule, previously violated throughout this file. `DPCAPS`/`DPSESSIONDESC2`/`DPNAME` raw dword-indexed struct accesses (`desc[6]`, `desc[12]`) were likewise converted to real named-field access (`desc->guidApplication`, `desc->lpszSessionNameA`) using the already-corrected struct layouts from the prior session. GUID constants converted from raw `uint32_t[4]` arrays to real `GUID` struct literals. Removed a duplicate/conflicting local `extern "C"` declaration block: `network/DirectPlay.cpp` had its own wrong-typed (`int32_t`/`void*` instead of `HANDLE`/`BOOL`/`HMODULE`) declarations of `CreateFileA`, `PlaySoundA`, `GetSystemMetrics`, `wsprintfA`, `LoadStringA`, `MessageBoxA`, `CloseHandle`, `Sleep` that only became a compile error once `network/DirectPlay.h` started including `stubs/dplay.h` for the `GUID` type (which transitively pulls in `stubs/windows.h`, the project's one real declaration source for these) — removed all 8 local declarations, fixed the one live call site (`DirectPlay_GetConnectionCaps`'s `CreateFileA`) to use `HANDLE`/`INVALID_HANDLE_VALUE` instead of `int32_t`/`-1`; the other 7 had zero or dead call sites in this file. Added `#ifndef` guards around 3 macros in `stubs/windows.h` (`INVALID_HANDLE_VALUE`, `HKEY_CURRENT_USER`, `HKEY_LOCAL_MACHINE`) that now collide (harmlessly, but with a warning) with `stubs/compat.h`'s pre-existing versions once `windows.h` is reachable from this TU.

**First attempt (reverted) — wiring the real mingw-w64 headers on `_WIN32`**: initially tried making `stubs/dplay.h`'s new interfaces conditional — `#include <dplay.h>`/`<dplobby.h>` directly under `#ifdef _WIN32` (matching CLAUDE.md's general preference for real Windows headers on the original path), falling back to the typed adapter only on host. This broke the MinGW typecheck build (`ninja -C build-mingw`): the Wine-derived `i686-w64-mingw32-gcc` system headers' `dplobby.h` uses `DEFINE_GUID(...)` throughout, which requires `<ole2.h>`'s COM/OLE preamble (per the real header's own top-of-file comment) — adding `#include <windows.h>` alone did not resolve it, and a first attempt at deeper investigation was inconclusive. **Confirmed via `git stash`-isolated pre-session baseline that this file compiled clean under mingw before this session started** — i.e. this was a regression from the `_WIN32`-conditional experiment, not a pre-existing gap. Reverted to the same typed-adapter interface unconditionally on both `_WIN32` and host, since it is SDK-verified, compiles clean on both toolchains, and fully satisfies the actual ask (no raw vtable interaction) without depending on unsolved COM-header plumbing. **Tracked as its own open item below** rather than reattempted here — this needs a dedicated investigation, likely starting from whether the project's global forced `-include stubs/compat.h` (which every TU gets via the build's `cpp_args`) pre-empts the include order `<ole2.h>`-based headers expect.

**Verification**: `meson compile -C build` clean (only pre-existing unrelated warnings in `game/PlayerConfig.cpp`, `ui/GameWindow.cpp`, `ui/UIPANEL_Draw.cpp`, and a pre-existing `DirectPlay_Close((int32_t)self)` narrowing-cast warning in this file, none touched by this pass). `meson test -C build`: 22/30, identical pre-existing 8 failures (resource-archive, resource-manager-sdl3, sdl3-game-audio, mode2-menu-backdrop, mode2-multiplayer-menu, input-world, persistence-adapter, GUI integration). `ninja -C build-mingw liblego_loco_typecheck.a.p/network_DirectPlay.cpp.obj`: compiles clean (confirmed both before this pass, via stash, and after). Full `ninja -C build-mingw -k 0`: 49 other pre-existing failures unrelated to DirectPlay (`ui/EditWindow.cpp`'s `loco::assets` namespace gaps, `game/Building.h`'s `-Werror=invalid-offsetof` on a non-standard-layout type, etc.) — none newly introduced; `graphics_DDRAW.cpp.obj`'s failure was independently confirmed to be the same pre-existing `Building.h` offsetof issue, unrelated to any file touched this session. `grep` confirms zero remaining `(*(void***)`/`(**(code **)`/`(**(int **)` raw vtable patterns and zero remaining old helper names (`dplay_Release(`, `com_QueryInterface(`, `dplobby3a_*(`, `ddraw_surface_*(`) anywhere in `network/DirectPlay.cpp`.
**Note (typed-vtable-cleanup, below): the fixed-address `.rdata` GUID reads at 0x479158-0x479164 that the entry directly below documents as an open, unfixed landmine (in `DirectPlay_SetSessionDesc`/`DirectPlay_OpenSession`) are gone as of that later pass — both functions now build `guidApplication` via `desc->guidApplication = GUID_SessionDesc` (a real `GUID` struct constant), not a raw address dereference.**

**2026-08-08 (directplay-cpp-cast-cluster)**: Fixed `network/DirectPlay.cpp`'s STRICT=2 diagnostics (310 → 0: 297 `old-style-cast`, 13 `zero-as-null-pointer-constant`; one pre-existing `cast-qual` in `DirectPlay_GetConnectionCaps` fixed by casting the read-only `g_device_path_null` view to `const uint32_t*` instead of discarding `const`). Went in planning to attempt this session's primary strategy — split the bookkeeping (session/player/group/message linked lists) into an `#ifdef _WIN32`-faithful path vs. a `#ifndef _WIN32` native-pointer host path, per CLAUDE.md's "exact x86 layout is a non-goal for host-only builds" — but an `advisor()` review before touching any code, then a direct `nm` census of the actual worktree build (not the shared checkout — see below), falsified the premise before any risky edit was made, so **no split/retype was attempted; every semantic behavior is unchanged.** (One structural, behavior-identical simplification did land alongside the pure respells: the ~14 duplicated `dplay_*`/`ddraw_surface_*` vtable-slot helpers each did their own `((RETTYPE (*)(ARGS))(*(void***)iface)[N])(...)` cast pair — collapsed into one `vtable_slot<Fn>(iface, index)` template used by all of them, same index/reinterpret/call per site, just deduplicated.) Two new, concrete mechanisms found (Ghidra + `nm`-verified, going beyond the 2026-08-06 investigation's "needs a pointer-width audit" framing):
1. **Retyping `int32_t session` → a pointer is not semantically inert — it silently rebinds a currently-dangling caller.** Ghidra confirms all four `int32_t session`-typed functions (`DirectPlay_Close` 0x45FC30, `DirectPlay_DestroyPeer` 0x45E5A0, `DirectPlay_EnumConnections` 0x45EAB0, `DirectPlay_GetSessionDesc` 0x45EEC0) are genuinely `__fastcall` with the parameter passed in ECX used purely as a base address (never arithmetic) — i.e. `session` really is `self`, matching the `void* self` convention the other DirectPlay functions already use. But C++ name mangling encodes the parameter type: `nm` on this worktree's own `build/lego_loco.p/*.o` shows `network/DirectPlay.cpp` defines `_Z16DirectPlay_Closei` (`int`) while `town/Town.cpp:2899` independently declares (and calls) `void DirectPlay_Close(void* peer)`, emitting an undefined reference to `_Z16DirectPlay_ClosePv` — currently satisfied by nothing at all, tolerated only because `meson.build`'s main-binary link uses `-Wl,--unresolved-symbols=ignore-all` (an intentionally-deferred LINK-001 remainder, not this file's problem to fix). Retyping `session` to `void*` would make `DirectPlay.cpp`'s mangled name exactly `_Z16DirectPlay_ClosePv` — silently binding `Town.cpp`'s call into the *real* implementation, which still round-trips every player/group-list node pointer through `int32_t`/`uintptr_t` internally. That's a reachability change smuggled inside what would look like a type-cleanup commit, on the exact file the prior session already got burned by.
2. **The connection-list node layout has an external consumer outside this file.** `DirectPlay_EnumConnections`'s return value is walked in `game/Train_network.cpp:284` as `uint32_t* item` (`item[0]`/`item[1]`) — so even the one list this file both creates (`operator_new(8)`, 2×`int32_t` fields) and destroys entirely within itself isn't safe to redefine with a native-pointer `next` field unilaterally; it's dormant today only because that call site currently binds to `network/sdl3_directplay_train_bridge.cpp`'s no-op overload (`_Z26DirectPlay_EnumConnectionsPv`, distinct mangled name from the real `_Z26DirectPlay_EnumConnectionsi`) — the same mangled-name-coincidence risk as above, not a structural one.

Also found and preserved-as-documented (not fixed — out of scope for a cast-respell, and dead today): `DirectPlay_SetSessionDesc`/`DirectPlay_EnumPlayers` both dereference fixed absolute addresses (`0x479158`/`0x47915C`/`0x479160`/`0x479164` — the original binary's `.rdata` GUID bytes) directly and unguarded by `_WIN32`; on host this is a read of unmapped memory if ever reached, a distinct defect from (but same shape as) the pointer-truncation landmine. Both functions are call-0/unreachable today via the same linkage chain as above.

**A worktree-hygiene near-miss worth recording**: early in this session, several `cd`/build/read commands were accidentally run against the shared main checkout (`lego-loco-rev-eng/`) instead of this session's isolated worktree, before the sandbox's git-command guard caught it on an unrelated `git status` call. No edits or commits were made there (read-only `ninja`/`nm`/`Read` only, against already-existing build directories); recovered by re-verifying `pwd`/`git rev-parse --show-toplevel`/`git branch --show-current` and redoing all substantive work (the STRICT=2 census, the fix itself, all builds/tests) from the correct worktree path from that point on. Separately, this fresh worktree has no `lego-loco-unpacked/` (gitignored, not replicated by `git worktree`) — symlinked it from the shared checkout (`ln -s .../lego-loco-rev-eng/lego-loco-unpacked lego-loco-unpacked`) to get real test assets; without it, 8/30 tests false-fail on missing files (matches the false-negative note in row 46 of `docs/landmine-sweep-worklist.md`).

**Verification**: `ninja -C build-strict2` targeted grep for `network/DirectPlay.cpp` — clean (was 310, now 0; confirmed via a fresh `-Dstrict=2` configure in this worktree, not carried over from the shared checkout). `ninja -C build`: succeeds. `meson test -C build`: 30/30 (after the symlink fix above). `meson test -C build --suite integration` run 3 times independently (given this file's hang-regression history): OK all three times (~47-52s each). `call 0` count (`objdump -d build/lego_loco | grep -cE "call\s+0 "`): **272, unchanged** — expected, since no signature, symbol, or control-flow change was made, only cast syntax. `ninja -C build-mingw -k 0` (fresh `meson setup build-mingw --cross-file cross/mingw32-typecheck.txt` in this worktree): both `network/DirectPlay.cpp` and `network/sdl3_directplay_train_bridge.cpp` compile with no associated errors in the typecheck build. `network/DirectPlay.h` needed no changes — it has no cast expressions, only declarations/comments.

**Net assessment of this session's primary strategy**: the host/`_WIN32` split insight from CLAUDE.md's "Host deviations and SDL3" section is still directionally correct in principle (x86 layout parity genuinely isn't required for host-only bookkeeping), but it doesn't unblock *this* cluster on its own — the actual blocker isn't the struct layout, it's that several call sites across `Train_network.cpp`/`Town.cpp`/`shared/link_stubs.cpp` already have their own independently-wrong, mutually-inconsistent declarations of these same symbol names, load-bearing via C++ mangled-name coincidence rather than any header contract. Fixing the pointer-truncation bug for real needs those call sites reconciled *first* (a cross-file linkage audit, same conclusion as 2026-08-06, now with the specific mangled-name/`nm`-verified mechanism instead of an abstract "pointer-width audit"). See `docs/landmine-sweep-worklist.md`'s DirectPlay_* section for the updated writeup. Commit: cast-only fix to `network/DirectPlay.cpp`, no header change, no other files touched.
**2026-08-08 (native-misc-cluster / tilemap_init-hijack)**: Fixed the `native/resmgr_screensaver.c` + `native/DDRAW_DimSurfaceRect.c` + `native/tilemap_init.c` STRICT=2 cluster (41 old-style-cast sites originally scoped) — but `tilemap_init.c` turned out not to need cast fixes at all; it needed deleting. **`native/tilemap_init.c` was a duplicate implementation of the already-`Status: INTEGRATED` `TileMap::Init` (`world/tilemap.cpp`, 0x454E60 — the exact same address this file's own header comment claimed) that was silently *winning* the link over the real method**: its free-function `TileMap_Init(TileMap*, char)` was a **strong** symbol, while `world/tilemap.h`'s intended dispatch wrapper (`inline void TileMap_Init(TileMap* tm, char m){ tm->Init(m); }`) only ever emits a **weak** one — so every caller of the header's wrapper was actually running the standalone duplicate the whole time, not `TileMap::Init`. Confirmed via `nm`: `native_tilemap_init.c.o` had `T _Z12TileMap_InitP7TileMapc`, `core_GameLoop.cpp.o` had the same symbol `W` (weak). Deleting the duplicate lets the weak inline wrapper win by default (verified post-fix: `nm build/lego_loco` shows exactly one `TileMap4Init` symbol). Values computed by the two implementations were numerically equivalent (cross-checked field-by-field against `world/tilemap.h`'s documented offsets — the duplicate's field *names* were locally wrong, e.g. its `center_x` at +0x2c is really `viewport_center_x`, but the values landing at each real offset matched), so this was a live landmine, not a live behavior bug — except for one real regression it caused: `ui/EditWindow.cpp` had its *own*, independently wrong, `extern void** g_tilemap;` (address 0x4855D0, zero xrefs anywhere in the binary — confirmed fabricated) and a bogus `TileMap_Init(void**, byte)` prototype (address 0x458380, which Ghidra resolves to the *middle* of an unrelated function, `Entity_GetSubObjectPosition`) that mismatched the real signature and silently bound to a `TileMap_Init(void**, unsigned char)` no-op stub in `shared/defsym_stubs.cpp` — so `EditWindow`'s single-player/multiplayer mode-transition calls to re-init the tilemap were doing nothing at all, on host, until this fix. Ghidra's decompilation of `EditWindow_OnPlayerNameChanged` (0x422660) confirms both calls really target the canonical singleton (`TileMap_Init(&g_tilemap, ...)` against 0x4AAD08, 120+ xrefs) — fixed by forward-declaring `TileMap`/`g_tilemap`/`TileMap_Init` narrowly in `EditWindow.cpp` rather than `#include`-ing `world/tilemap.h` directly, which surfaced an unrelated pre-existing linkage landmine of its own (that header's `SetRect`/`InvalidateRect`/etc. Win32-API redeclarations are missing `extern "C"` in several spots — `IntersectRect`/`UnionRect` right above them already have it — so combining it with `graphics/sdl3_window.h` in one TU, and separately with `world/scriptengine.cpp`'s and `tests/persistence_fixtures.h`'s own already-`extern "C"`-but-`void*`-typed local redeclarations of the same names, is a pre-existing multi-file mess; deferred, flagged here and in `docs/landmine-sweep-worklist.md` rather than expanded into a second cluster this session). Removed the three now-fully-dead `TileMap_Init` no-op overloads (`shared/defsym_stubs.cpp` x2, `shared/stubs_impl.cpp` x1) after confirming zero remaining callers via grep and `nm`. **`native/DDRAW_DimSurfaceRect.c`**: rewrote to lock/unlock through the typed `IDirectDrawSurface4` interface (mirroring `town/TownTiles.cpp`'s `Town_CheckOccupiedEx`, which locks/reads this identical global surface state with the identical `Lock(nullptr, &desc, 0, nullptr)`/`Unlock(nullptr)` call shape — confirmed via Ghidra disassembly of both functions' argument-push sequences) instead of the old raw `(*(void***)g_primary_surface)[0x64]` vtable index, which was wrong on three independent counts, all Ghidra-confirmed: (1) that index math treats a byte offset (0x64, confirmed via `CALL dword ptr [ECX+0x64]`) as a `void**` array index, i.e. byte offset 0x190 — 4x past the real Lock slot; (2) `g_primary_surface` was declared at 0x4FF0D8 (zero xrefs anywhere in the binary; every other one of ~20 files in the tree, plus this function's own disassembly, uses 0x4FD3C4, 128 xrefs); (3) `g_surface_bshift` was declared at 0x48527A (zero xrefs) instead of the real 0x485280 (referenced by this function's own AND-mask instruction and every `Town_DrawTiles16bpp_*` variant). None of this mattered functionally on host regardless, since host's `IDirectDrawSurface4` (`graphics/sdl3_ddraw.h`) is an ordinary SDL3-backed C++ class, not the original COM vtable layout — but it's exactly the vtable-byte-offset-misalignment bug class this sweep exists to catch, so fixed properly rather than left as inert-but-wrong. The pixel-dimming arithmetic itself (`pixel = (pixel>>1) & bshift`, pitch-halving for 16bpp) is preserved bit-for-bit — verified against a fresh Ghidra disassembly/decompile of 0x401540 and spot-checked in the compiled object's own disassembly (`shr $1`/`and $0xffff`). Also fixed two more bogus-address annotations surfaced while touching `game/Panel.h`/`.cpp` (this function's only callers): both declared it at 0x469A50 (zero xrefs) instead of 0x401540, and `Panel.h` additionally declared it `void`-returning against the real (always-`1`) `int` return — corrected; no caller reads the return value either way, so this is a documentation-only fix. **`native/resmgr_screensaver.c`**: converted the raw dword-at-a-time filename-copy loops to `strcpy` (documented equivalence: destination is either a stack buffer or freshly-`operator_new`'d memory, and the only difference is up to 3 extra never-read padding bytes past the NUL) and the linked-list's raw `node+0x504` offset arithmetic to a named `ScreenSaverNode{ char filename[0x504]; ScreenSaverNode* next; }` struct sized via `sizeof()` rather than the hardcoded `0x508` (per CLAUDE.md's host-layout-parity non-goal). **Found and fixed a real out-of-bounds heap write**: the pre-existing version of this file zeroed three dwords (`node+0x504`, `+0x508`, `+0x50C`) on every node allocation; Ghidra's disassembly of 0x448390 shows only *one* such store (`node+0x504`, the actual next-pointer field, written twice — once defensively before the filename copy, once to the real previous-head value after) — the other two offsets are one and two dwords *past* the 0x508-byte `operator_new` allocation, an 8-byte heap overflow on every enumerated file, never present in the real binary. The named-struct rewrite makes this class of bug structurally impossible. Also corrected the calling convention: both functions are `__stdcall` (Ghidra: `RET 4`, one callee-cleaned stack dword, confirmed via careful stack-offset accounting through a mid-function `PUSH EBX` that shifts later frame-relative reads), not the `__fastcall` this file and `__cdecl` `resources/ResourceManager.h` previously (and independently) claimed; both also read/forward ECX at entry but never actually consume it — a vestigial, functionally-dead passthrough, documented rather than removed since it doesn't affect host behavior. `RESMGR_EnumScreenSavers` has no caller anywhere in the tree outside this file (grepped tree-wide) — made `static`, removed its now-stale declaration from `ResourceManager.h`. **Deferred, not fixed**: `shared/link_stubs.cpp` still carries an inert, unreachable `extern "C" void RESMGR_SelectScreensaver(char*){}` no-op — dead because every real caller uses C++ (mangled) linkage, confirmed via `nm`, but a "never silent no-op" policy violation in principle; left alone since it's outside this cluster's 3 files and genuinely unreachable, not touched to avoid further scope creep. **Verification**: `ninja -C build-strict2` shows 0 errors for all 3 original target files (`tilemap_init.c` no longer exists). `ninja -C build`, `meson test -C build`: 30/30 including `--suite integration` (needed `lego-loco-unpacked/` symlinked into this session's worktree — see the standing false-negative note above). `call 0`: **272, unchanged** (`objdump -d build/lego_loco | grep -cE "call\s+0 "`) — expected, since the two real fixes (tilemap hijack, screensaver linkage) were silent-wrong-stub/dead-duplicate bugs, not call-0 sites. `ninja -C build-mingw -k 0` diffed before/after for both touched headers (`game/Panel.h`, `resources/ResourceManager.h`): the 5 mingw compile failures touching files that include them (`game/Panel.cpp`, `core/InitMode1.cpp`, `ui/EditWindow.cpp`, plus 2 unrelated) are all pre-existing and textually unrelated to these edits (missing `ushort`/`uint` typedefs, a missing `loco::host` namespace, `Building.h`'s `offsetof`-on-non-standard-layout issue) — confirmed by inspecting each error message directly; native/*.c files are not part of the mingw typecheck source list at all, so the `resmgr_screensaver.c`/`DDRAW_DimSurfaceRect.c` rewrites aren't typechecked there either way.
**2026-08-08 (netman-networkui-cast-cluster)**: Fixed `native/NETMAN_NetworkUI.c`'s 186-site STRICT=2 old-style-cast cluster. **Receiver class**: `NameEntryPanel` (`ui/NameEntryPanel.h`, extends `UI_WindowBase`) — already canonical; confirmed via raw vtable bytes at 0x4781D0 (read with Ghidra's `read_bytes`) that `NETMAN_LeaveSession`/`NETMAN_JoinSession`/`NETMAN_UpdateSessionInfo`/`NETMAN_SetSessionInfo` occupy slots [1]/[2]/[8]/[14] — `hide()`/`show()`/`on_update()`/`on_lbutton_down()` — three of which (`hide`, `on_update`, plus `on_create`/`window_proc`/`on_timer` at [7]/[11]/[12]) `NameEntryPanel.h`'s vtable table had wrongly documented as inherited defaults; corrected. All 7 functions converted from `int32_t`/`void*` params to `NameEntryPanel*`, converting every raw offset to a named field (adding `paintReadyFlag` +0x148, `blitDestWidth`/`blitDestHeight` +0x154/+0x158, `editControlRect` +0x15C, `panelClickRect` +0x19C, `sessionNameEditHwnd` +0x1D8, `originalEditWndProc` +0x1DC, `supportsTwoPlayerMode`/`supportsFourPlayerMode` (renamed from `field_1E0`/`field_1E1`) to `ui/NameEntryPanel.h`, propagating the two renames into `ui/EditWindow.cpp`'s direct field accesses and `ui/NameEntryPanel.cpp`'s `init()`). Literal vtable-byte-offset dispatch through `NameEntryPanel`'s own vtable (`on_create()`/`set_mode()`/`set_render_surface()`) converted to real typed virtual calls. **Bugs found and fixed** (all Ghidra-confirmed against the real decompile, not guessed): (1) `NETMAN_JoinSession`'s Ghidra-typed `int*` `this` means its `param_1[N]` accesses are ×4-scaled — `+0x52`/`+0x18`/`+0x19`/`+0x50`/`+0x3C` were mistranscribed as byte offsets instead of the real `+0x148`/`+0x60`/`+0x64`/`+0x140`/`+0xF0` (the `+0xF0` one independently corroborated by `NameEntryPanel.h`'s own pre-existing `textBuffer` doc comment, which already cited this exact call correctly while the `.c` file itself used `+0x3C`). (2) `NETMAN_SetSessionInfo` is `NameEntryPanel::on_lbutton_down` (vtable slot [14]) but was declared taking only `this`, with click coordinates hardcoded to `x=0,y=0` and a comment about the real coords needing "register magic" that was never implemented — every click hit-tested against the origin, so the panel's buttons could never actually be clicked. Fixed: real signature is `(HWND, UINT, WPARAM, LPARAM)` matching `UI_WindowBase::on_lbutton_down` exactly; `lParam`'s low/high words give x/y (`lParam & 0xFFFF`, `lParam >> 16`, matching Ghidra's `in_stack_00000010 >> 0x10` unsigned shift). (3) `DPlayManager_RenderConnectionPanel` — a completely different, wrongly-named, 0-arg stub (`shared/defsym_stubs.cpp`) that `NETMAN_JoinSession` called instead of the real `RenderConnectionPanel(NameEntryPanel*)` (confirmed both symbols coexist via `nm -C`); fixed the call, removed the dead stub. (4) `NETMAN_CreateSession` — `ui/EditWindow.cpp`'s own separate `void*`-typed declaration (address comment also wrong: cited 0x43C8C0, actually 0x43CC50-ish unrelated code; Ghidra confirms the real call target is 0x4419C0) bound to a mismatched-signature no-op instead of the real `int32_t`-turned-`NameEntryPanel*` function — this one is *live* (`EditWindow::show()`, called by every GUI test). Fixing it exposed a real, pre-existing, previously-inert bug: `_g_netman_data` (`shared/defsym_stubs.cpp:159`, `void* = nullptr`) is never assigned anywhere in this codebase's host build (`GameLoop_Setup`'s `GameConfig` construction, 0x406C5A, isn't ported), so walking its provider list unconditionally null-derefs. **Empirically verified**: temporarily disabled the null-guard, rebuilt, ran the `opens_main_menu` GUI integration test — reproduced an immediate crash right after the "EditWindow::show: create session" trace line, confirmed necessary, restored. Guarded with `#ifndef _WIN32` (on real Windows this pointer is always valid by the time any of these panel functions run — `GameLoop_Setup` executes at startup, long before the multiplayer lobby can open — so the guard is a host-initialization-gap deviation, not original behavior) in every function here that dereferences it. (5) `ResourceManager_GetById`'s declared `int32_t` return type silently truncated the real `void*` return on store (every other in-tree caller already uses `void*`). (6) `NETMAN_SendPacket` declared inside `extern "C"` with a `void*` param instead of C++-linkage `uint8_t*` (real def: `native/NETMAN_SessionSettings.c`) — fixed for this file's two call sites only (both inside the null-guarded block, so no live-call-site risk); `ui/EditWindow.cpp`'s own separate, *unguarded*, still-mismatched copy (real file I/O to NetSettings.dat) deliberately left alone and documented in `docs/landmine-sweep-worklist.md` rather than fixed blind. (7) This file's own top-of-file offset table had mislabeled `+0x08` as "HWND (parent)" — it's `UI_WindowBase::hWnd` (this window's *own* HWND, per `ui/UI_WindowBase.h`), not `hWndParent` (+0x0C, unused here); "parent" described its role as parent-of-the-child-EDIT-control, not its identity. Also fixed: `NETMAN_JoinSession`'s and `NETMAN_LeaveSession`'s resource-object vtable-slot[1]/[2] dispatch had a missing dereference (indexed into the object, not its vtable) and used byte offsets instead of pointer-sized slot indices (misindexes this host's 8-byte vtable entries); rewritten using `ui/AboutDialog.cpp`'s already-established, evidence-backed idiom for the same still-unmodeled `ResourceManager_GetById`-sourced object class. `PostMessageA`'s two calls had mislabeled Win32 messages (`0x30`/`0xC5` commented as `EM_SETLIMITTEXT`/`EM_SETMARGINS`; really `WM_SETFONT`/`EM_LIMITTEXT` — corroborated by `ui/EditWindow.cpp`'s parallel, already-correct call and `network/NetworkPlayerList.cpp`'s canonical `g_font_small` global, now reused here instead of a raw `*(uint32_t*)0x4855F8` dereference of what turned out to be the wrong interpretation of that address). The hardcoded `"EDIT"` window-class literal-address read (`(const char*)0x47E464`) was replaced with the literal string, matching `native/NETMAN_SessionSettings.c`'s established `FMT_FILE_PATH`/`STR_NET_SETTINGS` precedent for this exact anti-pattern. The EDIT control's subclass WndProc (0x4417E0) has no implementation anywhere in the tree (was a bare, unresolvable function-pointer address literal) — replaced with a loud `fprintf`+`assert` deferred stub (`ui/UI_ChildWindow.cpp` precedent); confirmed unreachable today (`NETMAN_EnumerateSessions` itself has zero external callers). **Deferred, not fixed this session** (documented in `docs/landmine-sweep-worklist.md`): `UIPANEL_EndPaintEx`'s extern-"C"-vs-real-C++-linkage mismatch in this file (tracked as its own follow-up commit, per the file's pre-existing note that the cluster spans 8 files); `sub_441360`/`sub_442150`/`sub_4423D0` (NameEntryPanel's `on_create`/`window_proc`/`on_timer` overrides) remain undecompiled; `_g_netman_data` vs. `_g_netman_state` (same 0x4FD3A8 singleton, two entirely separate, differently-initialized host globals — the latter *is* assigned via `shared/stubs_impl.cpp`'s `DPlayConfig::binary_data()`, the former never is) is a real architectural duplication, out of scope to unify here. Free-function→typed-virtual-override conversion (making these 7 functions real `NameEntryPanel` vtable overrides) also deferred — tracked as a follow-up; would ripple into how the vtable itself is constructed. **Verification**: `ninja -C build-strict2` targeted grep — 0 old-style-cast/missing-declarations errors remain for this file (was 186+6). `ninja -C build`, `meson test -C build`: 30/30 including `--suite integration` (multiplayer lobby is directly exercised; the null-guard fix was verified against a live crash repro, not just a clean exit code). `call 0` count: 271 (down from the 272 baseline — the `RenderConnectionPanel`/`NETMAN_CreateSession` linkage fixes resolved at least one). mingw typecheck diff (`ui/NameEntryPanel.h`/`.cpp`, `ui/ButtonSprite.h`, `ui/EditWindow.cpp`, `network/Netman.h`, `native/NETMAN_NetworkUI.c`) against an isolated `git stash` baseline at the same commit: identical error signatures/counts (only line-number churn from added comments) — the `HWND`/renamed-field retypes introduce no new mingw failures.

**2026-08-09 (strict2-cleanup-complete)**: Finished the STRICT=2 cleanup started 2026-08-08 — `meson setup build-strict2 -Dstrict=2 && ninja -C build-strict2` now compiles clean tree-wide (0 errors of any kind), down from the 5195-diagnostic 2026-08-08 baseline. Real unique-site counts were smaller than the raw census suggested (per-TU header-inclusion repetition inflated `missing-declarations` from 755 real sites to 874 raw, and `-Werror=effc++` from 33 to 409) — deduping by `file:line:col` before sizing work would have saved a batch. Fixed in five commits: (1) `network/vendor/mjansson_mdns/mdns.h` (third-party, quote-included so the pkg-config `include_type: 'system'` treatment from the previous session doesn't reach it — `#pragma GCC system_header` instead) + `world/tilemap.cpp`'s one `zero-as-null-pointer-constant` site. (2) `missing-declarations` (755 sites, `shared/{defsym_stubs,link_stubs,stubs_impl,core_stubs}.cpp` + `tests/persistence_fixtures.h` + 22 scattered files) via a script generating a file-scope prototype above each definition, copying its own signature verbatim — never adopts another file's declaration, so it cannot introduce a call-0 regression; one automation bug (doesn't understand `#ifdef`/`#else` signature splits, `network/PostBagCleanup.cpp`) caught by rebuild and fixed by hand. (3) `tests/persistence_fixtures.h`'s own `TileMap`/`Netman` test-fixture ctors got explicit member-init lists (behavior-neutral — these are host-only stand-ins that already `memset(0)`, not the real decompiled ctors). (4) `-Weffc++` "has pointer members, no copy-ctor" (14 classes: `DDRAW_Building`, `CGWND`, `InputMgr`, `Entity`, `Game`, `ResourceGameObject`, `Vehicle`, `Building`, `RESDATA_GameVehicle`, `GameVehicle`, `Netman`, `TileMap`, `IDirectDrawSurface4`, `IDirectDraw4`, test-local `FakeAvahiService`) — grepped for actual copy-construction before deleting each; `Entity` is genuinely copy-constructed (`ui/UI_ListBox.cpp`/`UI_ScrollBar.cpp` placement-new-copy an Entity/UIEntity to install the compiler vtable over a raw byte snapshot, 0x424040/0x424550 — real behavior, caught by the build rather than the grep sweep), so its copy ctor is `= default` (behavior-neutral) instead of deleted; every other class gets both deleted. (5) `-Weffc++` member-init-list in host-only/test-only headers (`platform/sdl3_types.h`, `network/{sdl3_net_protocol,sdl3_net_transport,network_discovery}.h`, `resources/{resource_archive,pe_string_table}.h`, `input/PersistenceAdapter.h`, `tests/*`) — plain in-class default member initializers, safe since none are decompiled original-game classes. **Verification, every commit**: `ninja -C build-strict2 -k0` clean for the touched scope; `ninja -C build`; `meson test -C build` 30/30 including `--suite integration`; `call 0` count (`objdump -d build/lego_loco | grep -cE "call\s+0 "`) unchanged at 266 throughout; `ninja -C build-mingw -k0` error count unchanged 496→496 (stashed-baseline diff) for every commit touching a header.
**2026-08-09 (mode3-render-freeze-revalidation)**: Revalidated `BUG-mode-3-render-freeze.md` against current HEAD (`185d54b`), ~79 commits after its original 2026-08-06 diagnosis at `4eabbbf`. Also found and fixed a housekeeping problem: this bug's own documentation (the `mode3-input-wiring-gap` entry above, and the bug-report file itself) was stuck mid-`git stash pop` — `PROGRESS.md` had literal unresolved `<<<<<<<`/`=======`/`>>>>>>>` conflict markers on disk, and `HEAD:PROGRESS.md` didn't mention this bug at all. Resolved by splicing the stashed entry into its correct chronological position (immediately after the `render-path-landmine-sweep` entry it cross-references as "immediately above") and dropping the stash.

Root-cause revalidation: **every substantive claim in `BUG-mode-3-render-freeze.md` still holds.** `active_host_menu()` (`core/CGWND_sdl3.cpp:35-38`) still gates all SDL input on `g_game_mode == 2`; no mode-3/9 counterpart dispatch exists anywhere in the tree; `mouse_move_flag`/`right_click_flag` are still never assigned `1` by any code path (repo-wide grep); `left_click_flag = 1` still has exactly the one non-external-input site at `core/Game.cpp:882`; `Game::Update()`'s `has_event` gate is unchanged at `core/Game.cpp:427`. Line-number citations drifted slightly from intervening unrelated commits (STRICT=2 cast cleanup, an allocation-size fix) and were refreshed in the bug report; none of those commits touched the implicated control flow. The regression-test gap is also still real: `tests/integration/test_game_gui.py`'s only mode-3 test (`test_singleplayer_accept_reaches_mode3_without_crashing`) does all its clicking before mode 3 is entered and asserts nothing about input once inside it; `GameSession` (`tests/integration/gui_sandbox.py`) has no mouse-move/hover helper at all.

One piece of supporting evidence changed: of the four `call 0` landmines the bug report names as "likely connected" (`Town::on_mouse_move`, `Cursor::handle_toolbar_hover`, `EditWindow::netPanelWndProc`, `HelpWnd::handle_mouse_move`), the landmine-sweep commits since diagnosis (`dab8fce`/`adbca85` Cursor family, `2ffda7b` Town_Draw*/TileCache cluster, the EditWindow/UIPANEL-family fixes) have fully implemented the first three — they're no longer landmines. `HelpWnd::handle_mouse_move` remains landmined (4 `call 0` sites), now root-caused precisely: `ui/HelpWnd.h` declares a same-signature `void set_mode(void* countPtr, void* dataPtr, int modeA, int modeB)` overload (documented as vtable[3], "inherited from GameWindow") that shadows the real inherited `GameWindow::set_mode(...)` via `using GameWindow::set_mode;`; `ui/HelpWnd_stubs.cpp` explicitly claims "No separate `HelpWnd::set_mode` stub is needed," but `handle_mouse_move` calls exactly this declared-but-never-defined overload. Doesn't change the bug's primary root cause or suggested fix — the mode-3 input-wiring gap remains the actual blocker — but corrects the report's supporting evidence, and is worth folding into the same pass if `HelpWnd::handle_mouse_move` turns out to sit on the real dispatch path. Status unchanged: root cause found, not yet fixed — implementing it needs assembly verification of the original modes-3/9 `WM_MOUSEMOVE`/`WM_LBUTTONDOWN` dispatch first, per this project's evidence-first convention, and is tracked as its own follow-up (see "Remaining work" above).

**2026-08-09 (mode3-render-freeze-fix)**: Implemented the fix for `BUG-mode-3-render-freeze.md`. Decompiled `MainWndProc` (0x4618C0) in Ghidra for the first time (never transcribed anywhere in this tree — `core/CGWND.cpp`'s `_WIN32` path points a raw `WNDCLASS.lpfnWndProc` directly at that address) to verify the original modes-3/9 `WM_MOUSEMOVE`/`WM_LBUTTONDOWN`/`WM_LBUTTONUP`/`WM_RBUTTONDOWN`/`WM_RBUTTONUP` dispatch, resolving the "needs assembly verification" blocker from the two entries above. Confirmed by disassembly that these write directly to fixed addresses `g_game (0x4854C8) + field-offset` (e.g. `MOV byte ptr [0x48556C],1` for `left_click_flag`) — decisive proof `Game` is statically addressed in the original, not accessed through a runtime pointer. `core/CGWND_sdl3.cpp` gained `active_host_game()` + `host_pack_game_lparam()`, wiring `SDL_EVENT_MOUSE_MOTION`/`SDL_EVENT_MOUSE_BUTTON_DOWN`/`SDL_EVENT_MOUSE_BUTTON_UP` to reproduce those five writes for modes 3/9, each cited to its source address. New `town_input_dispatched` host_test event (`stubs/host_test_events.h`/`.cpp`) gives direct observability that SDL input reached `Game`. New regression test `test_singleplayer_mode3_mouse_input_reaches_game` (`tests/integration/test_game_gui.py`, plus a new `GameSession.move_logical()` helper in `gui_sandbox.py`) drives a real mouse move through the Wayland sandbox in mode 3 and asserts the dispatch event fires — closing the "12/12 integration gate has no such test" gap from the original diagnosis.

Verified live via `coredumpctl`/GDB: `Game::UpdateInputState()` — previously confirmed unreachable ("zero hits" in the original GDB session) — now executes on every real mouse move in mode 3.

**Immediately exposed a second, separate bug**, tracked as its own file rather than folded into this fix since it turned out to span multiple unrelated subsystems: `Game`'s downstream input consumers had never executed with real input in this host build. Two dangling `extern` globals with no defining translation unit anywhere in the tree (`g_flag_4A9F80`, `g_ddraw_drag_rect` — both permitted to link by `-Wl,--unresolved-symbols=ignore-all`/LINK-001 and resolving to null GOT slots that crash on first read) sat directly in `Game::UpdateInputState`, the function this fix newly makes reachable, and were fixed alongside it (`shared/stubs_impl.cpp`, `core/Game.cpp`). A third crash, in `RESDATA_ScriptedObject::IsDragging`/`panel_view` (`world/scriptengine.cpp`), is different in kind (a real, non-null but apparently not-fully-constructed object, not a dangling extern) and was left for a dedicated investigation session. Also found while disassembling `MainWndProc`: two of `Game`'s field addresses (`mouse_move_flag` at 0x48558C, `click_on_selected` at 0x4855AE) are separately claimed by unrelated, independently-defined host globals elsewhere in the tree (`g_placement_blocked`, `g_mouse_capture`) — not a bug this fix introduces, but means those other globals' readers may be seeing stale/disconnected storage rather than what the original WndProc actually wrote. Full writeup, all three crash backtraces, the broader dangling-global enumeration (8 more found in `core/Game.cpp` alone, mostly gated to mode-4-only `HandleCursorHover` and thus out of scope here), and the address-aliasing findings: `BUG-mode3-input-processing-crashes.md`. The new regression test deliberately stops after the `mouse_move` dispatch assertion (does not click, does not assert survival) because of this follow-up bug.

**Verification**: `meson test -C build` — 28/30 (same 2 pre-existing environmental failures as baseline, `embedded-mdns-discovery`/`sdl3-net-discovery-transport`, both "Unknown device type" from the sandboxed network namespace, unrelated to this change), including `--suite integration` clean. `ninja -C build-mingw -k0`: `core/Game.cpp`'s pre-existing failures (missing `SystemParametersInfoA`/`LoadCursorA`/`WindowFromPoint`/`SetCursor` declarations, lines 389/998/1019/1024/1275/1467 — none touched by this change) are unchanged; the new `GameObject g_ddraw_drag_rect_obj` and `CGWND_sdl3.cpp` additions (the latter fully `#ifndef _WIN32`-guarded) introduce no new typecheck errors.

**2026-08-09 (mode3-input-processing-crashes-second-pass)**: Continued `BUG-mode3-input-processing-crashes.md` (systematic-debugging re-run) by literally driving a real click through the Wayland sandbox and following each `coredumpctl` backtrace forward, one landmine at a time, instead of guessing. Fixed 3 more, on top of the 2 already fixed:

`RESDATA_ScriptedObject::IsDragging` (Crash 3) and `DDRAW_Building::HitTest` (Crash 4) had the identical shape: both called a fabricated 22-slot `PanelDispatchView`/`DDRAW_SpriteView` virtual interface (`panel_view(this)->point_in_rect(x,y)`) on a class that only declares 1-2 real virtuals, reading vtable memory far past what the compiler actually allocated. Ghidra's real decompile of both original functions (0x449CE0, 0x459D60) shows a **direct, non-virtual** `GameObject_PtInRect(this, x, y)` call, not a vtable dispatch — `RESDATA_ScriptedObject::IsDragging` fixed by reproducing the equivalent field comparison directly against `this->x/y/right/bottom` (renamed from `field_10`/`field_14`, matching `GameObject::PtInRect`'s own `+0x08/+0x0C/+0x10/+0x14` reads). Chasing *why* `DDRAW_Building::HitTest` had the same problem led to the real fix instead of a second field-level patch: **`DDRAW_Building` really extends `Panel` (which extends `GameObject`) in the original binary** — its constructor calls `RESDATA_BaseInit(this)` → `GameObject_BaseCtor(this,...)` directly on its own base, then overwrites the vtable to Panel's (0x4784C8) — textbook base-then-derived construction, exactly the pattern `core/GameView` (`Status: INTEGRATED`) already uses correctly for a sibling Panel-family class. `DDRAW_Building` was split into a new `graphics/DDRAW_Building.h` (so `core/Game.cpp`, blocked from including the rest of `DDRAW.h` by a pre-existing `g_tilemap`/`g_primary_surface` ODR split, can call its real methods directly — an earlier bridge-function draft of this was corrected mid-session on direct user feedback that it was "abuse" of the pattern) now declared `: public Panel`; `HitTest`/`HitTestWithDrag` call the real inherited `GameObject::PtInRect`/`Panel::HitTestChildren` instead of dangling free functions (the latter — `RESDATA_HitTestChildren` — had three mutually-inconsistent declarations across three files, only one of which, an `assert(0)` stub, actually linked).

Fixing the vtable problem exposed the *reason* it was never caught: `g_ddraw_building`/`g_town_view` were never actually constructed at all (Crash 5) — `town/sdl3_town_mode3.cpp`'s bootstrap left both as raw zero-filled bytes with a comment claiming "constructors cannot be run," stale for both classes' already-host-safe `#ifndef _WIN32` constructor paths. Fixed via real placement-new construction into the same static storage. This regressed `tests/host_mode3_bootstrap_test` (an isolated-object-extraction test that previously needed zero external symbols for raw-byte construction) — fixed by adding the newly-needed object groups (`obj_game_view`/`obj_panel`/`obj_ddraw`/`obj_sdl3_window`) to its meson link line and a local `g_empty_string` definition; verified leak-free under `valgrind --leak-check=full` (2 allocs, 2 frees, 0 leaks).

With the hit-test/construction chain fixed, `UpdateInputState`'s `PlaySound(0x1400)` fallback reached `ResourceManager::AddString`, which called `INPUT_ExitGame` (Crash 6) — another dangling extern with **no definition anywhere**, even though `input/BuildingDescriptorEditor.h` already has the real, already-implemented replacement (`BuildingDescriptorEditor_Ctor`, whose own doc comment names this exact call site and Ghidra misnomer) sitting unused. Fixed by wiring the 3 call sites to it.

A real click now runs cleanly through all 6 fixes and stops at `UI_ChildWindow`'s host path — the already-tracked entry directly above this one in this file (2026-08-05), a deliberate, loud, honest `assert`, not a crash. That's the natural stopping point for this pass: porting the ChildWindow cluster to a real host receiver type is a feature-completion task, not a bug fix. Full writeup, all backtraces, and remaining follow-ups: `BUG-mode3-input-processing-crashes.md`.
