**2026-08-10 (WNDPROC_Stream facade recovery)**: Reverse engineered `WNDPROC_CriticalSectionLock` (0x4649F0) and its two tightly-coupled siblings `WNDPROC_CriticalSectionInit`/`WNDPROC_CriticalSectionUnlock` (0x4648F0/0x464B10) — despite the "CriticalSection" naming (Ghidra auto-analysis, inherited uncritically by a first dispatch this session that was caught and corrected before being trusted), these are the classic old-iostream `ipfx`/`eatwhite`/`operator>>(char*)` triplet: locking is a real side effect, not the primary purpose. xrefs also surfaced `StreamBuf_FlushOrPut` (0x465960) sharing the exact same "recompute a StreamObject virtual-base offset via a vbtable lookup on `this`" pattern as the other three — proving it's a facade method too, not a `WNDPROC_StreamBuf` method as its old name implied. Landed as a new `resources/WndProcStream.{h,cpp}` class (`WNDPROC_Stream`, virtual base `StreamObject`) with methods `InputPrefix`/`SkipWhitespace`/`ExtractToken`/`Flush`, validated instruction-by-instruction against fresh disassembly (not just the decompiler's pseudocode, which obscured the width/null-termination behavior below). Enriched the pre-existing `resources/StreamObject.h` (previously only `sync_flag`/`critical_section` at +0x34/+0x38, from `CGWND_AboutDialog_LoadCredits`'s direct calls) with the newly-evidenced `rdbuf`/`state_bits`/`tied`/`format_flags`/`width` fields — this is the SAME struct, not a parallel one, confirmed by the identical +0x34/+0x38 tail. Also added previously-undiscovered `WNDPROC_StreamBuf::ReadChar`/`GetChar` (0x4652A0/0x4651A0) plus a new virtual `Underflow()` hook (vtable+0x20, base body not yet located) to the existing `resources/WndProcStreamBuf.{h,cpp}`, and renamed its `reserved0C_` field to `peekCache_` now that its real role (an unbuffered-mode one-character peek cache) is evidenced — propagated to `Win32StreamFile.cpp`/`Win32StreamMem.cpp`'s existing writes of it.

**Process note — a dispatched `reverse-engineer` subagent's first attempt was substantively wrong and was caught before being trusted**: it invented an unevidenced `ios`/`istream`/`streambuf` class trio in a brand-new `runtime/` directory (a subsystem name not in `meson.build`'s `subsys_dirs`, so its claimed "compiles clean" was never actually built — nothing in `runtime/` was ever compiled), had the streambuf pointer at the wrong offset (ios_base+0x20, actually the *tied*-stream pointer — the real streambuf pointer is ios_base+0x4, independently confirmed three separate ways: direct disassembly of all three functions, `WndProcStreamBuf.h`'s pre-existing `readPtr_`/`readHigh_` field documentation at the exact offsets the "space available" check reads, and `HelpWnd_LoadHelpData`'s own caller-side eofbit check reaching the identical `ios_base+0x8` state field), inverted the width==0/width==1 boundary (guessed "unlimited" from memory of the C++98 istream spec rather than deriving it from the actual unsigned-wraparound machine code — width==0 does end up unlimited, but not for the reason or via the mechanism claimed), and unconditionally null-terminated the output buffer where the disassembly proves the original leaves it completely untouched on a zero-character failure. It also fabricated an unverified `ExtractNumeric` method (address and signature both guessed, explicitly flagged by the agent itself as out-of-scope in the same breath) and declared `WNDPROC_StreamBuf::ReadChar`/`GetChar`/`FlushOrPut` `virtual` despite their call sites being direct absolute `CALL`s, not vtable dispatch. All of this was found by independently re-deriving the same three functions from raw disassembly (not the decompiler's pseudocode) before relaying the subagent's report, per this project's "assembly first" rule — the corrected version above is what actually shipped; the invented `runtime/iostream.{h,cpp}` was deleted outright rather than patched. Lesson for future dispatches: a single subagent turn on a function cluster this entangled with pre-existing, similarly-named classes (`StreamObject`, `WNDPROC_StreamBuf`) needs an explicit instruction to grep the existing tree for related class names *before* writing new files, not just search for the reverse-engineer's own guessed vocabulary (it searched for "istream"/"ios::"/"streambuf"/"ipfx", which don't appear anywhere in this codebase's naming, and missed `StreamObject`/`WNDPROC_StreamBuf` entirely).

**Verification**: `meson setup --reconfigure build && meson compile -C build` clean (0 errors) after fixing the `reserved0C_`→`peekCache_` rename's two other call sites (`Win32StreamFile.cpp`/`Win32StreamMem.cpp`, caught by the build, not manually). `meson test -C build`: 28/30 (same 2 pre-existing sandboxed-netns failures, `embedded-mdns-discovery`/`sdl3-net-discovery-transport`, unrelated), `--suite integration` 1/1 (the aggregate GUI suite) unchanged. `ninja -C build-mingw -k 0`: all 5 touched/added files (`StreamObject.cpp`, `WndProcStreamBuf.cpp`, `WndProcStream.cpp`, `Win32StreamFile.cpp`, `Win32StreamMem.cpp`) compile clean; the run's `FAILED` lines are exclusively pre-existing, unrelated `-Werror=invalid-offsetof` sites (`input/InputMgr.cpp` via `BuildingMgrObjectGroup.h`, `ui/CursorEditWindow.cpp` via `UI_ChildWindow.h`) already tracked elsewhere. Ghidra: renamed `ios_ipfx`→`WNDPROC_Stream::InputPrefix`, `istream_operator_extract_string`→`WNDPROC_Stream::ExtractToken`, `ios_eatwhite`→`WNDPROC_Stream::SkipWhitespace`, `StreamBuf_FlushOrPut`→`WNDPROC_Stream::Flush`, `StreamBuf_ReadChar`/`GetChar`→`WNDPROC_StreamBuf::ReadChar`/`GetChar`, and reverted the first subagent's `WNDPROC_Enter/LeaveCriticalSection_wrapper` rename back to the plain `WNDPROC_Enter/LeaveCriticalSection` names every other file in the tree already uses for 0x464D90/0x464DA0; decompiler comments added at all 6 addresses; database saved.

**Wiring the real symbol to its real callers (found via an `advisor()` review flagging an unrun `call 0` check)**: `WNDPROC_CriticalSectionLock` turned out to already be a load-bearing free-function symbol with real callers in 5 files predating this session (`game/TrainStation.cpp`, `input/BuildingDescriptorEditor.cpp`, `ui/HelpWnd.cpp`, `ui/UI_ChildWindow.cpp`, `ui/CursorEditWindow.cpp`) — none of which this session's initial Ghidra-only rename pass touched, since it never looked at the C++ source tree for a same-named symbol. Reconciled:
- Added `void WNDPROC_CriticalSectionLock(int*, char*)` (C++-mangled linkage, matching `_Z27WNDPROC_CriticalSectionLockPiPc`) to `resources/WndProcStream.cpp` as a thin adapter to `WNDPROC_Stream::ExtractToken`, and deleted the crashing `assert(0)` stub it replaces in `shared/stubs_impl.cpp`.
- **Found and fixed a real, previously-silent landmine**: `ui/HelpWnd.cpp` declared this same symbol *inside* an `extern "C" { }` block (C/unmangled linkage) while the other 3 correctly-linked callers declare it with C++ linkage outside one — a mismatch, so HelpWnd's declaration bound to a *different*, wholly undefined symbol. Verified via `nm`: `U WNDPROC_CriticalSectionLock` (unmangled) with no matching definition anywhere in the tree, `-Wl,--unresolved-symbols=ignore-all` turning it into a null-pointer call at all 13 of that file's call sites (parsing every help-page directive line). Fixed by moving the declaration outside the `extern "C"` block to restore C++ linkage.
- Corrected a stale wrong-address comment in `input/BuildingDescriptorEditor.cpp` and `ui/UI_ChildWindow.cpp` (both claimed "real def at 0x4647A0" — that address is actually inside the unrelated `WNDPROC_StreamPrintf`; the real address is 0x4649F0).
- **Found, NOT fixed — flagged as a separate follow-up**: `ui/CursorEditWindow.cpp` declares a *third*, mutually-incompatible signature for the same name — `void* __thiscall WNDPROC_CriticalSectionLock(void* stream, int* errorCode, int16_t* fieldY, int16_t* fieldX, int* tempBuf)`, 5 parameters, explicitly annotated `/* 0x4649F0 */`. This is definitively wrong: 0x4649F0's disassembly ends in `RET 0x4` (__thiscall, exactly one stack DWORD beyond `this` — the confirmed `char* buffer` param), not `RET 0x10` (four stack params), so a 5-argument declaration cannot be the real signature. `CursorEditWindow::Render` (0x40E8D0) calls it expecting it to populate 4 separate output fields directly — architecturally incompatible with what the function actually does (extract one whitespace-delimited token into a caller buffer). Because the parameter list differs, this compiles to a distinct mangled symbol from the one just fixed, so it neither regressed nor improved by this session's changes — it was already, and remains, its own separate landmine. Properly fixing `CursorEditWindow::Render`'s whole parsing chain (which likely also mis-signatures `WNDPROC_StreamPrintf`/`WNDPROC_StreamWrite` nearby) needs its own dedicated RE pass, not a fraction of this one.

**Still open**: the wider `WNDPROC_Stream*` facade PROGRESS.md already tracked as unresolved (`WNDPROC_StreamRead`/`Write`/`Printf`/`ReadLine`/`SeekForward`, 0x4640B0-0x464D70, still no-op host stubs — see "win32_stream.c removed (partial)" above) is NOT this entry's `WNDPROC_Stream` class, just a plausible relative sharing the same `StreamObject` virtual base; a future pass reconstructing that cluster must confirm the relationship, not assume it — though note `WNDPROC_StreamPrintf` (0x464750) already calls this session's `WNDPROC_Stream::InputPrefix`, which is one confirmed data point in favor of the relationship. `WNDPROC_StreamBuf::Underflow`'s base/override implementation address is still unlocated. `WNDPROC_Stream::gcount_`'s "istream::gcount" identity is inferred from the write pattern only, not a direct read. `ui/CursorEditWindow.cpp`'s 5-parameter mis-signature (above) needs a dedicated pass.

**Verification, second pass**: `objdump -d build/lego_loco | grep -cE "call\s+0 "` measured precisely via `git stash`/reconfigure/rebuild on both sides of this session's full diff: **294 → 282** (12 fixed — the HelpWnd.cpp linkage fix, matching its 13 call sites minus one likely-shared/inlined codegen site). `nm -C build/lego_loco | grep CriticalSectionLock`: exactly one defined symbol, `WNDPROC_CriticalSectionLock(int*, char*)`, `T` (defined), matching the callers' expected mangled name. `meson test -C build`: 28/30 (same 2 pre-existing sandboxed-netns failures), `--suite integration` unchanged. `ninja -C build-mingw -k 0`: 51 FAILED both before and after this session's full diff (verified via the same stash-based before/after rebuild) — every one of this session's 5 newly-touched-for-this-fix files (`HelpWnd.cpp`, `HelpWnd_stubs.cpp`, `UI_ChildWindow.cpp`, `BuildingDescriptorEditor.cpp`, `stubs_impl.cpp`) fails for the identical pre-existing reasons at the identical line numbers (the tree-wide `-Werror=invalid-offsetof` class, and `stubs_impl.cpp`'s pre-existing `world/tilemap.h`/duplicate-definition issues, both already tracked elsewhere in this file) — zero new failure categories.

Initial verification found 6/13 GUI integration subtests passing with 7 multiplayer subtests failing: `has_scenario` (`*(int32_t*)(g_netSettings+0x10)`, populated by `EnumConnections`'s connection list) was now correctly null on a host with no DirectPlay DLL/COM devices, where the pre-existing code left a non-deterministic garbage pointer there instead (`793460400`/`544632960` observed across runs) that happened to read nonzero and enable the multiplayer button — the test suite's expectation had been built against that UB artifact, not real behavior. **2026-08-11 (CursorEditWindow::Render mis-signature — closes the 2026-08-10 "found, NOT fixed" follow-up above)**: Fixed `ui/CursorEditWindow.cpp`'s third, mutually-incompatible `WNDPROC_CriticalSectionLock` declaration flagged above. Dispatched a read-only `reverse-engineer` subagent first (per this project's established pattern for this cluster); it correctly proved the 5-argument declaration disassembly-impossible but had no Ghidra database available (none was open) and couldn't get further. Opened `loco` and re-disassembled the real call chain directly instead of re-dispatching blind: `CursorEditWindow::Render` (0x40E8D0) pushes all 4 pointers (`&errorCode`, `&field_7AA`, `&field_7A8`, `&tempBuf`) *before* a single `CALL 0x4649f0`, which looked like a stack-corruption bug (4 pushed dwords vs. the callee's proven `RET 0x4`) until the arithmetic was carried through all 4 calls in the chain: `ExtractToken`'s `RET 0x4` consumes only the top (last-pushed) slot, and the next three calls (`WNDPROC_StreamPrintf`/`WNDPROC_StreamPrintf`/`WNDPROC_StreamWrite`, each also `RET 0x4` with *zero* pushed args at their own call sites) each net +4 bytes, landing back exactly on the caller's expected `ESP` (-16+4+4+4+4 = 0) at the epilogue — a real, deliberate MSVC codegen idiom (hoist all argument LEAs once, let each single-arg callee's own cleanup reveal the next), not a bug. This also proved the *value routing* itself was wrong, independent of the declaration: real consumption order (LIFO) is `tempBuf`→call 1, `field_7A8`→call 2, `field_7AA`→call 3, `errorCode`→call 4 — the previous code had assigned `field_7AA`/`field_7A8` to call 1 and `tempBuf` to call 2. Disassembling `WNDPROC_StreamPrintf` (0x464750) and `WNDPROC_StreamWrite` (0x4646C0) in full (not just their shared `InputPrefix(0)` prologue, which is as far as the subagent got) proved both are misleadingly-named stream *extractors*, not output functions — `operator>>(int16_t*)` and `operator>>(int32_t*)` respectively, each parsing a formatted number via a CRT numeric-parse call with ERANGE overflow handling. This means the field previously called `errorCode` (compared against `-9`) is actually the extraction target of `WNDPROC_StreamWrite`/`ExtractInt` — a parsed integer, not an error code. `game/TrainStation.cpp` independently corroborates this: it already documents a real `"-9"` string literal (0x47E3CC, `s_terminator`) recovered from `.rdata` and used as a section-terminator sentinel tested against parsed lines in this same class of `.dat` text format — strong evidence the `-9` check here is that same end-of-record marker. Also resolved the "4-byte `tempBuf`" concern raised in review: the function's real stack frame reserves exactly 0x10C bytes total, of which 4 go to the int field and the remaining 0x108 (264) bytes match every sibling `WNDPROC_CriticalSectionLock` call site's `char lineBuf[264]` exactly (`game/TrainStation.cpp`, `input/BuildingDescriptorEditor.cpp`, `ui/UI_ChildWindow.cpp`) — it's a normal 264-byte line buffer, not an undersized `int`. Preserved (not fixed, per CLAUDE.md) one real original bug this surfaces: `Render` never initializes the stream's `width` field before the first extraction, and `ExtractToken` zeroes it on every use, so if `width` is 0 here (as `WNDPROC_StreamGetSize`'s constructor path leaves it, per the 2026-08-10 entry above) a leading token over 263 characters overflows the 264-byte buffer in the original binary as much as here — documented with a `BUG:` comment, not hardened. Rewrote `Render()`'s body and extern declarations to match; renamed `WNDPROC_StreamPrintf`→`WNDPROC_Stream::ExtractShort` and `WNDPROC_StreamWrite`→`WNDPROC_Stream::ExtractInt` in Ghidra (both proven not to be printf/write functions), added decompiler comments at 0x464750/0x4646C0/0x40E8D0, saved the database. `ui/CursorEditWindow.cpp`'s own pre-existing declarations of `WNDPROC_StreamPrintf`/`WNDPROC_StreamWrite` (2-arg) were already correct — only `WNDPROC_CriticalSectionLock`'s declaration and `Render`'s body were wrong.

**Review-caught control-flow fabrication, fixed before landing**: an `advisor()` pass caught that the first draft of the rewritten `Render()` invented control flow not present in the disassembly — `if (stream == nullptr) return 0;` and `if (streamVtable == nullptr) return 0;` have no corresponding test anywhere in the real 37-instruction body (it dereferences both unconditionally), and worse, the real function has a **single shared exit**: both the error-flag early-out (`JNZ 0x0040e934`) and the post-extraction `-9` check (`JZ 0x40E934`) jump to the *same* tail block that unconditionally calls `CGWND_ValidatePaletteData` and returns `BL` — the first draft's early `return 0;` skipped that palette call entirely on the error-flag path. Rewrote to match: no null checks (every real caller already passes a validated pointer, so removing them is both behavior-preserving and restores fidelity per CLAUDE.md's "preserve control flow" / "no validation for scenarios that can't happen"), one `if` gating the whole extraction+check block, `CGWND_ValidatePaletteData` and the `return resultFlag;` reached unconditionally at the bottom.

**Verification**: `meson compile -C build` clean, 0 errors, both before and after the control-flow fix. `nm -C build/lego_loco | grep -E 'CriticalSectionLock|StreamPrintf|StreamWrite'`: exactly one defined (`T`) symbol per name. Per the same advisor pass's correction — `nm -C` demangles and can hide the exact defect class this cluster cares about — re-ran **undecorated**: `nm build/lego_loco.p/ui_CursorEditWindow.cpp.o` shows all three references already correctly mangled (`_Z19WNDPROC_StreamWritePvS_` etc., matching the definitions), confirming this file itself has no HelpWnd-style linkage landmine. `meson test -C build`: **31/31**, including `--suite integration` (both known sandboxed-netns failures from prior sessions did not reproduce in this run's environment) — note this does **not** exercise the corrected extraction path itself: `WNDPROC_CriticalSectionLock`'s body is still the deliberate `assert`-abort stub from the 2026-08-10 postmortem above (no real caller in this tree constructs an actual `WNDPROC_Stream` object yet), so `Render` would abort, not silently misbehave, if actually reached; this fix corrects the *declaration, call shape, and control flow* only. `call 0` measured via `git stash`/rebuild on both sides of this session's diff: **282 → 278** (4 fixed, matching the 4 call sites that now bind correctly instead of mismatching). **Not attempted, deliberately out of scope** (advisor-flagged as non-blocking — `Render` never touches these offsets): the related `CursorEditWindow::init`/`UI_ChildWindow_Create` stack-offset ambiguity (0x18 vs 0x20 vs 0x24) noted in the "win32_stream.c removed (partial)" entry above stays open, as does the broader `WIN32_StreamOpen*` cluster wiring gap.

**Strict2/mingw check**: `meson setup build-strict2 -Dstrict=2 && ninja -C build-strict2 -k 0` is **not** clean — 14 pre-existing errors (`-Werror=old-style-cast` in `CursorEditWindow.cpp`'s untouched lines, `-Werror=missing-declarations` in `tests/win32_stream_file_test.cpp`, `-Werror=effc++` in `resources/WndProcStreamBuf.h`), confirmed via `git stash`/rebuild to be byte-for-byte identical on unmodified `main` — this session's memory note that "STRICT=2 has been clean tree-wide since 2026-08-09" is now **stale** (something regressed it after that date, unrelated to this fix); memory updated accordingly, not investigated further here as out of scope. `ninja -C build-mingw -k 0`: 51 `FAILED` before and after this change (unchanged count); `ui/CursorEditWindow.cpp` fails there, but exclusively for the identical pre-existing `-Werror=invalid-offsetof` reasons already tracked in the 2026-08-10 entry above — zero new failure categories.

**New finding, fixed as a separate follow-up commit (same session)**: while running the undecorated-`nm` check above, discovered that `game/TrainStation.cpp`, `ui/UI_ChildWindow.cpp`, and `input/BuildingDescriptorEditor.cpp` all declared `WNDPROC_StreamPrintf`/`WNDPROC_StreamWrite` (and, in `UI_ChildWindow.cpp`/`BuildingDescriptorEditor.cpp`, `WNDPROC_StreamReadLine` too) **inside an `extern "C" { }` block** (each with a comment explicitly noting the others share this same choice), while the real definitions (`shared/stubs_impl.cpp`, C++ linkage, no enclosing `extern "C"`) are C++-mangled — the identical defect class as the `ui/HelpWnd.cpp` landmine found and fixed in the 2026-08-10 "WNDPROC_Stream facade recovery" entry above. Confirmed via `nm` on all three `.o` files: each showed `U WNDPROC_StreamPrintf` / `U WNDPROC_StreamWrite` / `U WNDPROC_StreamReadLine` (unmangled, undefined) rather than the mangled names the real definitions export, meaning (per the same `-Wl,--unresolved-symbols=ignore-all` mechanism documented for the HelpWnd fix) every one of these three files' calls to these functions bound to a null-pointer call. An `advisor()` review correctly overrode its own earlier "not scope creep" guidance once this was confirmed with hard evidence (mechanically identical to a landed precedent, not a design question) — fixed by moving the affected declarations out of each file's `extern "C"` block, matching how `WNDPROC_CriticalSectionLock` was already correctly declared just outside it in all three. `CRT_wcsstr` (its own separate, pre-existing 4+-incompatible-declaration landmine spanning `shared/stubs_impl.cpp`, `shared/defsym_stubs.cpp`, `native/ddraw_filedata.c`, `native/assetmgr_loadfile.c`) and `CRT_fabs`/`CRT_fmod`/`CRT_mbstowcs_s`/`_CrtDbg_report_fmt_helper` (their own "identity unresolved" tags, already flagged in-file) were deliberately left untouched — same symptom, unresolved identity or a much larger multi-declaration cluster, not part of this fix.

**Latent trap, noted not fixed**: `ui/UI_ChildWindow.cpp` declares `WNDPROC_StreamReadLine` `void*`-returning and chains its return value into a second call (the "button" directive), while `shared/stubs_impl.cpp`'s real definition is `void`-returning. Return type doesn't affect Itanium mangling, so both now correctly bind to the same symbol — that's what this fix corrects — but it does NOT make the chained read safe: it's inert only because that stub aborts before returning anything today. Whoever gives it a real body must make it return `void*` (`this`, matching the original x86 ABI's EAX), or this chained read silently consumes an unset register. Documented in-file at the declaration site.

**Verification**: `meson compile -C build` clean, 0 errors. `nm build/lego_loco.p/{game_TrainStation,ui_UI_ChildWindow,input_BuildingDescriptorEditor}.cpp.o | grep -E 'StreamPrintf|StreamWrite|StreamReadLine'`: all references now correctly mangled (`_Z20WNDPROC_StreamPrintfPvS_` etc.), zero unmangled `U`. `meson test -C build`: 31/31 again. `call 0`: **278 → 214** (64 fixed — every real call site across the three files' `WNDPROC_StreamPrintf`/`StreamWrite`/`StreamReadLine` usage now binds correctly instead of to a null-pointer call), isolated from the `CursorEditWindow.cpp` fix above by measuring immediately before vs. after this specific change (278 was already the post-CursorEditWindow-fix baseline; also re-confirmed directly on the final, single-exit `Render()` body via a targeted stash/rebuild, not just the first draft). `ninja -C build-mingw -k 0`: 51 `FAILED` unchanged; the three touched files fail there only for the same pre-existing `-Werror=invalid-offsetof` class already tracked above — zero new failure categories (strict2's staleness is already recorded in the `CursorEditWindow.cpp` commit's entry above, not re-run separately here). This is a separate logical change from the `CursorEditWindow.cpp` fix above and lands as its own commit.
